It opens `#licensePanel` via the exact same fixed-position/toggle/outside-click/Escape pattern `#shortcutsPanel` already established (see "Keyboard shortcuts popup" above) — same `getBoundingClientRect`-based positioning below the button, same `.hidden` class toggle, wired into the same shared `document` click listener and the same `Escape` handler block. `#openMenu`/`#sessionMenu` are `position:fixed` (out of flow), so `#btnLicense` sitting after `#openMenu` in the markup (rather than immediately after the `#btnOpen` button itself) doesn't affect where it lands in the flex row — it's still the last visible child. Content is a proprietary-license text (rights holder Philipp Klein, Rendsburger Straße 14, 20359 Hamburg, philogg@kleinphilipp.de; prohibits redistribution and modification; "as is", no warranty; revocable at any time) plus a version line at the bottom.

Version display: `const PHILOGG_VERSION = "dev";`, declared once near the top of the inline `<script>`, is written verbatim (no "v" prefix — person-requested, same-session follow-up) into both `#brandVersion` (a small `<span>` next to `.brand-name`, e.g. "dev") and `#licenseVersion` ("Version: dev") at Init. It's a plain string literal, not computed at runtime — this file has no build step and no server, so there's nothing to compute it from at load time. It stays the literal `"dev"` in source control; only `.github/workflows/release.yml` ("Build tester release", manual `workflow_dispatch`) stamps it to a real short commit SHA, via a `sed` replace of that exact literal, in a build it publishes as a GitHub Release asset named `philogg-<commit-date>-<short-sha>.html` (so the version and its age are readable from the filename alone, not just the in-app display) — the tracked `philogg.html` is never committed back with a real hash, so `git diff`/`git log` on this file stay meaningful and a plain local checkout always reads "dev" (see the workflow file's own header comment, and the chat instructions given alongside this feature for the GitHub-side setup, e.g. enabling the Actions permission needed to create a release, if the default `GITHUB_TOKEN` write permission is off).

**No-file-loaded state: one hint, not three (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested)**. With zero files loaded, `#emptyState`'s centered "No log file loaded yet" message (unchanged) is now the **only** such hint — two duplicates were removed outright: `#dropHint`, a dashed-border "Drag & drop log files… or use Open…" box that used to render directly in the tree sidebar (`renderTree()`'s own visibility toggle, condition `state.rootIds.length === 0 && state.folders.length === 0`) whenever the tree was empty; and the header's `#statusText`, which used to read the literal string `"No files loaded"` (`renderStatus()`) — it's now just an empty string in that state, only ever populated with the real "N files · M entries" count once something's loaded. Both were pure UI trims with no behavior behind them to preserve (the `#dropOverlay` drag-over overlay, shown transiently while a drag is in progress over the window, is unrelated and untouched). See "`#viewBar`" above for the third piece of the same request (hiding the toolbar row itself, not just its hints).

## The three "active node" views

`renderMainView()` picks a view based on the active node's `filterType`, all mutually exclusive:

1. **Log view** (default) — virtualized (`ROW_HEIGHT = 28`, `BUFFER_ROWS = 10`) so it stays smooth at any file size; only the rows in/near the viewport are ever real DOM nodes. Has the level quick-filter bar, click-to-select + arrow-key navigation, right-click context menu (filter before/after this, extract numbers), double-click **reveals the entry in the Highlight view** (see below) with flash highlight, and a resizable detail panel at the bottom (drag the handle above it) showing the full selected message with preserved line breaks. The tree sidebar's width (`#sidebarResizer`) and, in Stacked layout, the Filtered/Full split (`#fhSplitResizer`) are resizable the same way — see "Highlight view" below.
2. **Extraction table** (`filterType === "extract"`) — spreadsheet-style grid, not virtualized (extraction results are expected to be selective). Has its own Table/Plot tab switcher (see Plotting below). Cell selection is a `Set` of `"row,col"` keys (not a rectangle) specifically so Ctrl+click can build **non-contiguous** selections (e.g. columns 1 and 3, skipping 2) — plain click/drag/shift-click still do contiguous ranges. Copy is always plain TSV (paste-ready for Excel) via `copyTextToClipboard` (Clipboard API with an `execCommand` fallback for `file://` contexts where the modern API may be restricted). The pattern is shown above the table as numbered, colored chips (`COLUMN_PALETTE`, cycles if >6 columns) with **bidirectional hover linking**: hover a column header/cell → its chip highlights; hover a chip → its column highlights; hover a row number → all chips highlight (the whole pattern produced that row). The only view with **no Highlight-view companion** — double-click still does the old destructive full-log jump (`jumpToFullLog`), since there's nowhere else in this view to reveal the entry.
3. **Link pair view** (`filterType === "link"`) — each pair rendered as two individually-clickable/double-clickable rows joined by a visual brace with a Δ-time label between them. Right-click the brace = context menu on the *combined* pair entry (so "Extract numbers" works across both sides); right-click one row = context menu on just that side; double-click a row = reveals that row's real underlying entry in the Highlight view, same as the log view (see below — link view is a Filter-view "flavor" for this purpose, not a separate case).

**Log view and link view are both "Filter view" flavors**, each paired with a **Highlight view** — see the dedicated section below.

## Highlight view (Filter/Highlight split)

Two side-by-side views of the same file, introduced together as `#fhSplit`: the **Filter view** (UI label "Filtered") is the pre-existing log/link view, narrowed by the active filter-tree node. The **Highlight view** (UI label "Full") is always the active node's *whole root file* (`getRootFileId(state.activeId)`, unaffected by which filter node is active), with any filter-tree node that has a highlight colour set colouring its matching rows. Internally every identifier still says "Highlight" (`renderHighlightView`, `#highlightWrap`, `highlightColorMap`, `fhLayout`, …) — only the button label was renamed to "Full"; a full internal rename was judged a much bigger, riskier diff for what was a label-only ask, so it was left alone. The point of the split is tracking 2–3 patterns across the *whole* file while the Filtered view stays narrowed to something specific — e.g. filter down to one thread's messages in the Filtered view while the Full view shows where ERRORs and a particular retry pattern land across the entire file.

**Turning on a highlight rule**: each filter-type tree row (not file rows, not `extract` rows — see `renderNode`) has a small colour **swatch** button where the old multi-select checkbox used to be (see "Tree interactions" below for where that checkbox went). Click it to open a small custom colour picker (`#colorPickerPopup`) — a hue wheel (`conic-gradient` disc, click anywhere to pick a hue at a fixed 68%/55% saturation/lightness) plus a row of curated presets, deliberately **not** the native `<input type="color">` (Windows' native picker UX varies too much to be predictable here). Right-click the swatch, or "Clear colour" in the picker, clears it. The colour **is** the on/off state — no separate boolean; an uncoloured node just isn't a highlight rule. Two colours can coincide across different nodes (not deduplicated — may be intentional grouping) and travel with the node through filter save/load JSON, copy/paste (`cloneSubtree`), and undo/redo (`snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`), the same way `inverted`/`linkedId` do.

**Computing matches** (`computeHighlightMap(fileId)`): walks every node under the file (not just the active chain — a highlight rule anywhere in the tree should show up), and for each coloured filter node adds its colour to every entry it matches, via `getEntries(node.id)` — cheap since `getEntries` is already memoized per node. `link` nodes are a special case: their results are synthetic pair entries, not real ones, so every real underlying entry — via `getTupleEntries(pair)`, which recurses through arbitrarily many nesting levels for a chained/multi-hop link, not just `pair.first`/`pair.second` one level deep, see "Link filter" → "Chained links" — gets the colour instead of the synthetic pair id. A row matching several rules gets several small colour markers (`.hl-marker`, thin stripes in the left gutter) rather than one blended colour — there's no natural priority between arbitrary user rules the way `LEVEL_SORT_RANK` orders log levels, so no attempt is made to pick a "winning" colour. **Shown in both views**: originally Full-view-only, `renderVisibleRows()` (the Filtered view) now renders the same markers from the same `highlightColorMap`, computed once per `renderMainView()` call (before either view's own render) rather than twice, so the Filtered view's first paint after a node/filter switch already has the right colours instead of showing the previous node's map until the next scroll. The Filtered view's own `.ctx-bracket` (time-context filter) uses the same left gutter slot — a row that's both context-bracketed and highlight-matched shows both markers overlapping; accepted as a known edge case rather than adding a second gutter lane.

**Selection stays unified**: `state.selectedId` is shared between both views (it always was, for the detail panel — see `updateDetailPanel`), so `updateSelectedRowClass` now syncs the `.selected` class on whichever of `#tableRows`/`#highlightRows` currently has that row rendered, regardless of which view the click came from. Plain click in either view just selects (`selectEntry`/`selectHighlightEntry`) — no scrolling of the other view. Double-click in the Filter view (or a link-view pair row) calls `revealInHighlightView(entry)`: selects the entry, reveals the Full panel (switches to it, in tabs layout — a no-op in stacked layout, both panels are already shown), and sets `state.highlightScrollTargetId` to scroll/flash it there — **without** touching `state.activeId` or the level filter, unlike the old `jumpToFullLog` this replaced. This is deliberately not routed through `render()`: a full render would reset the Filter view's own scroll to the top (see `renderTable`'s "any other change... starts back at the top" logic), exactly the destructive behavior this was built to avoid.

**Level quick-filter applies to the Filtered view ONLY.** Originally both views computed their entries via the same global `state.levelFilter`/`applyLevelFilter`, with `renderLevelBar()`'s click handler calling `renderMainView()` (not just `renderTable()`) so both views' renders stayed in lockstep — that shared-filtering design lasted through 32e282b4. **Changed 2026-08-19 (person-requested: *"die Log Level Filter sollen sich nicht mehr auf das full Log auswirken. stattdessen soll ein andern der Log Level Filter auch zu einem automatischen Sprung von Full nach Filtered führen"*)**: `renderHighlightView()` no longer calls `applyLevelFilter()` at all — `currentHighlightViewEntries` is always the root file's complete, unfiltered `entries` array, so the Full view now reads as a genuinely stable "whole file" reference that the level quick-filter can't thin out. `renderLevelBar()`'s click handler still calls `renderMainView()` (the Filtered view's own render still needs it, and the Full view still needs its OWN re-render even though its entry list no longer changes — e.g. `highlightColorMap`/scroll-anchor bookkeeping happen there too), but now also calls `revealFilteredView()` right after — the exact same auto-reveal a filter/node switch already triggers (see "General 'don't jump' scroll anchoring + auto-reveal Filtered" below) — so a level-filter change made while on the Full tab is never invisible: it jumps you to the view it actually affects. No-op in Stacked layout (both panels already visible) or when already on the Filtered tab.

**Layout**: a single tri-state toggle (`#fhTabs`, three buttons: "Full" / "Filtered" / "Stacked") — `applyFhView(view)` is the one entry point, `fhLayout` ("tabs" | "stacked") and `fhActiveTab` ("filter" | "highlight") are still the two pieces of underlying state, persisted to `localStorage` the same way the theme toggle is (a display preference, not session/filter data). "Full"/"Filtered" set `fhLayout` to `"tabs"` and pick which panel shows; "Stacked" sets `fhLayout` to `"stacked"` directly, showing both panels at once, **Full on top / Filtered on the bottom** (that's the actual DOM order inside `#fhSplit` now, not just a CSS `order` override) — `#filterSlot` at a resizable explicit height (`#fhSplitResizer`, drag handle between the two panels, same mechanism as the detail panel's resizer below, and same bottom-panel sign convention: dragging the handle down shrinks the panel below it), `#highlightWrap` filling whatever's left above it. Session-only, not persisted, same as the detail panel height. Each panel also carries a small top-right **identifier badge** (`.fh-panel-badge`, "Full" / "Filtered") that's only shown in Stacked layout — in tabs layout the active `#fhTabs` button already says which one is showing, so the badge would be redundant there.

`#fhTabs` itself lives inside `#viewBar`, a single merged toolbar row that also holds `#levelBar` and `#breadcrumb` (see "Header/toolbar layout" below) — only `#fhTabs`'s own visibility is toggled by `renderMainView()` (hidden with no file loaded or on an extraction node, since extract has no Highlight companion); `#levelBar`/`#breadcrumb` stay visible in every state.

**Rendering**: the Highlight view has its own complete copy of the Filter view's virtualization scheme (`renderHighlightView`/`renderHighlightVisibleRows`/`scrollToHighlightIndex`/`setHighlightScroll`, `#highlightBody`/`#highlightSpacer`/`#highlightRows`) — a second, independent scroll container. It reuses the Filter view's `.row-grid`/`.log-row`/`.col-*` CSS wholesale (extended the relevant ID selectors rather than duplicating rules) so the two views' rows line up visually. One deliberate behavioral difference from `renderTable()`: **it does not reset scroll to the top on every render.** The Filter view resets on every tree/filter change because that always means "new content" there; the Highlight view's content (the whole file) usually hasn't changed across those same actions, so resetting it every time would make it useless as a stable reference while browsing the Filter view. It only resets when the root file itself changes (`lastHighlightRootId`).

## General "don't jump" scroll anchoring + auto-reveal Filtered

Person-requested this session (2026-08-19; German: *"Stelle sicher, dass beim ändern der angezeigten Log-Level, die aktive Zeile nicht aus dem Bild springt, sondern sich das Log darüber/darunter erweitert. Generell gilt: wechsel von Filtern, Log-Leveln, Message Expand etc. die aktive Zeile bleibt sichtbar (sofern sie im Ziel-View vorhanden ist)... Das Ziel soll sein, dass ein Wechsel zwischen unterschiedlichen Anzeigen, nicht als Sprung wahrgenommen wird, insbesondere wenn ein Element ausgewählt ist. Bei wechsel zu einem anderen Filter, wechsle automatisch vom Full zum Filtered View (wenn Stacked aktiv ist, keine Änderung)"*). Two related but separate fixes:

**1. Scroll anchoring, generalized.** The multiline toggle (see below) previously had its own one-shot re-anchor mechanism (`topVisibleEntryId` + `pendingTableTopAlignId`/`pendingHighlightTopAlignId`, manually captured by that one button's click handler right before `render()`). Every OTHER view-changing action — a level-filter pill click, switching the active tree node/filter, a column sort — still fell through `renderTable()`'s old default: *"any other change to the displayed set starts back at the top of the new list."* That default is exactly what read as a jarring jump, especially with a row selected.

Replaced with `captureViewAnchor(entries, offsets, scrollEl)` / `restoreViewAnchor(anchor, entries, offsets, scrollEl, setScrollFn)` (declared together, right above `renderVisibleRows()`), called automatically **inside** `renderTable()`/`renderHighlightView()` themselves on every render — no call site needs to remember to invoke anything:

- `captureViewAnchor()` runs first, against the OLD (pre-change) entries/offsets/scroll position, before the caller recomputes them. If `state.selectedId` is present in the OLD entries, it's the anchor — pixel offset from the top of the viewport captured too when the row is currently on screen (`onScreen`), or `null` when it isn't (nothing to preserve, just an obligation to reveal it once restored). With no selection, it falls back to `topVisibleEntryId()` (the multiline toggle's original fallback, now the general one) — the entry currently at the very top of the viewport, offset `0`.
- `restoreViewAnchor()` runs after the NEW entries/offsets exist, looking the anchored id up in the NEW list. Found + had a pixel offset → `setScrollFn(newRowTop - offset)`, landing the SAME row at the exact SAME screen position it had before (the log visibly "expands/collapses" above or below it, not a jump). Found + no prior offset (was selected but off-screen) → centers it in the viewport instead of edge-snapping, so a stale-but-selected row actually gets revealed rather than staying hidden. Not found at all (filtered out, or nothing was anchored and even the fallback list was empty) → returns `false`, and the caller falls back to its own pre-existing default (`renderTable()`: reset to top, same as always; `renderHighlightView()`: leave `scrollTop` exactly as it was — that view's own established default, see "Highlight view" above, only ever actively touched on a genuine root-file switch).
- `topVisibleEntryId()`/`captureViewAnchor()`/`restoreViewAnchor()` all key variable-vs-flat row-height math off **`offsets` truthiness**, not `state.multilineMessages` directly — the global flag can already reflect a NEWER mode than the specific entries/offsets pair being captured (e.g. right after the multiline toggle flips it but before the affected view actually re-renders), so the offsets array's own presence is the only reliable signal for "were THESE rows laid out with variable heights."
- `renderTable()` now calls `captureViewAnchor()` unconditionally as its first real step (right after the `!hasFiles` early return) and tries `restoreViewAnchor()` after `state.scrollTargetId`/`pendingTableScroll` (both explicit navigation intents, still highest priority) have had their turn. `renderHighlightView()` only attempts capture/restore when the root file hasn't changed (`sameRoot`, computed once and reused) — a genuine root switch has nothing worth preserving from the old viewport, same as before.
- `#btnMultilineMsg`'s click handler lost its entire manual-capture block (including the old Link-view `isLinkActive` guard) — trivial now, just flip the flag and `render()`. The Link-view case needs no guard at all anymore: `captureViewAnchor()` only ever runs from inside `renderTable()`, and `renderMainView()` calls `renderLinkView()` instead of `renderTable()` while a `link` node is active, so there's simply no call for it to make during that whole period — nothing stale can linger.

Regression-tested: **Group 61** (`tests/philogg.regression.test.js`) — 61a: selecting a row, scrolling it to a known on-screen offset, then clicking a level-filter pill that removes many rows above it — asserts the resulting `scrollTop` keeps that row's on-screen pixel offset bit-for-bit unchanged in the Filtered view (its Full-view assertion was updated same-day per point 3 below — Full no longer narrows at all, so there's nothing to re-anchor there). 61b: a selected row that gets filtered OUT entirely falls back to reset-to-top, same as no selection. 61d: a selected row that was off-screen before the change (never previously visible) gets revealed centered once it survives into the new view, instead of staying scrolled away. Group 55d (still passing, updated in place — see "Multiline message display toggle" below) covers the no-selection fallback path this generalization also has to keep working, plus the Link-view case specifically.

**2. Switching filters auto-reveals the Filtered view from Full.** A newly *created* or *edited* filter already called `revealFilteredView()` (switches `fhActiveTab` from `"highlight"` to `"filter"` when in tabs layout; no-op in Stacked, both panels already visible — see "Highlight view" above) at each of its own call sites, so its result would actually be on screen. Simply *switching* the active node to an already-existing filter — via the tree, the breadcrumb, arrow-key nav, drag-drop reparenting, undo/redo, or any of the ~30 other places `state.activeId` gets set directly — did not, so browsing filters while on the Full tab silently showed nothing changing.

Fixed once, centrally, instead of touching every one of those call sites: `render()` now tracks the active id across calls (module-level `lastActiveIdForReveal`, updated at the top of every `render()`) and calls `revealFilteredView()` itself whenever `state.activeId` changed AND the new active node's `type === "filter"` — this works because "every state mutation ends in a `render()`" (see `render()`'s own comment) is already an established invariant across the whole app, so a check inside `render()` sees every real activeId change regardless of which of the ~30 call sites caused it. Switching to a plain FILE node deliberately does NOT trigger this — the Full view already shows the whole file regardless of which node is active, so there's nothing to reveal.

Regression-tested: **Group 61c** — switching between two filter nodes while on the Full tab reveals Filtered; already being on Filtered is a no-op; Stacked layout stays unchanged; switching to a plain file node does not reveal.

**3. Same-day follow-up: the level quick-filter no longer narrows the Full view at all, and changing it ALSO auto-reveals Filtered from Full** (person-requested: *"die Log Level Filter sollen sich nicht mehr auf das full Log auswirken. stattdessen soll ein andern der Log Level Filter auch zu einem automatischen Sprung von Full nach Filtered führen"*). `renderHighlightView()` stopped calling `applyLevelFilter()` — `currentHighlightViewEntries` is always the root file's complete entry list now, full stop, regardless of `state.levelFilter`. `renderLevelBar()`'s click handler gained the same `revealFilteredView()` call point 2 added for node switches, so a level-filter change made while on the Full tab jumps to Filtered the same way. See "Highlight view" above ("Level quick-filter applies to the Filtered view ONLY", updated in place) for the full reasoning.

Regression-tested: **Group 61e** — Full view's own entry count stays the file's full count across a level-filter toggle (Filtered's own count still narrows as expected); changing the level filter while on Full reveals Filtered; already on Filtered is a no-op; Stacked stays unchanged. Group 4's older dual-view assertion (32e282b4, originally asserting Full ALSO narrows) updated in place to assert the opposite.

Regression-tested: **Group 61c** — switching between two filter nodes while on the Full tab reveals Filtered; already being on Filtered is a no-op; Stacked layout stays unchanged; switching to a plain file node does not reveal.

## Multiline message display toggle

`#btnMultilineMsg` (`#viewBar`, next to `#btnPinBookmarks`) toggles `state.multilineMessages` — default `false` (unchanged behavior: `.col-msg` stays `white-space:nowrap` + ellipsis, one truncated line per row). On: a `.multiline-messages` class on `<body>` (toggled by `updateMultilineMsgButton()`, the same "reflect state on the button" helper pattern `updatePinBookmarksButton()` uses) switches `.col-msg` to `white-space:pre` — literal `"\n"`s in `e.message` (continuation lines the parser already folds in for stack traces, see "The log format it parses" above) render as real line breaks. Applies to **both** views that render `.col-msg`: the Filtered table (`renderVisibleRows`) and the Highlight/Full view (`renderHighlightVisibleRows`) — a single body-level class reaches both without per-row class bookkeeping. The Extraction table and Link pair view don't use `.col-msg` at all and are untouched.

**Deliberately `pre`, not `pre-wrap`**: a long single line is never soft-wrapped to fit the column width, only the message's own embedded breaks produce new lines. This isn't just fidelity to the request's literal wording ("gemäß der Zeilenumbrüche in der Message") — it's what keeps a row's rendered height **exactly derivable from its message's line count**, with no DOM measurement involved: `messageLineCount(message)` (`message.split("\n").length`) and `rowHeightForEntry(e)` (`ROW_HEIGHT + (n-1) * MULTILINE_LINE_HEIGHT` for `n > 1` lines, else plain `ROW_HEIGHT`) are pure functions of the entry, computable before the row ever touches the DOM. `MULTILINE_LINE_HEIGHT = 15` (px) is duplicated as a literal in the `.multiline-messages .col-msg` CSS rule's `line-height:15px` — the two values must stay in sync by hand if either ever changes, there's no shared source (a CSS custom property would remove that risk if this needs touching again).

**Why the height needs to be exact, not just "close enough": variable-height virtualization.** The Log view's `ROW_HEIGHT = 28`/`BUFFER_ROWS = 10` virtualization (see "Log view" above) assumes every row is the same height — `index * ROW_HEIGHT` locates any row's pixel offset in O(1), which is what makes scrolling a 100k-row file smooth. Multiline mode breaks that assumption (rows vary in height), so exact-but-not-measured heights let a **prefix-sum array** stand in for the same O(1)-ish lookup instead of falling back to a full non-virtualized render (which would have defeated the whole "stays smooth at any file size" goal) or a measure-as-you-scroll virtualizer (real complexity for no benefit when the exact height is already knowable in advance):

- `buildRowOffsets(entries)` returns a `Float64Array` of length `entries.length+1` where `offsets[i]` is the cumulative pixel height of every row before index `i` (`offsets[0] = 0`). Built once whenever the current view list changes — `renderTable()`/`renderHighlightView()`, and the `pinBookmarksInFilteredView`-triggered partial refresh in `toggleBookmark`'s caller — never per scroll frame. `tableRowOffsets`/`highlightRowOffsets` hold the two views' arrays; both stay `null` when `state.multilineMessages` is off, so that path's virtualization code is untouched (`if (state.multilineMessages && tableRowOffsets) { … } else { /* original ROW_HEIGHT arithmetic, byte-for-byte */ }` at every call site below).
- `findOffsetIndex(offsets, y)` binary-searches for the largest row index whose offset is `<= y` — "which row is pixel position y inside." Used everywhere the old code did `Math.floor(scrollTop / ROW_HEIGHT)`.
- Every call site that used to do index↔pixel math via `ROW_HEIGHT` now branches on `state.multilineMessages`: `renderVisibleRows`/`renderHighlightVisibleRows` (which rows to render + the container's `top` offset), `renderTable`/`renderHighlightView` (the scroll spacer's total height — `offsets[total]+22` instead of `total*ROW_HEIGHT+22`), `scrollToIndex`/`scrollToHighlightIndex` (both the target row's top AND its own height, needed for the center/into-view math — a tall row centers differently than a 28px one), and `updateMinimapRenderedRange` (which entries the minimap's "currently rendered subset" rectangle spans).
- The tail-follow "how far from the bottom is the user scrolled" heuristic (`ROW_HEIGHT * 1.5` threshold in the `#tableBody` scroll listener) was deliberately left as a flat constant — it's a coarse "close enough to the bottom" check, not a per-row lookup, and a taller effective threshold under multiline mode is if anything more forgiving, not wrong.

**Toggling re-anchors on the top-visible entry, not on scroll position 0** — originally a dedicated one-shot mechanism built just for this toggle, **superseded this session (2026-08-19) by the general "don't jump" scroll anchoring** described in its own section right after "Highlight view" above: `captureViewAnchor()`/`restoreViewAnchor()`, called automatically inside `renderTable()`/`renderHighlightView()` on every render, not just this one toggle. `#btnMultilineMsg`'s click handler itself is now trivial again — flip `state.multilineMessages`, call `render()` — with no manual capture step of its own. See that section for the current mechanism (including how it now also prioritizes the *selected* row, not just the topmost-visible one).

Regression-tested: **Group 55d** — scrolled well past three rows that are about to grow, toggling on grows `scrollTop` by exactly the sum of those rows' height increase (not 0, not an unchanged raw pixel value) in both views, with the same entry's DOM row still actually rendered at the top afterward; toggling back off shrinks `scrollTop` back to the exact original pixel value; the degenerate already-at-0 case stays at 0; and the Link-view case (toggling while a `link` node is active, no table rendered that cycle) — updated in place this session to assert the view now re-anchors on the entry that was on screen before the Link-view interruption instead of resetting to top, since "reset to top on any other change" is exactly the old default this session's general fix replaced.

**Persistence**: `state.multilineMessages` rides in the exact same `settings` object `buildCacheMeta()`/`restoreSessionFromCache()` already carry `pinBookmarksInFilteredView` in — additive field, no `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION` bump (same reasoning already established for `linkOrderEnforced`/`linkExclusive`, see "Link filter" above). This is the browser-local IndexedDB session cache specifically — **not** threaded into the separate `buildSessionExport`/`runSessionImport` JSON session-file feature, since the request asked for state "im Cache gespeichert" (in the cache), a narrower, different mechanism than that portable-file export.

Regression-tested: **Group 55** (`tests/philogg.regression.test.js`) — 55a covers default-off, per-row heights in both views, the spacer summing real heights, and exact reversion on toggle-off; 55b builds a 50-entry file with one 21-line entry positioned before a jump target and asserts `scrollToIndex` (via `jumpToEntry`) lands on the pixel offset the tall row's real height demands, with the target's DOM row actually rendered — proving the offset-based windowing, not just the toggle's on/off state; 55d (see "re-anchors on the top-visible entry" above) covers the scroll-preservation fix; 55c round-trips the setting through a simulated reload via the shared session-cache mechanism, same pattern as Group 20.

## Log view column visibility / width (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`)

`#btnColumns` (`#viewBar`, next to `#btnMultilineMsg`) opens `#columnsPanel`, a small fixed-position popup (same pattern as `#bookmarksPanel`/`#shortcutsPanel`) with checkboxes for **Δt/Thread/Location/Method** — Time/Level/Message stay always-visible, no checkbox. Each of the six columns (Time/Δt/Level/Thread/Location/Method) also has a drag handle in `#tableHeader` (`.col-resize-handle`) for live width resizing; Message has no width of its own — it's the grid's `1fr` flexible remainder, so growing/shrinking any other column resizes it automatically.

**Applied entirely through `--row-grid`, the CSS custom property `.row-grid` (shared by `#tableHeader`, `#highlightHeader`, and every `.log-row` data row in both Log views) already used as its `grid-template-columns`** — `applyRowGrid()` recomputes the whole string from `state.columnWidths`/`state.columnVisible` and writes it via `document.documentElement.style.setProperty`. This is the reason the feature costs essentially nothing at render time: flipping a checkbox or dragging a handle just changes one CSS variable, and every row (Filter + Highlight, virtualized or not) reflows natively — no per-row DOM writes, no `render()` needed for the drag itself (only at drag-end, to persist). A hidden column's track collapses to `0px` (not removed from the grid, not `display:none` on the per-row `.col-*` spans) specifically to avoid corrupting CSS Grid's row auto-placement — see the mobile-breakpoint paragraph below for the bug this exact mistake caused elsewhere in the same feature.

**Resize handles are positioned via JS, not nested inside `.th`**: `.th` has `overflow:hidden` for its own text-ellipsis truncation, which would clip an absolutely-positioned handle poking out past its own border box. Instead, six `.col-resize-handle` spans sit as siblings of the `.th` elements inside `#tableHeader .row-grid` (`position:relative`, scoped to the header only — `.row-grid` is shared with every data row, which must NOT become a positioning context), each with `data-col` and a `left` computed by `positionColumnResizeHandles`-equivalent logic folded into `applyRowGrid()` itself (cumulative `COLUMN_BAR_WIDTH + Σ(gap + column width)`). Being `position:absolute`, they're out of grid flow entirely and don't consume an auto-placement slot among the real 8 grid items — the same property that makes the 0px-track (not `display:none`) choice above necessary for the *real* row items.

**Mobile breakpoint bug found and fixed while building this**: the pre-existing `@media (max-width:760px)` rule set its own `:root{--row-grid:...}` override AND `.col-thread,.col-loc,.col-method{display:none;}` directly in CSS. Both had to go: (1) an inline style (what `applyRowGrid()` now always sets) unconditionally wins over any media-query rule, so the old override would simply never have applied again once this feature shipped; (2) independently of that, the `display:none` half was **its own latent bug**, discovered by reasoning through it while designing the handles above — a `display:none` grid item doesn't reserve its track, so with 3 of the row's 8 real grid items removed from participation, the remaining 5 (bar/time/delta/level/msg) auto-place sequentially into tracks 1-5, meaning **Message would silently render in track 5** (Thread's slot, 0px wide in the mobile `--row-grid`) instead of track 8 (`1fr`). Neither this session's own reuse of the mechanism nor the original narrow-viewport feature had ever exercised this path in a way that surfaced it. Fixed by folding the narrow-viewport override into `applyRowGrid()` itself (`COLUMN_MOBILE_BREAKPOINT = 760`, `COLUMN_MOBILE_FORCE_HIDDEN = ["thread","loc","method"]` — deliberately narrower than the visibility toggle's own `HIDEABLE_COLUMNS`, since the old mobile rule kept Δt visible, just narrower) — same 0px-track mechanism as the desktop visibility toggle, so no auto-placement risk. `window.addEventListener("resize", applyRowGrid)` keeps it live across the breakpoint.

**Persistence**: `state.columnVisible`/`state.columnWidths`, same `settings` tier as `multilineMessages`/`pinBookmarksInFilteredView` (`buildCacheMeta`/`restoreSessionFromCache`) — global, not per-file. `DEFAULT_COLUMN_WIDTHS` matches the original hardcoded `--row-grid` values exactly, so a first-ever load (or "Reset widths" in the popup) is visually identical to before this feature existed. `COLUMN_MIN_WIDTH = 40` clamps a drag from shrinking a column to nothing.

Regression-tested: **Group 58** — 58a (checkbox toggle collapsing/restoring a track and its own handle's visibility, Reset widths), 58b (drag-resize live width update + `COLUMN_MIN_WIDTH` clamp + no stray effect after mouseup), 58c (persistence across a simulated reload), 58d (the mobile-breakpoint fix — forces Thread/Location/Method to 0px without mutating the stored preference, restores on widening back past the breakpoint).

**Two person-reported follow-up bugs, same day.** (1) `.col-delta`/`.col-time` were missing `overflow:hidden` — unlike `.col-thread`/`.col-loc`/`.col-method`/`.col-msg`, which already had it — so a column collapsed to a `0px` track (via the visibility toggle, or just a very narrow drag) didn't clip its own text; it kept rendering at natural width and spilled into the next column, showing up underneath its (opaque) background instead of actually disappearing ("wenn ich die deltaT Spalte verstecke, bleiben die Einträge in der Tabelle aber hinter den nun darüber liegenden Log-Levels sichtbar"). Fixed by adding `overflow:hidden; text-overflow:ellipsis` to both — see "Log row multi-select + copy" below's own CSS-pitfall paragraph for the *other* half of this same class of bug (background vs. box-shadow) found the same session. (2) `#btnColumns` was missing the `float:left`/sizing override its two siblings (`#btnPinBookmarks`/`#btnMultilineMsg`) both have — see "`#viewBar`" above — so it rendered as a plain, non-floated `29x29px` box sitting in normal flow right after three floated elements, breaking out of their shared line and dragging `#levelBar`/`#breadcrumb` (which come after it in the DOM) down and out of alignment with it ("Die Darstellung der LogLevel und aktuellen Filterkette ist durch die Einführung der neuen Option-Buttons verrückt"). Fixed with the matching `#btnColumns{float:left; margin:0 14px 6px 0; width:auto; height:26px; padding:4px 8px;}` rule. Regression-tested: **Group 60a** (overflow:hidden bugfix) — no dedicated jsdom coverage exists for the float/alignment bugfix (a pure CSS-layout bug with no state signature — same class of thing PROJECT.md's "Testing approach" flags as jsdom's blind spot, verified instead via a live-browser Playwright screenshot).

## Log row multi-select + copy (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` suggestion, person-requested this session)

Ctrl+click toggles a row into/out of `state.logMultiSelect` (a `Set` of entry ids); Shift+click selects the contiguous range from the last-clicked anchor (`logMultiSelectAnchorId`), replacing the set — standard file-explorer semantics, including that a repeated Shift+click re-derives the range from the SAME anchor, not the previous Shift+click's own target. A plain click keeps its pre-existing single-selection behavior unchanged and clears the multi-selection. The first Ctrl+click after a plain click folds the existing `state.selectedId` into the set too, so "click A, then Ctrl+click B" reads as "A and B selected" rather than silently dropping A. `handleLogRowClick(ev, entryId, viewEntries, selectFn)` is the one shared handler wired onto both Log views' row `click` listeners (`currentViewEntries`/`selectEntry` for the Filter table, `currentHighlightViewEntries`/`selectHighlightEntry` for the Highlight/Full view) — `state.logMultiSelect` is shared between them exactly like `state.selectedId` already is, so a range built in one view's rows shows the same tint on the other view's copy of the same entries.

**Ctrl+C copies the raw text** (`copyLogSelectionToClipboard`) — the multi-selected entries (or just `state.selectedId` alone when nothing's multi-selected), looked up via the global `entryIndex` and joined by `entry.raw` (which already accumulates continuation lines for multi-line stack traces, see "The log format it parses"), **sorted chronologically by timestamp** rather than click/DOM order, so a series of out-of-order Ctrl+clicks still copies in log order. This takes priority in the keydown handler over the tree's own filter-node clipboard (`state.clipboard`, existing Ctrl+C/X/V) — but only when `state.focusRegion === "entries"` (the log view, not the tree, was the last thing interacted with); the tree-node copy is otherwise unaffected.

**Rendering**: no full re-render on Ctrl/Shift+click — `updateLogMultiSelectClasses()` toggles the `.row-multi-selected` class directly on whichever rows are currently in the DOM, same in-place-toggle technique `updateSelectedRowClass` already uses and for the same reason (a full `renderVisibleRows()`/`innerHTML` rebuild on every click would break native `dblclick` detection — see CLAUDE.md's gotcha list). A normal `render()` (e.g. after Escape clears the set) still bakes the class into each row's `className` string directly from `state.logMultiSelect` at render time, so scrolling the virtualized window afterward stays correct without any extra sync call.

**CSS pitfall hit and fixed while building this, caught via a real-browser (Playwright) screenshot check, not just the jsdom suite**: the first cut styled `.row-multi-selected` with a plain `background`. It rendered completely invisible except on rows with no level class. Root cause: `.log-row.lvl-error`/`.lvl-warn`/etc. (the existing per-level row tinting) ALSO set `background`, at equal specificity (`.log-row` + one class, `0,0,2` either way) and later in the stylesheet — so the level tint always won outright, not blended. This is the *exact* fight `.pinned-row`'s own comment already documents and solves the same way: switched to `box-shadow:inset 0 0 0 2px var(--accent-strong)` instead, which doesn't touch `background-color` at all and so composes over any level tint unconditionally. Since `.selected` also uses `box-shadow` (a single-value property — two separate class rules setting it don't stack, only one wins per the cascade), an explicit `.log-row.selected.row-multi-selected{box-shadow:inset 0 0 0 1px var(--accent), inset 0 0 0 3px var(--accent-strong);}` combines both shadows for a row that's simultaneously the multi-select's focused row and a member of the set — its higher specificity (three classes vs. two) wins over either rule alone regardless of source order. **Lesson for future row-decoration work here**: `background` is claimed by the level-tinting rules; any new per-row visual state needs `box-shadow` (or a `left`/gutter marker, see `.pinned-row`/`.ctx-bracket`/`.hl-markers`), not `background`, unless the new state is meant to compete with (or is mutually exclusive with) level tinting.

Regression-tested: **Group 57** — plain click behavior unchanged; first Ctrl+click folding the prior selection in; Ctrl+click toggle-off (and its anchor move); Shift+click range selection (replace, not extend) and repeated-Shift-click re-deriving from the same anchor; Ctrl+C priority over the tree clipboard gated on `focusRegion`; Escape clearing state + DOM class; and the selection/class being shared between the Filter and Highlight views. The CSS specificity bug above was caught via manual Playwright screenshot review (jsdom has no layout/paint engine, see "Testing approach" below), not by the automated suite — same class of gap the multiline-toggle CSS-flex-direction bugfix note already flags.

## Extraction table: synthetic Index + t(ms) columns

Every extraction table leads with two synthetic columns, ahead of the pattern's own — person-requested (2026-08-17): a 0-based row-order **Index** and **t (ms)**, the *cumulative* elapsed time in ms since the extraction's first entry (0 on row 0). `INDEX_COL = -2` / `ELAPSED_COL = -1` — negative so they can never collide with `spec.columns`' dense `0..n-1` range, the same stable-`colIndex` identity scheme "Ignored columns" above already established for the pattern's own columns. Both are typed `"int"`, so they're automatically plottable/sortable/assertable/statable through every existing `colIndex`-keyed mechanism (`findExtractColumn`, `extractRowComparator`, `checkAssertion`, `computeColumnStats`, the Plot tab's axis-candidate list) with **no new code** at any of those call sites — the only places that needed to know about them at all were `renderExtractTable` (prepending the two descriptors to `extractColumns` and stamping their values) and `columnColor`.

**t(ms) is cumulative, not a row-to-row delta — a same-session correction.** It first shipped as "ΔT," the gap to the *previous* entry (blank on row 0). The person immediately flagged the design: *"deltaT macht so keinen Sinn für einen zeitlichen Plot. Der erste Eintrag müsste 0 haben, der Rest dann die bis dahin aufsummierten deltaT."* The reasoning holds up: a per-step delta can't work as a plot X value at all — row N's position on that axis would depend on the sum of every prior row's spacing, not on row N's own column value, which is exactly what a plot axis needs. Cumulative elapsed time fixes that: it's a real time axis, just relative (start = 0) rather than absolute. Renamed "t (ms)" to match, since it no longer represents a delta.

**Computation**: `values[ELAPSED_COL] = String(e.ts - entries[0].ts)` — elapsed time from the extraction's first entry, which is naturally `0` for row 0 itself (`e === entries[0]`) with no special-casing needed. Values live as **negative-index expandos on `row.values`** (`values[INDEX_COL] = String(r)` alongside it) rather than as dense array elements — every existing colIndex-keyed bracket read (`row.values[ci]`, used throughout copy/export/sort/plot/assertions) already picks these up unchanged, and `values.length` (relied on by Group 24's "matching itself is untouched" assertion) stays exactly the dense `spec.columns` count it always was, since JS arrays don't count negative-index properties toward `.length` or `.map`/`.forEach` iteration.

**Computed from the pre-sort, chronological entry order** — `entries.map((e, r) => ...)`, before `extractSortCol` is applied, and always relative to `entries[0]` regardless of any later re-sort — same as every other captured value: fixed per row at extraction time, not recomputed when the table is re-sorted by a different column. Re-sorting by Index or t(ms) itself works normally through the existing generic sort machinery.

**`#btnCopySelection`/`#btnCopyAllExtract` removed (this session, person-requested)** — the two buttons that used to sit in `.extract-toolbar-row2` (now-empty `.extract-actions` wrapper removed with them) are gone from the UI. `copyTableSelection()` is unaffected — it's reachable via Ctrl+C whenever `state.tableSelection` is non-empty, independent of any button. `copyWholeExtractTable()` (copies the whole table as TSV **with a header row**, respecting ignored columns — genuinely different output from any cell-range selection, which never includes headers) lost its only UI trigger and is currently unreachable from the UI at all; kept as a function anyway, same precedent as `jumpToFullLog` surviving un-wired to a double-click (see "The three 'active node' views" above) — still directly tested (Group 60c), in case a future entry point wants it.

**Default X axis, for free**: `renderPlotControls`' existing `plottable[0].index` fallback already picks whatever's first in `extractColumns` — since Index is now unconditionally prepended there, it becomes the default X-axis selection for every extraction's plot with no dedicated code path. The Y-column auto-pick (`plotConfig.yColsInit`) was adjusted to prefer a real pattern column over the synthetic ones (`plottable.find(c => c.index >= 0)` before falling back) — defaulting Y to t(ms) (plotted against Index) would be a much less useful first look than the pattern's actual extracted value.

**Header rendering**: the numbered, colored circle badge (`.pattern-chip-num`) that marks a column's position in the pattern is skipped for `colIndex < 0` — Index/t(ms) aren't placeholders in the regex pattern, so a number there would be misleading; they still get the ordinary sort button, type tag, and (since they're plottable) an assertion button.

**Gotcha fixed while adding this**: `columnColor(i)` used a naive `i % COLUMN_PALETTE.length`, which returns a negative result for JS's `%` on negative `i` (e.g. `-1 % 6 === -1`), indexing before the array and silently producing `undefined` → an invalid `background:undefined` inline style. Fixed to `((i % n) + n) % n`. This is exercised anywhere a negative `colIndex` reaches `columnColor` — the header swatch (moot now that the badge is skipped for negatives, but the call still happens), and, non-moot, the Plot tab's Y-column-list swatches and multi-series legend, where t(ms) can legitimately be selected as a Y series.

Regression-tested: **Group 42**, rewritten in place the same session for the cumulative correction — column descriptors/ordering, computed values (explicitly checked against what the wrong per-step value would have been), header DOM (badge presence/absence), body rendering, sorting (descending reverses order, now that every row's value is parseable — unlike the old blank-first-row delta), copy/export, the Plot tab's X/Y defaults, and the `columnColor` fix. Three older groups needed small updates because their assertions assumed the extraction table had no leading columns: **Group 5** and **Group 14** now target their sort/assert buttons by `data-sort-col`/`data-assert-col` instead of "first one in the DOM"; **Group 24**'s column/button counts and copy-export text were bumped by the 2 new always-visible columns.

## Virtualized rendering: extraction table & Link pair view

Both views now only ever put the scrolled-into-view window (plus a small buffer) into the DOM, closing the gap noted in Known limitations ("not virtualized... would need work if someone tries to extract from an entire multi-hundred-thousand-line file with a very loose pattern"). Same underlying idea as the main log table's `renderVisibleRows`/`#tableSpacer`/`#tableRows`, adapted to each view's own DOM shape.

**Extraction table** (`renderExtractVisibleRows`): `extractRowsData`/`extractColumns` always hold the FULL result — sort, cell selection, copy/export, column statistics, and the Plot tab all keep operating on the complete set exactly as before, completely unaffected by virtualization. Only the row-building loop that used to run once per `renderExtractTable()` call moved into its own function, keyed off a fixed `EXTRACT_ROW_HEIGHT` (28px, enforced via an inline `style.height` on every `<tr>`, same reasoning as the main table's `ROW_HEIGHT`) and a `EXTRACT_BUFFER_ROWS` (15) buffer above/below the viewport. Because `#extractHead`/`#extractBody` are a real `<table>` (needed for the sticky header + sticky gutter column + native cell borders the existing UI already relies on), the windowed subset can't be absolutely-positioned the way `#tableRows` is — instead **two spacer `<tr>`s** (a single `<td colspan>` each, sized via inline `height`/`padding:0`/`border:none` so no dedicated CSS rule is needed) stand in for the rows above/below the rendered window, keeping the scrollbar's range correct. `#extractHead` is itself NOT virtualized (one sticky row, always fully rendered) but its own rendered height still occupies scroll-flow space above row 0 (sticky positioning doesn't remove it from layout) — `extractHead.offsetHeight` is read directly and subtracted from `extractScroll.scrollTop` wherever the body's own scroll offset is computed, rather than assuming the header takes 0px. `applyExtractSelectionClasses()` now runs at the end of every windowed re-render (not just the initial full build), so a selection made before scrolling still shows `.cell-selected` correctly on whatever happens to scroll into view later. Switching to a different extraction node now also resets `extractScroll.scrollTop` to 0 — needed once virtualization exists (a stale deep scroll position into a much smaller new result would otherwise render starting mid-air, or entirely past the new table's own end); this was a latent gap before virtualization too, just invisible since every row always had a DOM node regardless of scroll position.

**Link pair view** (`renderLinkVisibleBlocks`): a pair-block's height varies with its tuple size (`getTupleEntries(pair).length` — 2 rows/1 delta for a plain link, N rows/N-1 deltas for a chained/multi-hop tuple, see "Link filter" above), so unlike the two fixed-row-height table views this can't just divide `scrollTop` by a constant. `computeLinkBlockOffsets()` builds a prefix-sum array (`linkBlockOffsets`, length `linkPairsData.length + 1`) analytically from each block's real-entry count via `linkBlockHeight(n) = n·LINK_PAIR_ROW_HEIGHT + (n-1)·LINK_DELTA_HEIGHT + (2n-2)·LINK_ROWS_GAP + LINK_BLOCK_MARGIN` — the exact same constants (`.pair-row`/`.pair-delta` heights now enforced via inline `style.height`, `.pair-rows`' own flex `gap`, `.pair-block`'s `margin-bottom`) the CSS already used, just made exact instead of left to natural layout, so the formula's output is guaranteed correct with no DOM measurement needed. `linkBlockIndexAtOffset()` binary-searches that array to find which block a given pixel offset falls into. The rendered window is buffered in **pixels** (`LINK_BUFFER_PX = 300`), not a row/block count, since blocks aren't uniform height. `#linkBody` moved from a plain block-flow container to the same `#linkSpacer`(`position:relative`, full height)/`#linkBody`(`position:absolute`, offset via inline `top`) split `#tableSpacer`/`#tableRows` already established, since (unlike the extraction table) it's plain `<div>`s, not a native `<table>`, so absolute positioning works directly — no spacer-row workaround needed here.

**Pair selection had to stop being DOM-class-only.** Before virtualization, `.pair-block` for every pair always had a live DOM node, so "which pair is selected" could just be read back off whichever element currently carried `.pair-selected` (`linkBody.querySelector(".pair-selected .pair-brace")`). Once a block can be torn down and rebuilt between a click and any later read (scrolled away, then back), that stops being a safe source of truth — a selection made, then scrolled out of view, would silently vanish the instant the block left the DOM, and `minimapMarkedEntries()` (which used exactly that query) would incorrectly report nothing marked. Fixed by tracking selection as `linkSelectedPairIndex` (an index into `linkPairsData`, not a DOM reference) — `applyLinkSelectionClasses()` re-applies `.pair-selected` to whichever *currently-rendered* block matches that index after every windowed re-render, and `minimapMarkedEntries()`/`applySelection()` (the "a plain row selection supersedes a stale Klammer" clearing logic) both read/write the index directly instead of touching `linkBody` at all. A side effect, not a regression: pair selection now also **persists across a re-render of the same link node** (e.g. toggling a level filter) — previously any full `renderLinkView()` rebuild silently dropped the selection since the DOM class had no state to survive the rebuild; now it does, matching how `state.tableSelection`/`state.selectedId` already behave elsewhere. Switching to a genuinely *different* link node still resets both the scroll position and `linkSelectedPairIndex` (`renderLinkView`'s `linkNodeId !== node.id` check, same pattern `renderExtractTable`'s `extractNodeId` check already used).

The minimap's own "rendered subset" sub-rectangle stays deliberately hidden for the Link view (unaffected by this work) — `updateMinimapRenderedRange` remains gated to the table view specifically; extending it to the Link view's own (differently-shaped) virtualization wasn't part of what this item asked for.

Regression-tested: **Group 45** in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` — extraction table: windowed DOM row count bounded well below a 300-row result, correct top/bottom spacer heights before and after scrolling, select-all still covering every logical cell (not just the rendered subset), a rendered cell correctly showing `.cell-selected` after a windowed re-render, and scroll resetting on a node switch; Link view: `linkPairsData`/`linkBlockOffsets` always reflecting the full 50-pair result regardless of what's rendered, a bounded rendered block count, the render window actually moving to a deterministically-computed block index on scroll, a pair selected then scrolled fully out of view keeping no stale `.pair-selected` anywhere while `minimapMarkedEntries()` still resolves it correctly, `.pair-selected` correctly reappearing once scrolled back, and both scroll position and selection resetting on a genuine node switch.

**Panel-resizer drag handlers now rAF-batch their re-render too** (code-review finding, 2026-08-18): every high-frequency scroll listener above already collapses rapid events onto `requestAnimationFrame` via a `xTicking` boolean guard — `tableBody`/`highlightBody`/`extractScroll`/`linkScroll`'s `"scroll"` listeners all follow this shape. The three panel-resize drag handlers (`#detailResizer`, `#sidebarResizer`, `#fhSplitResizer` — see "Header / toolbar layout"/"Highlight view" for what each resizes) had been missed: their `mousemove` handlers called `renderVisibleRows()`/`renderHighlightVisibleRows()`/`renderTimelineMinimap()` synchronously on every raw event. `mousemove` fires far more often than the display repaints, so a fast drag reran that work (for the sidebar case, two full row-window rebuilds *plus* a full timeline-minimap SVG rebuild — the overlay pass is O(current view size) even with the background pass memoized, see "Known limitations") once per input event instead of once per frame — visible stutter on a large filtered view. Fixed with the exact same `xTicking`/`requestAnimationFrame` guard the scroll listeners already use per handler (`detailResizeTicking`/`sidebarResizeTicking`/`fhResizeTicking`); the geometry write itself (`style.width`/`style.height`, what makes the panel visibly track the cursor) stays **outside** the throttle so there's no added input latency — only the expensive re-render is deferred and collapsed. Regression-tested: **Group 47** — for each of the three resizers, dispatching several rapid `mousemove` events confirms the style geometry updates synchronously on every one while the re-render function stays uncalled until a `setTimeout` lets the batched `requestAnimationFrame` actually fire, at which point it's been called exactly once despite the multiple input events.

## Plotting (inside the extraction table's Plot tab)

Custom SVG chart builder, no library. Line / Bar / Scatter, chosen per-plot via `plotConfig`. Only columns with a numeric-capable type (`isPlottableType`: `float`/`int`/`hex`/`time`) are offered as axis candidates; `time` values are parsed to milliseconds (`parseTimeToMs`) so time columns are plottable too.

- **Line/Bar**: multi-select Y columns (checkboxes, colored to match their extraction-table chip); line points are connected in **row order**, not sorted by X value, because extracted rows are already in the log's chronological order and that's almost always the meaningful sequence.
- **Scatter**: single X, single Y, optional third **color-by-value** column rendered with a blue→yellow→red gradient (`plotColorScale`) plus a legend.
- **Axis scaling**: `niceTicks()` is a standard "nice numbers" algorithm (picks a 1/2/5×10ⁿ step) so tick spacing looks sensible regardless of whether the data is in the hundreds (mm) or 10⁻⁵ range (µm) — this was a specific requirement, not an afterthought. Manual min/max override inputs exist per axis for when auto isn't wanted. For comparing multiple Y-series that live on very different scales in the *same* chart, there's a **"Normalize each series (0–1)"** toggle (only shown with 2+ series selected) — auto-scaling alone can't fix that case since it's one shared axis across series.
- 3D scatter was explicitly deferred (lower priority per the person).
- **Gotcha already hit and fixed**: don't auto-refill an "empty" multi-select (Y columns) on every re-render — it fights the natural "uncheck the old one, check the new one" interaction, since the render in between the two clicks sees zero-selected and silently restores a default. Fixed by only auto-picking Y defaults once per extraction (`plotConfig.yColsInit`), not every render.
- **Bugfix: tailing was silently closing an open axis dropdown** (this session, 2026-08-19, person-reported: *"während ein Log im Tailing ist, kann ich die Plots nicht korrekt Bedienen. wenn ich das dropdown zur Auswahl einer Datenspalte für z. B die x Werte öffne, schließt es sich kurz darauf von selbst."*). `renderExtractTable()` calls `renderPlotControls()` unconditionally whenever the Plot tab is open — including from `onTailChange()`'s plain `render()`, which fires on every tail poll tick while a file is actively being written to. `renderPlotControls()` did a flat `plotControls.innerHTML = html`, tearing down and recreating `#plotXSelect`/`#plotYSelectSingle`/etc. on every one of those calls — a real browser closes an open native `<select>` the instant its element is removed from the document, which is exactly what a tail tick was doing mid-interaction. This is the same class of DOM-identity bug CLAUDE.md's "Known gotchas" already documents for `renderVisibleRows()`/native `dblclick` and `renderTree()`/native `click` during a load tick — just for `<select>`/native dropdown-open state this time. The wasted rebuild itself was also just wrong on the merits: tailing only ever appends **row** data (`extractRowsData`); it never touches the pattern/columns/`plotConfig` the controls' HTML is actually built from, so the DOM never needed to change in the first place. Fixed with a content-diff guard: `html` (the string about to be written) is a pure function of `extractColumns`/`plotConfig`, so comparing it against `lastPlotControlsHtml` (the string from the last real write) is a simple, general "would this rebuild actually change anything" check — identical means skip the `innerHTML` write (and the listener re-attachment right after it, which only makes sense paired with fresh nodes) entirely, leaving whatever's currently open alone. `resetPlotConfig()` clears `lastPlotControlsHtml` too, so a genuine node switch always forces one real rebuild regardless of any coincidental string match. The chart itself (`renderPlotChart()`) is untouched by this and keeps updating live every tick, as it should — only the controls' own DOM churn is guarded. Regression-tested: new **Group 62** — a real `tailTick()` (fake `FileSystemFileHandle`, same technique Group 12 uses) that adds a new extraction row asserts both axis `<select>` elements are the SAME DOM node afterward (not recreated) while the chart's own mark count DID grow to 4; a second, empty tick is equally inert; and a genuine control change (chart type Line→Bar) still produces a new node, proving the guard doesn't suppress real updates.
- **Point/bar → log entry** (this session, 2026-08-18, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`'s "Plot point → log entry" — the Plot tab used to be a dead end, an outlier visible but not reachable): every mark (line/scatter point circle, bar rect — not the assert-violation ring drawn on top of a flagged one) carries `class="plot-mark" data-row="<index into extractRowsData>"` (`p.rowIndex`, already computed for bar's group positioning, reused here). One delegated `click` listener on `#plotSvg` (not per-mark — `renderPlotChart` rebuilds the SVG's `innerHTML` wholesale every render) resolves `ev.target.closest(".plot-mark")` back to `extractRowsData[row].entry` and calls **`jumpToFullLog`** — the same destructive jump the extraction table's own row double-click already uses, for the same reason (see that function's comment): the Plot tab has no Highlight-view companion of its own to reveal into instead. Clicking chart background/axes/gridlines (no mark under the cursor) is a no-op. `.plot-mark{cursor:pointer}` + a `:hover{opacity:.7}` dim are the only visual affordance — no per-mark tooltip, kept deliberately minimal.
- **Equal axis scale** (`plotConfig.axisEqual`, this session, 2026-08-18, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`'s "Axis-Equal" suggestion): a checkbox (`#plotAxisEqual`, default off, hidden for Bar — its X is categorical/one-bar-per-row, so a pixel-per-unit ratio doesn't apply) that keeps X and Y at the same pixel-per-data-unit ratio, so a plot of two same-unit quantities (the motivating case: `Position x=[value:float] y=[value:float]`) isn't visually stretched by the chart area's own aspect ratio. Implementation stays a pure presentation-layer step, deliberately not touching `niceTicks`' output: after the home domain's ticks are computed as usual, whichever axis has the smaller data-units-per-pixel (i.e. the *looser* one — more screen space than it strictly needs) gets its **pixel-mapping domain** (`xHomeDomainMin/Max`/`yHomeDomainMin/Max`, separate from the tick-generating min/max) symmetrically padded until both axes' ratios match; the tighter axis is left untouched. Ticks/gridlines still render at their original "nice" values and positions — the padding only ever adds blank margin beyond the data, it never re-buckets the ticks themselves. Ephemeral view state like `normalize`/`xMin` etc. — not threaded through any persistence carrier.

### Plot zoom / pan / hover tooltip (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested)

Mouse-wheel zoom anchored at the cursor, middle-click-drag panning, an editable zoom-level readout (`#plotZoomBar`: +/- buttons, a typed %, Reset), left-click-drag rectangle zoom (multi-step), and a hover tooltip showing a mark's exact underlying value — for all three chart types (Line/Bar/Scatter), including Bar's categorical X.

**Zoom state**: `plotZoom` (`{x0,x1,y0,y1}` in data-domain units, or `null` for "no zoom, show the home view") — ephemeral like `plotConfig.xMin`/`normalize`, not threaded through any persistence carrier, reset in `resetPlotConfig()` and whenever an axis-shape-changing control fires (chart type, X column, Y column(s)/scatter Y, normalize toggle, a manual X/Y range edit) since a zoom domain computed for the old axis shape is meaningless for the new one.

**"Home" domain and the zoom-out ceiling**: `renderPlotChart` first computes the exact same domain it always did with no zoom (now named `xHomeDomainMin/Max`/`yHomeDomainMin/Max`, post-`axisEqual`-padding) — this is exactly what's shown when `plotZoom` is null (the default view is completely untouched by anything below). `clampZoomAxis(homeLo, homeHi, wantLo, wantHi)`, a small helper nested in `renderPlotChart`, caps the requested span at the home span **plus a small buffer on each side** (`PLOT_ZOOM_OUT_BUFFER = 0.08`, i.e. 8% of the home span per side — bugfix, this session, 2026-08-18, person-reported: a mark sitting exactly on the home view's own edge rendered only half-visible, with the old exact-home clamp giving no way to zoom out or pan even slightly to center it) and keeps the requested position within those same widened bounds (`boundLo/boundHi = homeLo∓buffer .. homeHi±buffer`); zooming IN still has no ceiling — this is "zoom out a *little* past the current default view, zoom in unlimited," the buffer existing purely so an edge mark can be fully centered with some padding around it, not to meaningfully change what "zoomed out" means. The clamped result becomes `xDomainMin/Max`/`yDomainMin/Max`, the actual pixel-mapping domain for that render; ticks/gridlines are recomputed from this *effective* (possibly zoomed) domain every render via a fresh `niceTicks()` call, so grid density stays sensible at any zoom level instead of only ever showing the home view's spacing. Because panning goes through this exact same clamp, the buffer also quietly fixes panning: even at 100% zoom (span == home span) there's now `±buffer` of room to slide the view, where before the position clamp forced `lo === homeLo` exactly whenever `span === homeSpan`, making any pan at the default zoom level a hard no-op.

**Bar charts made zoomable/pannable in X** by generalizing what was previously bespoke `groupW`/row-index arithmetic: a bar chart's raw X domain is simply `[0, rowCount]` (row-index units), fed through `niceTicks`-free but otherwise identical to line/scatter's domain pipeline — `xPix` becomes the single source of truth for row placement (`xPix(rowIndex)`, `xPix(1)-xPix(0)` for the per-row pixel width) instead of `left + groupW*rowIndex` computed directly from `plotW/rowCount`. Zooming into a bar chart's X now shows a subset of rows, wider; the X-tick label loop only walks the row range that overlaps the current domain (`Math.floor(xDomainMin)..Math.ceil(xDomainMax)`) instead of always `0..rowCount`.

**Clipping**: since a zoomed/panned view can legitimately place a point, bar, or line segment outside the plot rectangle (SVG doesn't clip that on its own), all marks render inside a `<g clip-path="url(#plotClip)">` wrapping a `<clipPath>` rect matching the plot area exactly; every legend (the multi-series swatch row, the scatter color-scale strip) renders *after* that group closes, since both sit outside the clip rect's bounds (above/right of it) and would otherwise be invisible.

**Interaction layer** (`plotSvg`/`window` listeners, registered once, reading/writing `plotZoom` and a `plotLastRender` snapshot — geometry + domain written at the end of every `renderPlotChart()` call — so mouse handlers never recompute the chart themselves):
- **Wheel** (`zoomPlotAt(dataX, dataY, factor)`): converts the cursor to data-space via the last render's mapping, proposes a new `plotZoom` scaled around that point (`factor<1` in, `>1` out), ignored when the cursor is outside the plot rectangle. The anchor point is left fixed on screen because the new domain is built symmetrically around it before clamping.
- **Middle-click-drag pan**: rAF-batched exactly like the panel-resizer drag handlers (`plotPanTicking` guard, same shape as `xTicking` elsewhere) — translates the domain by the pixel delta converted to data units, re-clamped every frame by `renderPlotChart` itself, so panning at 100% zoom (home view, no headroom) is naturally a no-op rather than needing a special case.
- **Left-click-drag rectangle zoom** (`PLOT_DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX = 3`, same threshold convention as the timeline minimap's own drag-select): a plain `<div>` overlay (`#plotDragRect`, not SVG — same reasoning as the minimap's drag overlay, a re-render mid-drag would otherwise wipe it) tracks the dragged rectangle in `#plotChartArea`'s own coordinate space; on mouseup, the pixel rect is inverse-mapped to a data-space `{x0,x1,y0,y1}` and set as the new `plotZoom` directly — no extra logic needed for "multi-step" (zooming further into an already-zoomed view), since it's just another absolute domain, clamped the same way. The mouseup's trailing `click` event is swallowed via a one-shot `plotDragSuppressClick` flag, the exact same pattern the minimap's `minimapDragSuppressClick` already established, so a drag never also triggers the mark-click jump-to-log-entry.
- **Zoom-level readout** (`#plotZoomLevelInput`, `updatePlotZoomIndicator()`): shows `round(avg(homeXSpan/curXSpan, homeYSpan/curYSpan) * 100)` as a %, skipped while the field has focus so it doesn't fight active typing. Typing a value (`setPlotZoomLevel`) sets an **absolute** zoom relative to the home span, centered on the current view's own center — not relative to whatever's currently on screen, matching how a zoom-level number is normally read. `+`/`-` buttons call `zoomPlotAt` centered on the current view; Reset sets `plotZoom = null`.
- **Hover tooltip** (`#plotTooltip`, `plotHandleHoverMove`): reads `plotHoverPoints`, one entry per rendered mark rebuilt alongside the marks themselves every render (`{px,py,rowIndex,xCol,yCol,...}` for line/scatter, `{bx,by,bw,bh,...}` for bar). Line/scatter use nearest-point-within-`PLOT_HOVER_MAX_DIST_PX`(14) — their marks are small circles, exact-pixel hit-testing would be too finicky; Bar hit-tests the actual rect bounds directly, since adjacent bars never overlap. The tooltip always looks the value up **live** from `extractRowsData[rowIndex].values[col]` rather than caching a formatted copy, so it shows the real extracted string (the "exact value" requirement) rather than an axis-rounded one. **Positioning** (`positionPlotTooltip`, bugfix this session, 2026-08-18, person-reported: a mark far right in the chart pushed the tooltip partly outside `#plotChartArea`, since the old fixed `cursorX + 14`/`cursorY - 28` offset never accounted for the tooltip's own rendered size): the tooltip's `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` are measured for real (after `innerHTML`/unhiding, so they're non-zero) and used to prefer above-and-right of the cursor, flipping to below and/or left independently on whichever axis actually lacks room, then clamped a final time to `#plotChartArea`'s own bounds (`PLOT_TOOLTIP_MARGIN = 4`) as a backstop. Placing it above the cursor by default (`PLOT_TOOLTIP_GAP = 12` clearance) is also what keeps the tooltip box itself from ever overlapping the pointer, not just an accident of the old fixed offset.

## Value assertions (extraction columns)

Per numeric-capable extraction column (`isPlottableType`: `float`/`int`/`hex`/`time` — same set the Plot tab already offers as axis candidates), an optional expected-value check: either a **range** (min and/or max, either side optional) or a **target ± tolerance**. Stored as `node.assertions = { [colIndex]: { mode: "range"|"target", min, max, target, tolerance } }` on the `extract` node itself — lazily created the first time a column gets one, never on other filter types.

**Checking a value**: `checkAssertion(node, colIndex, rawValue, type)` parses the cell via the same `parseValueForPlot` the Plot tab uses, and returns `true` (violated) / `false` (passed) / `null` (no assertion configured, or the value didn't parse as a number — an unparseable cell is neither a pass nor a violation, same "leave it alone" stance sorting already takes with unparseable values). `assertionSummary(node, colIndex)` scans the current `extractRowsData` once to produce the `{violations, checked, total}` used for the header badge.

**UI**: a small target-icon button (`.extract-assert-btn`, `ICON_TARGET`) appears in every numeric column's `<th>`, next to the existing sort button — same `stopPropagation` pattern on `mousedown`/`click` so opening the dialog doesn't also trigger the header's column-selection drag. Clicking it opens `#assertDialog` (mirrors the `#contextDialog`/`#linkDialog` small-modal pattern), where Range/Target±Tolerance is a two-button mode toggle rather than a dropdown. Once a column has an assertion, its header shows a small badge — "N of M outside range" — colored via `--level-error` only once `violations > 0` (a configured-but-currently-passing assertion stays quiet, not attention-grabbing). Violating cells in the table get `.assert-violation` (a `--level-error-soft` tint + colored text, same visual language as `.log-row.lvl-error`).

**Plot tab**: a point/bar whose value on *either* axis fails that axis's column's own assertion gets an extra error-colored ring/border drawn on top (line: outlined circle; bar: colored stroke; scatter: larger outlined ring) — on the theory that a plot is often where an out-of-range value actually gets *noticed*, since the table's cell tint is easy to scroll past. The ring color (`PLOT_ASSERT_COLOR`) is a plain hex literal matching `--level-error`, not a CSS custom property — kept consistent with every other plot color (`COLUMN_PALETTE`, `PLOT_COLOR_STOPS`), since the whole chart is built as raw SVG attribute strings in JS, not as CSS-styled elements.

**Persistence**: assertions travel with the node through `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson` (filter save/load JSON) and `cloneSubtree` (copy/paste), the same way `inverted`/`linkedId`/`contextBefore` etc. do — an assertion is part of what makes an extraction "the same filter," not a transient view setting. They are **not** reset when the extraction pattern is edited to add/remove columns; a stale assertion on a column index that no longer means what it used to is a known sharp edge (see Known limitations). `node.ignoredColumns` (see "Extraction: live pattern preview + ignored columns" below) follows this exact same 4-touch-point precedent (plus `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree` for undo/redo, which assertions also already went through).

## Column statistics

Min / max / mean / stddev per numeric-capable extraction column (`computeColumnStats`), rendered directly above the table (`#extractStatsBar`, `renderStatsBar`) — no toggle, always visible when at least one column is numeric, empty (and collapsed via `:empty{display:none}`) otherwise. Single pass to collect values + running min/max/sum, second pass for the variance sum — two passes over `extractRowsData`, not `Math.min/max(...spread)`, specifically to avoid the call-stack argument-count ceiling some engines hit on very large arrays (extraction results are already documented as "not virtualized — fine for realistically-sized results," so this stays in that same performance envelope rather than trying to be smarter). Numbers are formatted with the Plot tab's existing `formatAxisNumber` (nice/short representation) rather than a separate formatter.

## Extraction: live pattern preview + ignored columns

From person-supplied `REQUIREMENTS-extract-preview-and-ignore.md`: two related gaps in the extraction workflow — no preview of what a pattern actually captures *while typing it* (only an after-the-fact match count), and no way to have a placeholder anchor the pattern without becoming a permanent column.

### Ignored columns — data model (Option B)

**Option B chosen** over the doc's alternative ("a new `[ignore]` pattern token"): `node.ignoredColumns: number[]` — indices into the compiled pattern's columns — on the `extract` node itself, lazily created the first time a column gets ignored, same convention as `node.assertions`. Chosen because it's the smaller, self-contained change the doc itself flagged as the likely winner: the regex/matching is **completely untouched** (an ignored placeholder still anchors the pattern exactly as before), it's a pure display/export-time filter, and it doesn't need to decide how a new token interacts with column auto-naming (`value`, `value 2`, ...) the way a `[ignore]` token would. `isColumnIgnored(node, colIndex)` / `setColumnIgnored(node, colIndex, ignored)` are the two small helpers everything else goes through.

**A structural consequence worth understanding before touching extraction code again**: `extractColumns` (the module-level global) is no longer a dense, positional array — it's the *currently visible* subset of the compiled pattern's columns, and it can have gaps. Each surviving element keeps its original compiled-pattern position as a stable `.colIndex` property (this is the number `node.ignoredColumns` contains, the number `data-col`/`data-sort-col`/`data-assert-col` DOM attributes carry, and the number `node.assertions`/`plotConfig.xCol`/`.yCols`/`.colorCol`/`extractSortCol` are keyed by) — while `row.values` in each `extractRowsData` entry stays the **full, unfiltered** array (one value per compiled column, ignored or not), since matching itself never changes. `findExtractColumn(colIndex)` resolves a stable `colIndex` back to its (visible) descriptor, returning `null` when that column is currently ignored — every colIndex-keyed lookup (`computeColumnStats`, `assertionSummary`, `openAssertDialog`, `extractRowComparator`, the Plot tab's axis-candidate derivation) goes through it rather than bracket-indexing `extractColumns` directly. The cell-selection subsystem (`rowCells`/`rangeCells`/`columnsRangeCells`/`allCells`) was adjusted the same way — it walks `extractColumns`' actual `.colIndex` values instead of assuming a dense `0..length-1` range, so a shift-click column-range spanning an ignored column simply skips it rather than silently selecting hidden data.
