**Excluded from**, per the requirements doc's list — all confirmed by Group 24's regression coverage: the extraction table (`renderExtractTable` never emits a `<th>`/`<td>` for an ignored column), column statistics (`computeColumnStats` returns `null` for one), the Plot tab's axis-candidate list (an ignored column is simply absent from `extractColumns`, so the existing `plottable` derivation — and its already-existing stale-selection reconciliation for `plotConfig.xCol`/`.yCols`, originally built for pattern edits — transparently also handles a column being ignored/un-ignored, no extra code needed there), copy-to-Excel/TSV export (`copyWholeExtractTable` now maps through each *visible* column's `.colIndex` rather than joining `row.values` wholesale; `extractSelectionToTSV` needed no change at all, since the cell-selection generators above already never produce a selection cell for an ignored column). Value assertions: an ignored column gets no assert button (no `<th>` at all), but — same accepted sharp edge as assertions surviving a pattern edit — an assertion configured *before* a column was ignored is left in `node.assertions` untouched, not deleted; it simply stops being checked/shown while the column stays ignored (`assertionSummary`/`computeColumnStats` both return `null` via `findExtractColumn`).

**Two toggle surfaces**, both wired to the same `setColumnIgnored`:
- **Post-creation**, in the existing `#extractPatternView` (the colored-chip template view above the table): `renderExtractPatternView` gained an optional third `opts` argument, `{ onToggle(colIndex) }` — when present, every chip becomes clickable (`.chip-toggleable`) and dimmed (`.chip-ignored`) when ignored, `title` reflecting the current state. `renderExtractTable` passes the **full, unfiltered** column list here (re-derived from `spec.columns` — NOT the filtered `extractColumns` — since this function independently re-walks every raw placeholder token in the pattern text and must produce exactly one chip per token regardless of ignore state) plus an `onToggle` that calls `setColumnIgnored`/`renderExtractTable` directly on the live node.
- **While authoring**, in the new `#filterPopup` preview (see below) — clicking a highlighted span toggles a *popup-local* working set, `filterPopupIgnored` (a `Set`, module-level `let`), applied to the node only on submit. Pre-populated from the edited node's existing `ignoredColumns` in edit mode (`openEditFilterPopup`), empty in create mode, cleared on close — so a filter can be created with columns already marked ignored, and re-opening Edit shows the current state immediately. `createFilterNode`/`updateFilterNode` both gained a 5th optional `ignoredColumns` parameter for this (backward compatible — every other existing call site is unaffected).

### Live pattern preview (`#filterPatternPreview`, in `#filterPopup`)

While typing/editing a pattern, `evaluateLiveMatch`'s existing 150ms debounce now also calls `updateFilterPatternPreview(val, baseEntries)` (one shared timer, not a second competing debounce) — it finds the *first* of `baseEntries` (the same rows `evaluateLiveMatch`'s match-count already uses) the compiled pattern actually matches, and renders that real message with each capture group's substring wrapped in a `<span>` colored via the same `columnColor`/`COLUMN_PALETTE` scheme the table and post-creation pattern view use, so colors stay visually consistent across all three. `white-space:pre-wrap` (matching `#detailMessage`'s own handling) so a multi-line stack-trace sample renders faithfully. Exact per-group `[start,end]` offsets come from a **second** `RegExp` built from `spec.regexStr` with the `d` (match-indices) flag (`m.indices[i+1]`) — the compiled `regex` actually used for matching is untouched, this is purely a preview-time convenience. No matching sample yet → a muted "No matching sample message yet." note (same spirit as the live-match count's `--warn` "0 of N" state, just phrased for a single-message preview). Hidden entirely for a non-extraction (plain text) filter or an invalid pattern.

**Gotcha hit and fixed**: the preview span's click handler must call `ev.stopPropagation()`. Without it, clicking a span — whose handler synchronously does `filterPatternPreview.innerHTML = ""` to re-render — detaches the clicked span from the DOM *while the click event is still bubbling*. The existing global "click outside a popup closes it" handler (`document`'s click listener, `!filterPopup.contains(ev.target) && ...`) then sees a detached `ev.target`, `contains()` correctly returns `false` for it, and the popup closes itself out from under the click. The post-creation pattern-chip toggle already had this same guard for the identical reason (a chip click re-renders the whole `#extractPatternView`); this is the second instance of the same class of bug, worth remembering for any future click handler that re-renders its own clicked ancestor. **A third instance turned up in the very next session** (person-reported): `treeContextMenu`'s click handler — right-click → "Edit filter…" opened `#filterPopup`, then the still-bubbling click reached the same global handler and closed it right back, since `closeTreeContextMenu()` only toggles a class rather than removing the menu item, so `ev.target` was simply *outside* `#filterPopup` this time rather than detached — same root cause (a click that opens something else must not keep bubbling to a close-on-outside-click listener), different trigger. Fixed the same way; see "Status / changelog" for the bugfix-session entry.

### Testing note

The person-supplied companion `tests/extract-preview-ignore.spec.js` is kept as a standalone file (its own acceptance criteria call for that — a BASELINE group pinning today's "every placeholder is a column" behavior, and a CONDITIONAL group that self-skips until `node.ignoredColumns`/`setColumnIgnored` exists, then activates automatically) — but, following this project's established convention (see README.md "Extending this suite", and the same choice made for `link-chaining.spec.js` in Group 22), the **primary** coverage lives in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` as **Group 24**: the popup preview's span rendering/toggle/submit flow, edit-mode pre-population, the post-creation chip toggle, exclusion from table/stats/assertions/plot/export, and persistence through all four of `cloneSubtree`/undo-redo/save-load/session-cache (session export/import gets this for free, since it reuses the session-cache serialization functions).

## Tree interactions

- **Edit a filter** (`F2` with the node active, or right-click → "Edit filter…"): reopens the `#filterPopup` pre-filled with the node's current value/NOT state, submit button reads "Save" instead of "Add filter". Only offered for `text`/`extract` filters (`openEditFilterPopup`) — the two filter types with a plain value the popup's single input can represent; `and`/`or`/`link` derive their value from two other nodes, and `context` has its own before/after dialog. Updates the existing node in place (`updateFilterNode`: value, `filterType` — typing/removing extraction tokens can flip which one applies —, `inverted`, `name`) rather than creating a new child, then `invalidateAllCaches()` (same global sweep as move/delete/invert, since every descendant's cache derives from this node's result). The live-match preview evaluates against the node's *existing parent* in edit mode (not its own current result) — same base entries as when it was first created. Deliberately not undoable, same as the invert toggle — see "Undo / redo" below.
- **Highlight-colour swatch** (visible on filter-type tree rows, left of the icon — replaces the old always-visible multi-select checkbox that used to sit here; see "Highlight view (Filter/Highlight split)" above): click opens the custom colour picker, right-click clears the colour. Not shown on file rows or `extract` rows (which get an empty spacer instead, for column alignment) — see `renderNode`.
- **Ctrl+click** is the way to build a multi-selection now that the checkbox is gone — it already did this independently of the checkbox (toggles the clicked node into/out of `state.multiSelect`, same as before), so nothing else needed to change here when the checkbox was removed. Also updates the active/viewed node, which the old checkbox intentionally didn't.
- **Single click** (on the row body, not the swatch): sets both the active (viewed) node and resets multi-select to just that node.
- Each row also shows a small **type tag** (`FILE`/`TXT`/`TIME`/`TBL`/`AND`/`OR`/`LNK`, via `typeTagFor(node)`) and, for filter nodes, a **row-count percentage bar** against the parent's count (`getEntries(node.parentId).length`) — a quick visual for how much a given filter narrowed the data. Root file nodes have no parent to compare against, so they render an empty placeholder there instead.
- The moment `state.multiSelect.size >= 2`, an always-visible **action bar** (`#treeActionBar`, `renderTreeActionBar()`) appears above the tree with the applicable buttons — no right-click needed to discover what's possible.
- **Right-click** still opens the equivalent bulk-action context menu (`openTreeContextMenu`) when the clicked node is already part of a ≥2-item selection, for people who prefer it or are mid-drag with the mouse already there.
- **Both surfaces share one source of truth**: `describeBulkActions(nodes)` computes which actions apply (and the explanatory note when none do) and `performBulkAction(action, ids)` runs them — the action bar and the context menu are just two different renderers over the same logic, so they can never drift out of sync:
  - 2+ **files** selected → **Merge**: new chronologically-sorted root file (`mergeFiles`), name = source names joined with " + ", entries reused by reference. **Starts with zero filters** — this was an explicit requirement so existing per-file filter trees aren't disturbed; copy filters over manually afterward if needed.
  - exactly 2 **filters from the same root file** → **Combine (AND)**, **OR**, **Link…**. Different root files → an explanatory disabled note instead (cross-file filter combination is intentionally unsupported; merge the files first if you need it — filters only ever operate within one tree).
  - anything else (mixed types, wrong count) → an explanatory note, never a silent no-op.
  - "Link…" opens a small modal (`#linkDialog`) to pick which of the two is the reference/anchor, direction, and N; the other two actions apply immediately.
- Selected/checked rows get a stronger highlight than before: `--accent-soft` background *plus* the existing 1.5px accent ring (previously ring-only) — see the "Visual language" section for why rings were preferred over fills originally, and why this case gets both.
- The per-row delete (hover-only ✕ icon) is unaffected by any of the above — it stays available on every row regardless of swatch/multi-select state, so bulk selection is never the only way to remove a single node.
- **Copy/Cut/Paste** (Ctrl+C/Ctrl+X/Ctrl+V, operating on `state.activeId`) work for every filter type, including `and`/`or`/`link` (see the Core data model section for why those were briefly excluded and how that was fixed). Copy duplicates and stays pasteable multiple times; Cut is a one-time move, clipboard cleared only once the move actually lands (see below). Del deletes the active filter; both predate the multi-select work and are unaffected by it.
- **Arrow-key navigation** targets one of two panes, tracked by `state.focusRegion` ("entries" | "tree", default "entries"): clicking a tree row, a breadcrumb chip, or dropping a drag onto a new parent sets it to `"tree"`; any entry-table selection (`applySelection`, the single choke point `selectEntry`/`selectHighlightEntry`/`revealInHighlightView` all share) sets it back to `"entries"`. With `"entries"` focus (the default, and the log-table row-selection behavior that predates this feature) ↑/↓ move `state.selectedId` through `currentViewEntries` as before. With `"tree"` focus, ↑/↓/←/→ move `state.activeId` instead (`moveTreeSelection`): ↑/↓ step through `flattenTreeIds()` — the same depth-first, root-then-children order `renderNode` renders in, so it matches the tree's visual top-to-bottom order — while ← jumps to the current node's parent and → to its first child. There's no collapse state to toggle (every node is always expanded), so → is simply "descend one level," not "expand." Deliberately reuses the plain arrow keys rather than a modifier combo, matching how a click already disambiguates which pane you're interacting with.
- **Right-click a single node** (not part of an existing 2+ multi-selection) opens a per-node menu — Copy, Cut, Paste (when the clipboard has something), Delete — the mouse-only equivalent of the keyboard shortcuts above, sharing the exact same underlying functions (`pasteClipboard`, `deleteNode`) via `treeCtxId`. Right-clicking a node that *is* part of a ≥2 multi-selection still opens the bulk-action menu instead (`openTreeContextMenu` branches on this).
- **Drag-and-drop**: any filter row is `draggable`; every node (file or filter) is a valid drop target to become the dragged node's new parent. `dragover`/`drop` call the same `moveNode()` cut/paste already uses, so the linked-cycle guard applies here too — a row that would create one never even gets the `drag-over` highlight, and a rejected drop shows the same toast as a rejected paste. The window-level "drop a file to load it" overlay (`#dropOverlay`, `position:fixed` over everything) is gated by `isFileDrag()` (checks `dataTransfer.types.includes("Files")`) specifically so it doesn't pop up — and swallow the tree's own drop target underneath it — while dragging a filter row; only a real OS file drag shows it.
- **`#sidebar` scrolls its whole column once it overflows** (`overflow-y:auto` on `#sidebar` itself, this session, 2026-08-20, person-reported twice — first for `#tree` alone, then a same-day follow-up screenshot showing the *folder watch* section, which sits above `#tree` and is its own `flex:0 0 auto` block, overflowing past the sidebar's bottom with no scrollbar either). The first pass gave `#tree` its own internal `overflow-y:auto`/`min-height:0`, which covers a tree full of opened files but not a `#folderWatchList` full of not-yet-opened ones sitting above it — two independent flex children can't share one scrollbar. Fixed by moving the scroll container up to `#sidebar` itself (`overflow-y:auto`, its existing `min-height:0` already bounds it correctly against `#main`) and letting `#treeActionBar`/`#folderWatchList`/`#tree` all just take their natural heights (`flex:0 0 auto`) as one continuously-scrolling column, matching how they already render as one visual list. Pure CSS — no jsdom coverage possible (see `tests/README.md` "Known gaps", the `#tableWrap` precedent).

## Undo / redo

Covers filter node **delete** and **move** (drag/drop reparent, and Ctrl+X-then-paste, which is also a move), plain (non-folder) **file delete**, filter **value/pattern edits** (the F2/"Edit filter…" popup and the time-range dialog, including the legacy `after`/`before` → `timerange` migration), the **invert (NOT) toggle**, and **value-assertion** add/clear. Still deliberately out of scope: filter/file *creation*, folder-file close (not destructive — the file just returns to its folder's grayed listing, see "Folder watch"), and context-filter creation (no edit path exists for it at all). A small snapshot-based command stack was chosen over deriving undo generically from every tree-mutating function, since the tree's structural invariants (a node's own `parentId` vs. the *other* nodes that might reference it via `linkedId`, see Core data model) are easier to reason about as "restore exactly this snapshot" than as a generic inverse of arbitrary mutations.

**Delete/move call sites**: the only two a user-facing delete/move should ever go through are `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo(nodeId)` and `moveFilterNodeWithUndo(nodeId, newParentId)` — every existing trigger (Delete key, per-row hover ✕, tree right-click menu, drag-and-drop `drop` handler, Ctrl+X-then-Ctrl+V's move branch) calls one of these instead of `deleteNode`/`moveNode` directly. Anything that bypasses them silently isn't undoable. The one intentional exception is `importFilterJson`'s cleanup of a dangling `linkedRef` on a hand-edited file — that's not a user-initiated delete of an existing node, so it calls `deleteNode` directly. `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo` branches three ways: a folder-loaded file (`node.folderId`) goes to `closeFolderFile` (unchanged, not undoable); any other **file** node snapshots+deletes and pushes a `"deleteFile"` action; a **filter** node does the original `"delete"` action.

**Snapshotting**: `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree` deep-clone a node's whole subtree the same way `cloneSubtree` does for copy/paste, with one crucial difference — **original ids are kept**, not reassigned. This matters because `getEntries()` already treats a missing `linkedId` target as "fail gracefully, empty result" (see Core data model) rather than crashing; keeping the same id means that if some *other* node elsewhere had a `linkedId` pointing into the deleted subtree, restoring it under its original id transparently heals that reference again — no separate bookkeeping needed for "what else pointed at the thing I deleted." Both functions branch on `node.type`: a **file** node's own fields (`entries`, `cacheKey`, `merged`, `tail`) are captured/restored by *reference*, not cloned — the same entry objects and live tail handle just get unplugged from `entryIndex`/`state.rootIds` on delete and plugged back in on undo, same as a real close/reopen; `restoreSubtree`'s file branch also re-adds every entry to `entryIndex` and calls `persistFileNode` (fire-and-forget, re-creating the session-cache record `cacheDeleteFile` dropped) since a file is never structurally nested, this branch only ever runs at the top of a `"deleteFile"` snapshot — its filter-type children still go through the existing branch.

**The generic "edit" kind**: unlike delete/move, a value edit has no cheap structural inverse to derive, so `captureNodeFields(node)`/`applyNodeFields(node, fields)` capture/restore a node's whole set of mutable, non-structural fields (`filterType`, `value`, `inverted`, `name`, `ignoredColumns`, `caseSensitive`, `columns`, `assertions`) wholesale. `withFieldEditUndo(nodeId, mutateFn)` captures a `before` snapshot, runs the mutation, captures `after`, and pushes `{ kind: "edit", nodeId, before, after }` — `undo()` applies `before`, `redo()` applies `after`. Four call sites: `updateFilterNodeWithUndo`/`updateTimeRangeFilterNodeWithUndo` (thin wrappers around the existing `updateFilterNode`/`updateTimeRangeFilterNode` mutators, called from `commitFilter` and the time-range dialog's submit handler), `toggleInvertWithUndo` (the tree context menu's "Invert (NOT)" action), and the value-assertion dialog's Save/Clear handlers (call `withFieldEditUndo` directly, since their mutation is a one-liner). The plain `updateFilterNode`/`updateTimeRangeFilterNode` mutators still exist undecorated — nothing else in the codebase calls them without the `WithUndo` wrapper.

**The stack**: `undoStack`/`redoStack`, capped at `UNDO_STACK_LIMIT = 50` (oldest entries drop off silently). `pushUndo()` — called only from the wrapped mutators above — always clears `redoStack`, since a genuinely new action makes any previously-undone branch unreachable (standard undo/redo semantics). `undo()`/`redo()` themselves move the same action object directly between the two stacks (applying the inverse or re-applying the forward mutation) without going through `pushUndo`, so they never clear each other's history — undoing three deletes and redoing one still leaves the other two redoable.

**UI**: `#btnUndo`/`#btnRedo` (toolbar, left of the theme toggle) — disabled via the plain `disabled` attribute when their respective stack is empty, tooltip names which action would be undone/redone next (`UNDO_KIND_LABELS` maps each `kind` to its human-readable label: delete/file delete/move/edit). `Ctrl+Z` undoes, `Ctrl+Shift+Z` **and** `Ctrl+Y` both redo (the second purely for muscle-memory compatibility with editors that use one or the other). A toast (`showCopyToast`) confirms which kind of action was undone/redone, since the tree can be deep enough that the effect isn't always visually obvious at a glance.

## Bookmarks

`state.bookmarks`: `Map<entryId, { note, bookmarkedAt }>` — **not part of filter save/load**, since a bookmark anchors a spot in the *data*, not a filter definition; it doesn't belong in a filter-tree JSON file the way `inverted` or a value assertion does. Originally session-only; now persisted across reloads by the session cache (as file + entry ordinal, since entry ids don't survive a reload — see "Session cache"). Deliberately scoped to real log entries selected in the plain log view (`state.selectedId`) — not link-view pair entries or extraction-table rows, which have synthetic/derived ids that are never added to `entryIndex` (see Core data model), so a bookmark on one could never be resolved back to a real row later. The row context menu (`#ctxBookmark`) and `openContextMenu` hide/no-op that menu item when the right-clicked entry isn't in `entryIndex` for exactly this reason.

**Toggling**: right-click a row → "Bookmark this row" / "Remove bookmark" (`#ctxBookmark`, label reflects current state), or press **B** with a row selected (`state.selectedId`). Bookmarked rows get a small filled star (`.row-bookmark-dot`, reuses `ICON_BOOKMARK_FILLED`) in the same left-gutter slot the context-filter bracket/anchor-dot uses — the two can in principle land in the same visual spot if a row is both a context-filter reference *and* bookmarked; accepted as a minor, rare cosmetic overlap rather than reserving a second gutter slot for it.

**Jumping**: `jumpToEntry(entryId)` is a generalized version of the existing `jumpToFullLog(entry)` — the latter assumes the entry belongs to whatever chain is *currently active* (true for every existing caller, which all operate on a row already on screen), which doesn't hold for a bookmark clicked from the panel while a completely different file/filter is showing. `jumpToEntry` instead resolves the entry's own root file via `findRootIdForEntry(entryId)` (a linear scan over `state.rootIds`, O(root file size) — fine since this only runs on a rare user click, not a render-path hot loop, same reasoning already applied to the timeline minimap's per-render rebuild) before switching `state.activeId`, clearing the level filter, and setting `state.scrollTargetId`, same as `jumpToFullLog`.

**Panel**: `#btnBookmarks` (toolbar) shows a count badge (`.toolbar-badge`) when non-empty and opens `#bookmarksPanel`, a `position:fixed` dropdown (same positioning style as the context menus) listing bookmarks sorted by entry timestamp, each with a level badge, truncated message, an editable note (plain `<input>`, saved on `change`), and a remove button. Clicking a row (outside the note input, which stops propagation) calls `jumpToEntry` and closes the panel. A bookmark whose entry no longer resolves (`entryIndex` lookup failed — e.g. the file was closed) renders as "Entry no longer available" with only a remove action, rather than being silently dropped from the list.

## Pin bookmarks into the Filtered View

From `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` item 1 (person-requested, modeled on glogg/klogg's "marks" — lines marked in the main view that always break through the search pattern into the filtered results, merged in log order). Deliberately built as an extension of the existing Bookmarks feature rather than a third marker type, to avoid a second visual marker competing for the same left-gutter slot the bookmark dot and context-filter anchor dot already share (see "Bookmarks"), and because a bookmark's existing meaning — "this entry matters regardless of context" — already implies the pin semantics; introducing a parallel concept would just duplicate it.

**Toggle**: `state.pinBookmarksInFilteredView` (bool, default `false`), a toolbar button `#btnPinBookmarks` (`ICON_PIN`, a map-pin glyph). It's a plain persistent on/off switch, not a popup — styled via a general-purpose `.toolbar-icon-btn.active` state (reused accent colors, same ones `.crumb.current` already uses) rather than anything bookmark-specific, so any future toolbar toggle can reuse it. Deliberately styled as its own control rather than a `.level-btn` pill: the level pills are subtractive (AND — narrows the result set; empty selection is the "show everything" special case), while pinning is additive (OR — adds entries the filter chain would otherwise exclude). Visually identical pills with inverted logic in the same row was judged a likely source of confusion, so pinning keeps its own distinct look instead. **Location (moved this session, person-requested)**: originally sat in the header next to `#btnBookmarks`; now lives in `#viewBar` itself, floated between `#fhTabs` and `#levelBar` — the row that controls what the active views show/hide, which is what this toggle does, so it belongs there rather than among the header's mostly popup-trigger buttons. The move is purely positional: same element, same id, same `.toolbar-icon-btn` class and click handler, still deliberately *not* inside `#levelBar` itself or restyled to match `.level-btn` (see "Header / toolbar layout" for the float mechanics).

**Merge logic** (`getVisibleEntries()`, the function `renderTable()`'s plain-table path already used for "node filter + level quick-filter"): when the toggle is on, the filter-tree result is unioned with the bookmarks belonging to the *active root file* — resolved via a new `getPinnedBookmarkEntries(rootId, orderIndexMap)` — and the union is re-sorted into that root's own chronological (array) order. A pin is **absolute**: it bypasses both the filter tree's own result *and* the level filter, the same way glogg/klogg's marks bypass the search pattern — consistent with the "regardless of the filters" framing the feature was originally requested with. `getPinnedBookmarkEntries` reuses `buildOrderIndexMap` (already used for the link filter's same-timestamp tie-break) as a combined membership test ("does this bookmark belong to this root") and position source for the final merge sort — a single O(root size) map build per render instead of an O(root size) scan per bookmark the way `findRootIdForEntry` would cost if reused here instead.

**Gotcha (bugfixed this session, person-reported)**: `toggleBookmark()` is a second choke point that can change WHICH rows the Filtered view shows, not just `render()`/`renderTable()` — while pin mode is on, adding or removing a bookmark can add or drop a row, so `toggleBookmark()` must recompute `currentViewEntries`/`pinnedOnlyIds` (i.e. call `getVisibleEntries()` again) itself before its own `renderVisibleRows()` repaint, guarded behind `state.pinBookmarksInFilteredView` so the common (pin-off) case is untouched. Deliberately does **not** call `renderTable()` for this — that always resets scroll to the top, which would yank the reading position on every bookmark toggle. See "Status / changelog" for the fix.

**Scope decisions made this session** (the backlog doc's open questions):
- *Persistence*: yes — part of `state`'s persisted settings, same tier as `levelFilter`/`sortColumn`. Written to and restored from all three existing settings carriers: the IndexedDB session cache (`buildCacheMeta`/`restoreSessionFromCache`), and the portable session export/import JSON (`buildSessionExport`/its import counterpart) — kept alongside `levelFilter`/`sortColumn` in all three places rather than singled out, since it's the same kind of view preference.
- *Extract / Link views*: **unaffected, deliberately** — not by an explicit guard, but structurally: `getVisibleEntries()` is only ever called from `renderTable()`, which only runs for the plain-table path (`renderMainView` routes extract nodes to `renderExtractTable()` and link nodes to `renderLinkView()` instead, neither of which calls it). This falls out of the existing view-routing, not a new special case, and matches the precedent already set by Bookmarks itself being scoped to real `entryIndex`-backed entries only (extraction rows and link pairs have synthetic ids that were never bookmarkable to begin with).
- *Visual treatment*: rows present only because of a pin (not because they matched the filter chain) get `.pinned-row` — an inset left `box-shadow` (`--accent-strong`) rather than a background tint, so it doesn't fight the existing per-level row background (`lvl-error`/`lvl-warn`/etc.). A bookmarked row that *also* genuinely matches the filter does **not** get this class — it's a normal match, not a "here only because of the pin" row, and `renderVisibleRows` distinguishes the two via a `pinnedOnlyIds` set refreshed on every `getVisibleEntries()` call. The status strip also surfaces a `"· N pinned"` suffix when `pinnedOnlyIds` is non-empty, so a row that visibly doesn't match the active filter text doesn't read as a bug.

**Non-goals**: no cross-root/cross-file pinning (scoped to the active root, same as the merge's `orderIndexMap`); no changes to the Bookmarks panel, notes, or `jumpToEntry` themselves — this is purely additive to how bookmarks are *surfaced* in the Filtered View.

## Tailing (live file updates)

Files opened with a **FileSystemFileHandle** (File System Access API — the "Open files…" picker via `showOpenFilePicker`, or drag&drop via `DataTransferItem.getAsFileSystemHandle()`, both Chromium) get a `node.tail = { handle, offset, pending, failed, busy, errorCount, lastGrowth, wasLive }` and are polled every `TAIL_POLL_MS` (1500ms) by `tailTick()`: `handle.getFile()` returns a *fresh* snapshot each call, and if it grew, only the new byte range (`file.slice(offset)`) is read and parsed incrementally by `appendTailText()` into the existing `entries` array — handling chunks that start mid-entry (stack-trace continuation lines written across polls) and keeping an unterminated trailing line in `tail.pending` until its newline arrives. On any change, caches are invalidated globally (`invalidateAllCaches`, same `linkedId` reasoning as for moves), so **filtered views update live too**. Truncation/rotation (`size < offset`) resets that file's entries and re-reads from 0. Files loaded as plain `File` objects (Firefox, the legacy `<input>` fallback, drops without handle support) are byte snapshots and simply never get a `node.tail` — they load normally, without tailing. Tailed files show a pulsing accent dot (`.tree-live`) in the tree.

**The live dot means "actively receiving new entries right now", not just "still polled"** (`isTailLive(t)`, this session, 2026-08-19, person-reported): `t.lastGrowth` is stamped on every real byte-level change (a genuine append, or a truncation/rotation reset), and the dot only shows while `Date.now() - t.lastGrowth < TAIL_LIVE_MS` (10s) *and* `!t.failed` — a valid, unfailed handle alone is no longer enough. Without this, a file a writer had moved on from (idle, or rotated away from in Folder watch) kept its dot lit forever, since the handle stays perfectly readable, just idle — `t.failed` only ever flips on read errors, never on inactivity. Because a poll tick with nothing new otherwise triggers no re-render at all, `tailTick()` also does a second, cheap pass every tick comparing each tail's current live status against its own cached `t.wasLive` and fires a `renderTree()` (not a full `render()`, no caches to touch) when it flips purely from time passing — otherwise a dot could only go dark on the *next* unrelated tail change, not on its own. See `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 63a/63b.

**Read failures are retried, not fatal on the first one**: a `getFile()`/read failure increments `t.errorCount` and is logged via `console.warn` (previously silent — see "Known limitations"), but only sets `failed` (which stops polling and hides the live dot) once `errorCount` reaches `TAIL_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_ERRORS` (5) **in a row** — any clean poll in between resets the streak to 0. This distinguishes a transient hiccup (most plausibly the writer briefly holding the file locked without shared-read access while it appends a line — an ordinary condition for a log actively being written to by another process) from a genuinely permanent one (moved/deleted/permission revoked), which fails every single poll and still reaches the threshold in ~7.5s.

**Survives a reload**: `persistFileNode` stashes `node.tail.handle` (if any) into the file's session-cache record, and boot-time `restoreSessionFromCache` calls `tryReattachFileTail(node, handle)` for each restored non-folder file — `handle.queryPermission({mode:"read"})` needs no user gesture, so this either silently resumes tailing (with a fresh `offset` computed from the restored text's own length) or leaves the file a static snapshot, same shape `restoreWatchedFolders` already uses for directory handles. See "Known limitations" for the bug this fixed.

**Desktop wrapper files also get tailed** (Bugfix, this session, 2026-08-22, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` item 11): a file opened via the desktop app's launch argument, file association, or macOS "open" goes through `loadUrlIntoTree` (see "Deep-link loading (?url=)" below), not the FileSystemHandle path above — it used to build its node from already-fetched text alone and never get a `node.tail`. `isDesktopLocalUrl(url)` now recognizes the desktop wrapper's own `philogg://local/<id>/…` scheme (every fetch to it re-reads the file fresh off disk, see `desktop/main.js` `registerProtocol`) and attaches a `node.tail` whose `handle` (`urlTailHandle`) just re-`fetch()`es that same URL and wraps the bytes in a `Blob` — a drop-in stand-in for `FileSystemFileHandle.getFile()`, so it needs no changes to `tailTick`/`appendTailText` at all. An ordinary `http(s)` `?url=` (CI report link) is deliberately left untouched. Since this reuses the same unconditional poll loop, a desktop-opened file that never grows still gets re-checked every `TAIL_POLL_MS` for as long as the file stays open — the backlog item's secondary ask ("periodically re-check static files for changes") falls out of this for free rather than needing separate handling. See `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 89.

**Follow behavior**: while `state.tailFollow` is true and the active node's root file is tailed (`isTailedLogViewActive()`), the log view scrolls with the newest entry. Manual scrolling away from the bottom or clicking/selecting a row (incl. arrow keys) disengages follow; the floating **`#tailJumpBtn`** ("Newest", bottom-right over the table) jumps back to the end and re-engages it. Programmatic scrolls (follow itself, jump-to-entry, node-switch reset) are distinguished from user scrolls via a timestamp (`lastProgrammaticScrollTs`, all programmatic writes go through `setTableScroll()`) so they never disengage follow. Tail-driven re-renders route through `pendingTableScroll` (`"bottom"` = follow, `"keep"` = preserve reading position) so `renderTable()`'s normal reset-to-top never yanks the view during live updates; follow only moves the view when the file *behind the current view* grew.

## File loading: usable while it loads, progress on the real row

A file being read+parsed (`loadOneFileIntoTree`, shared by `loadFileDescriptors` for a plain "Open…"/drag-drop load and `loadFolderFile` for a watched-folder file — see below) used to block the whole app behind a full-screen `#progressOverlay` pop-up. That was replaced (2026-08-18) with a `.tree-loading-row` placeholder shown while the file loaded — and THAT was superseded again, same session, by a person request to go further: *"die Datei, soweit geladen, direkt zu visualisieren und die Minimap fortlaufend zu befüllen... ähnlich wie eine Datei zu tailen"* (make the file directly usable as it loads, filling in the minimap continuously — similar to tailing a file). The placeholder row is gone; the **real file node now exists, and is fully interactive, from the instant loading starts** — before a single byte has even been read — with just a thin progress fill riding along on its own row until parsing finishes.

**`createFileNode(name, folderId)`** builds the node (`entries: []`, `loadFraction: 0`) and inserts it into `state.nodes`/`state.rootIds`/`state.activeId` synchronously, before any async work — so by the time `loadOneFileIntoTree`/`addFile` hits their first `await`, the row is already a normal, selectable, filterable tree row (click, drag, delete, right-click all work on it exactly like any loaded file), just with zero entries so far.

**Entries stream in during the parse, not just tailing does it**: `parseLogTextAsync(text, node, onProgress)` now takes the node directly and pushes each completed entry onto `node.entries` (and `entryIndex`) as soon as it's found, instead of building a private array and handing it all over once the whole file is parsed — the exact same incremental-append shape `appendTailText` already uses for live tailing, just driven by the initial parse instead of a 1.5s poll. Since `getEntries()` for a file node returns `node.entries` directly (no memoization layer), every downstream consumer — the tree row's own count, the Filtered/Full views, the level bar, the timeline minimap — sees the growing file live, with **no scrolling to follow it**: `renderTable()`'s existing "start at the top of a new list" default (`pendingTableScroll` untouched) applies to every incremental render exactly as it would to any other node switch, so the view just stays wherever it is while the file streams in underneath — unlike tail auto-follow, which is opt-in and only engages for an already-fully-open file.

**A load tick never rebuilds `#tree` or the level bar** — both were found to, in three same-session follow-ups (see below), and every time the symptom was the same class of bug: some interactive element not reliably responding while a file loaded/animated, because it kept getting torn down and recreated. `renderTree()`/`renderLevelBar()` both do `el.innerHTML = ""` then rebuild everything from scratch; a native click only fires if mousedown/mouseup land on the same, still-attached element, so tearing that element down and recreating an equal-but-different one between the two — up to once per animation frame — is exactly what breaks it. The current split, after all three fixes:

- **`updateLoadRowLiveData(rootId)`** — direct DOM writes to the loading file's own row AND every currently-rendered filter row already created under it: a file row's `.tree-load-fill` width, and every row's `.tree-count` text (a filter row's `.tree-pct-fill`/`.tree-pct` percentage-of-parent too) — all found via a `row.dataset.nodeId` `renderNode()` now stamps on **every** tree row (file or filter), same "cheap write on a hot path" idea the pre-2026-08-18 `setLoadingFileProgress` used. A small recursive walk of the subtree under `rootId`, not just the root's own row — a filter created while the file is still loading needs its own count to keep growing live too, not freeze at its creation-time value. Called on **every** progress tick (`readFileWithProgress`'s byte-level `onprogress`, `parseLogTextAsync`'s per-`PARSE_CHUNK_LINES`-chunk `onProgress`), synchronously, active view or not — it's cheap and never touches `#tree`'s structure, so there's nothing to throttle.
- **`updateLevelBarCounts()`** — same idea, for the level-filter bar: refreshes each of the four `.level-btn`'s own `.cnt` text (matched via a `btn.dataset.level` `renderLevelBar()` now stamps on each button) without touching which buttons exist or their `.active` state — that only ever changes via a real click, which already goes through the full `renderLevelBar()`.
- **`renderLoadTickMainView()`** — the table/minimap/status a person actually watches fill in while the loading file IS the active view (Group 49's live behavior), built from the same functions `render()` calls MINUS `renderTree()`, MINUS `renderLevelBar()` (replaced by `updateLevelBarCounts()` above), and minus breadcrumb/detail-panel/tail-jump-button, which can't change from a parse tick alone.
- **`scheduleLoadRender(rootId)`** — calls `updateLoadRowLiveData` unconditionally, then, only while `loadRenderRootIsActive(rootId)` (`getRootFileId(state.activeId) === rootId`), coalesces `renderLoadTickMainView` onto `requestAnimationFrame` (same batching shape as the scroll/resize handlers — `scrollTicking`), re-checking the active state a second time **inside the rAF callback itself** (not just at schedule time) so a switch-away between scheduling and the next actual frame doesn't still cost one stray call.
- **`flushLoadRender(rootId)`** — cancels any pending rAF tick and runs a **real, full `render()`** (including `renderTree()`/`renderLevelBar()`) immediately — used only at the true structural moments of a load, `createFileNode` (a row appearing) and natural completion (the progress fill disappearing), never per-tick. A one-off cost either way, so the full rebuild there is fine; this was also the fix for a real, caught-in-testing bug (Group 47's resizer-batching test started intermittently seeing one extra render from a leftover load tick landing mid-drag, before `flushLoadRender` existed to cancel it).

**Both `scheduleLoadRender`/`flushLoadRender` also call `invalidateCachesForRoots([rootId])` before doing anything else**, active view or not — a file node's `entries` growing mid-parse is exactly the kind of content change `node._cache`/`node._levelCounts` don't otherwise know to distrust (that memoization assumes a node's own result only changes on structural moves/deletes — see "Memoization" above), so without this the level bar would cache whatever (possibly still-empty) counts happened to be current at the very first render right after `createFileNode`, and never update again even once the file finished loading. Same reasoning `onTailChange`'s own `invalidateCachesForRoots` call already established for tail ticks; this was a real regression caught while writing Group 49's test (level counts came back `{}` after a full load), not a hypothetical.

**Fix 1 — a background load (not the active view) stays cheap** (person-reported same-session follow-up: *"Aktuell blockiert das Laden und die Animation teilweise das UI. Ich möchte eine Datei Laden und während die Animation läuft auf eine andere, bereits geladenen Datei wechseln können. Das UI soll dann sofort bedienbar sein... Von dem gerade ladenden Log möchte ich dann nur noch den Fortschrittsbalken am Dateinamen sehen."*). Group 49's original `scheduleLoadRender` ran a full `render()` on every chunk regardless of what was actually on screen — a second file loading in the background, while a different already-open file was the active view, competed for full tree/table/minimap rebuilds on every animation frame and made switching to it feel sluggish. `loadRenderRootIsActive` + the row-update cheap path (above) fixed this: starting a new load still shows it live (a fresh load auto-activates, unchanged); switching to a different already-loaded file makes it the active view immediately with nothing disturbed; the background file's own row keeps updating, cheaply; switching back resumes full live updates for it.

**Fix 2 — the ACTIVE loading file's own ticks don't rebuild `#tree` either** (person-reported same-session follow-up to fix 1, since it only covered the background case: *"Während eine Datei lädt, muss ich noch immer mehrfach auf eine andere Klicken bis der Wechel erfolgt, es scheint nicht jeder Klick registriert zu werden. Nach dem Wechsel auf die bereits vorhandene Datei funktioniert aber alles flüssig..."*). Fix 1 left `scheduleLoadRender` calling the FULL `render()` — `renderTree()` included — whenever the loading file (auto-activated from the start) WAS the active view, i.e. for the entire early part of every normal single-file load. That full tree rebuild, up to once per animation frame, was exactly what made a click on some other, already-loaded row need several attempts before it registered — this is the SAME class of DOM-identity bug CLAUDE.md's "Known gotchas" already documents for `renderVisibleRows()`/native `dblclick`, just for `renderTree()`/native `click`. `renderLoadTickMainView` (above) is the fix: it updates everything the active loading view actually needs to show progress, without ever calling `renderTree()`, so every OTHER row's DOM node — and any click gesture in flight on one — survives untouched for the whole loading period.

**Fix 3 — the level-filter bar and filter-child rows stay live and clickable too, matching tailing** (person-reported same-session follow-up to fix 2: *"Jetzt würde ich aber gerne auch schon während des Ladevorgangs in der Lage sein, Log-Level Filter zu bedienen, neue Filter anlegen, etc. Also alles was ich auch tun könnte, wenn es sich um ein Tailing und nicht um einen Ladevorgang handeln würde."*). Fix 2's `renderLoadTickMainView` still called the full `renderLevelBar()` every tick — the exact same class of bug as `renderTree()`, just for the four level-filter buttons: it recreates all four (and their click listeners) from scratch, so toggling one while the loading file was active suffered the identical click-loss symptom tree rows had. `updateLevelBarCounts` (above) fixes it the same way. Filter *creation* itself (the popup, `createFilterNode`) was never actually blocked by anything load-related — it's a discrete action, independent of the per-tick render path, and `#filterPopup` is a static top-level element `renderMainView()`/`renderTree()` never touch — but a filter created under a still-loading file used to have its tree row's count freeze at whatever it was at creation time, since only the root's own row got live updates before this fix; `updateLoadRowLiveData`'s subtree walk (above) closes that gap, so a new filter's row keeps growing live exactly as it would if the same growth came from tailing instead of the initial parse.

**The read phase** (`readFileWithProgress`, real `File` objects only — `loadOneFileIntoTree`) still runs before there's any text to parse, so it drives `node.loadFraction` across both phases with the pre-existing `READ_WEIGHT`/`PARSE_WEIGHT` split (0.55/0.45) on the SAME row the whole time — there's no separate placeholder to hand off from/to anymore. `addFile` (text already fully in memory — session-cache restore, embedded-log import) has no read phase, so it just creates the node and parses straight into it, no weighting needed (`loadFraction` is a plain 0..1 parse fraction). A load that fails (read or parse error) removes the empty node it created (`catch` → `deleteNode`) instead of leaving a permanently-empty file behind, mirroring the old ghost-row's guaranteed cleanup on error.

**Rendering**: `renderNode()` draws the progress fill (`.tree-load-track`/`.tree-load-fill`, a thin 2px bar pinned to the row's bottom edge — `.tree-row` is already `position:relative`) directly on a file row whenever `typeof node.loadFraction === "number"`, alongside all its normal interactive chrome (swatch spacer, icon, label, count, delete button, click/drag handlers) — it's not a special row shape anymore, just an extra element on the same one. `renderFolderSection()` (watched-folder files) finds a still-loading root node by `(folderId, name)` among `state.rootIds` as a fallback when `rec.nodeId` isn't linked yet (that only happens once `loadFolderFile`'s own `await` resolves, well after the node itself already exists), so the real, progress-carrying row appears in place of that file's grayed `.folder-watch-file` placeholder immediately, not just once loading finishes.

**Test coverage**: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 48b/48c cover the row-identity/interactivity side — the real (not a placeholder) row exists and is clickable the instant loading starts, carries the progress fill, and the fill disappears once loading finishes, for both a plain load and a watched-folder file (via the by-name fallback, since `rec.nodeId` isn't linked yet at that point). Group 49 covers the actual "usable while loading" behavior: entries/tree-row-count/level-bar-counts all reflecting a genuinely mid-parse state (not zero, not the final total) on a 9000-line file forced across multiple `PARSE_CHUNK_LINES` chunks; level-bar counts being correct again once loading finishes (the staleness bug above); and the Filtered view not auto-scrolling to chase the growing content. Group 50 covers the background-load cheapness fix: `render()` and `requestAnimationFrame`/`cancelAnimationFrame` mocked (the latter manually flushable, sidestepping a real-timing race against the parse loop's own much-faster `setTimeout(0)` yields) to prove zero `render()` calls across several background parse chunks while a different file is active, the fire-time re-check correctly falling back to the cheap path for a tick that was scheduled before a switch-away, the background row's progress fill still updating directly, the active file's own view staying completely undisturbed, and exactly one full render at the background load's natural completion. Group 51 covers the ACTIVE-view tree-identity fix: `renderTree()` mocked to count calls, proving zero calls across several real parse ticks while the loading file IS the active view (its own view still genuinely updating live, via `getVisibleEntries()`); a different, already-loaded row's DOM element captured right after the one legitimate structural rebuild (the loading file's own creation) stays the exact same object through every subsequent tick; and — the strongest proof — a real click dispatched on that held reference still switches to it, which would fail if the row had been torn down and replaced at any point. Group 52 covers the level-bar/filter-creation fix the same way: `renderLevelBar()` mocked to count calls, proving zero calls across several real parse ticks while its `.cnt` text still updates live and a click on the SAME held button reference still correctly toggles the level filter; plus a filter created mid-load whose own row count is shown growing (not frozen) across further ticks, matching `getEntries()` exactly, with its percentage-of-parent bar kept live too.

### Multi-file load: grayed queued placeholders + merge-on-load prompt

Person-requested, this session (2026-08-20, German): dropping/picking several files at once used to only ever show the file currently being read — the next one only appeared once its own turn to load arrived, so a 5-file drop looked like files trickling in one at a time with no visibility into what was still queued.

**`createQueuedFileNode(name)`** builds a lightweight placeholder file node (`queued: true`, empty `entries`) and inserts it into `state.rootIds` immediately — but, unlike `createFileNode`, does **not** set `state.activeId` or render: a placeholder can't be selected or filtered yet, so landing on/showing one would be actively confusing. `loadFileDescriptors` now creates one placeholder per file in the batch, in order, and calls `render()` once — so every file's eventual tree position is visible right away, before any reading starts. `renderNode()` special-cases a `queued` file node (checked first thing, before building the normal interactive row) into `renderQueuedFileRow()`: a non-interactive row — no click/drag/context-menu handlers at all, since there's nothing yet to act on — styled with the new `.tree-row-queued` CSS class, deliberately mirroring folder watch's own grayed `.folder-watch-file` look (`opacity:.55`, same tertiary text color) since it's the same "known its spot, not read yet" idea, just living directly in the main tree instead of a separate box. `flattenTreeIds()` (arrow-key tree navigation) skips queued nodes for the same reason — nothing there to land on.

**`activateQueuedFileNode(node)`** is the counterpart: clears `.queued`, sets `loadFraction = 0`, sets `state.activeId`, and calls `flushLoadRender()` — the exact "insert into the active view" job `createFileNode` normally does itself. `loadOneFileIntoTree` gained an optional 5th `existingNode` parameter: when given a queued placeholder, it calls `activateQueuedFileNode` on it and reuses it instead of calling `createFileNode` fresh — so the SAME node object flips from grayed to a normal, actively-loading row (progress fill and all — see "File loading" above) exactly when `loadFileDescriptors`' loop reaches it. `loadFolderFile` (folder watch) doesn't pass `existingNode`, so its own already-established grayed-listing mechanism (a *different* placeholder shape, `folder.files` records rather than `state.nodes` entries — see "Folder watch" below) is completely unaffected.

**Merge-on-load prompt** (second half of the same request): loading 2+ files in one batch now asks upfront, via a small promise-based dialog (`confirmMergeOnLoad`/`#mergeLoadDialog`, modeled on the existing `sessionMatchDialog` promise pattern), whether to merge them into one chronologically-sorted file (`mergeFiles` — see "File merge follows the same load-progress pattern" below) once they're all loaded. The dialog opens right after the placeholders render (before any file is actually read), so answering it doesn't block the grayed rows from showing — only the actual reading waits on the answer. The "Merge" button is focused the instant the dialog opens, so **Enter answers Yes for free** via the browser's own default button-activation behavior (a focused `<button>` responds to Enter/Space natively — no custom keydown code needed); **Escape answers No** via the existing global `Escape` keydown handler (`if (mergeLoadResolver) closeMergeLoadDialog(false);`, same pattern as `sessionMatchResolver`). A single-file load never shows this dialog at all (`descs.length > 1` gate) — matches the request's own scope ("mehrere Dateien gleichzeitig"). `loadFileDescriptors` `await`s `mergeFiles` before its own promise resolves, so callers (including the test suite) see the merged node only once it's actually finished.

**Resilience fix, needed for the above to actually work**: `loadFileDescriptors`' per-file `await` used to have no error handling at all — one file's read/parse failure threw out of the whole `for` loop, silently abandoning every file queued after it (invisible before this session, since nothing was visibly queued yet; now that every later file has a grayed placeholder on screen, an unhandled throw would leave it stuck grayed forever). Each iteration is now individually `try`/`catch`ed — a failure shows a toast (`Couldn't load "name"`, same wording style as `loadFolderFile`'s own failure toast) and the loop continues to the next file; `loadOneFileIntoTree`'s own existing `catch` (deletes the half-created node) still runs first, so the failed placeholder disappears rather than staying grayed.

### File merge follows the same load-progress pattern

Person-requested, same session, immediate follow-up: *"Für ein File Merge folge der gleichen Logik. Lege den Eintrag zuerst an und Zeige den Fortschritt des Merge an diesem Eintrag."* — `mergeFiles` used to build the whole merged `entries` array synchronously in one blocking call (`flatMap` + `sort`) before the node ever existed in the tree; for several very large files that could freeze the UI for a noticeable moment with nothing on screen to explain why. It's now `async` and follows the exact same "insert the row first, stream progress onto it" shape `createFileNode`/`loadOneFileIntoTree` established for a real file load (see "File loading" above):

- The merged node (`merged: true`, empty `entries`, `loadFraction: 0`) is inserted into `state.nodes`/`state.rootIds` and made the active view immediately, via `flushLoadRender()` — a real, fully interactive tree row (same `ICON_MERGE` icon, same click/drag/delete) from the instant merging starts, just like a freshly-opened file's row.
- Entries are copied from every source file in `MERGE_CHUNK_ENTRIES`-sized chunks (20,000), pushed directly onto `node.entries` (so the row's own count grows live during this phase, same idea as `parseLogTextAsync`'s streaming append), with `node.loadFraction` advancing across `MERGE_COPY_WEIGHT` (0.6) and `scheduleLoadRender(node.id)`/a `setTimeout(0)` yield after each chunk — mirrors `READ_WEIGHT`/`PARSE_WEIGHT`'s role for a real load's two phases.
- The final chronological sort (`node.entries.sort(...)`) isn't itself chunkable, so it runs as one blocking call covering the remaining `MERGE_SORT_WEIGHT` (0.4) — but only after the copy phase's last progress tick has already had a chance to paint, thanks to that phase's own trailing yield.
- `flushLoadRender(node.id)` clears `loadFraction` and does a real, full render once sorting finishes, same as a real load's natural-completion step; a `state.nodes[node.id] !== node` guard (mirroring `loadOneFileIntoTree`'s own) handles the node being deleted mid-merge.

**Call sites**: `performBulkAction`'s "Merge N files" action and `loadFileDescriptors`' merge-on-load path (above) both call `mergeFiles` — the former fire-and-forget (its own synchronous prefix already inserted the row by the time `performBulkAction` returns and calls its own `render()`), the latter `await`ed so the batch's own promise doesn't resolve until the merge is genuinely done. `persistFileNode`/entry-reuse-by-reference semantics are unchanged from before this session.

**Test coverage**: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 69 — the merged row exists as a real, interactive, progress-carrying row (not the grayed queued-placeholder row multi-file *loads* use) the instant `mergeFiles` is called, before any chunk has finished copying; `loadFraction`/the progress fill are gone once merging completes; the final result is in correct chronological order (an earlier-timestamped source file's entries sort to the front, not just appear in call order) with the right total count. The two pre-existing direct `mergeFiles()` callers in the suite (Groups 10 and 30c, originally synchronous) were updated to `await` it.

**Test coverage**: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 68 — 68a: a 3-file batch shows all 3 as grayed placeholders (in order) with zero real rows before the merge dialog is answered, `#mergeLoadDialog` open with its Merge button focused, Escape closing it and each placeholder flipping to a real loading row in turn as the batch actually processes, and no merged file at the end. 68b: answering Yes (click) produces a merged file node once both sources finish loading, sources kept (same as the manual bulk action). 68c: a single-file load never opens the dialog. 68d: one file that fails to read (a non-Blob object forcing `FileReader.readAsText` to throw synchronously) doesn't stop a second, valid file in the same batch from loading, and produces a toast naming the failed file.

## Deep-link loading (`?url=`)

`philogg.html?url=<encoded-url>` fetches a log at boot and opens it, straight into `addFile` — the same path a session-cache restore uses (text already fully in memory, no separate read phase). `loadFromUrlParam()` (next to `loadFileDescriptors`, "File loading & progress" above) parses `location.search`, `fetch()`s the URL, derives the tree row's name from the URL's last path segment (falls back to `"remote.log"`), and calls `addFile(name, text)` + `render()`. If the url is the desktop wrapper's own `philogg://local/…` scheme, `loadUrlIntoTree` also wires up a live `node.tail` so the file gets polled/tailed like any other locally-opened file — see "Tailing (live file updates)" above.

Two consumers this is meant for: a CI/report link straight to a build's log artifact on a webserver, and — planned, not yet built — a desktop (Electron) wrapper that reads a local file via Node `fs` (no browser-gesture requirement) and serves it through a loopback URL, reusing this exact mechanism instead of building a second, separate content-injection path.

**Requires PhiLogg itself to be served over `http(s)`** — a `file://` page can't `fetch()` anything at all, cross-origin or not (Chromium's own restriction, nothing PhiLogg can work around). `loadFromUrlParam()` checks `location.protocol` first and shows a dedicated toast instead of a confusing raw fetch error in that case. A remote log server also needs to answer with CORS headers (or be same-origin) — same browser rule as any other cross-origin `fetch()`, not something worth trying to route around.

Errors (network/CORS failure, non-2xx HTTP status) show a toast (`showCopyToast`) and add no file, instead of failing silently.

**Test coverage**: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 66 — successful load (name derived from the URL, text parsed into entries), an HTTP error status, and a rejected fetch (network/CORS), each via a fake `window.fetch` (jsdom ships none). The `file:` protocol guard itself has no jsdom coverage — jsdom treats every `file:` URL as an opaque origin and throws on the app's own boot-time `localStorage` access before `loadFromUrlParam` ever runs, for any URL shape (see `tests/README.md`'s "Known gaps") — verified instead with a real Chromium session (Playwright) against a local static server: a same-origin `?url=` load rendering the file, and an unreachable URL producing the expected error toast with zero tree rows.

## Desktop wrapper (`desktop/`)

A separate, optional Electron shell around the **unmodified** `philogg.html` — adds `.log` file associations, CLI-argument/double-click file opening, and a frameless window with integrated close/minimize/maximize controls on Windows/Linux/macOS, without touching `philogg.html` itself or its own release path (`.github/workflows/release.yml`). Lives entirely in `desktop/` (own `package.json`, `main.js`, `electron-builder.yml`) — see `desktop/README.md` for build/run steps and current status.

**Frameless window**: `main.js` creates the `BrowserWindow` with no OS frame and `Menu.setApplicationMenu(null)` (no File/Edit/View/Window/Help bar). Windows/Linux get `titleBarStyle: "hidden"` + `titleBarOverlay` (Electron's Window Controls Overlay — native-drawn minimize/maximize/close top-right, no custom HTML needed); macOS gets `titleBarStyle: "hiddenInset"` (native traffic lights, left at their normal top-left position rather than moved to match Windows, since relocating a Mac app's own controls would be the actually-jarring choice). `philogg.html`'s own `#toolbar` becomes the only header and the only thing left to drag the window by — since that's not automatic on a frameless window, `main.js` injects a small stylesheet at runtime (`webContents.insertCSS()`, **not** editing `philogg.html` itself, same rule as everything else in this section) making `#toolbar` draggable and every button/input inside it `no-drag` again, plus (Windows/Linux only) right padding on `.toolbar-right` via the `titlebar-area-*` CSS environment variables Chromium exposes specifically for this. `TITLEBAR_HEIGHT` is 49 (1px shorter than `#toolbar`'s own 50px) so the overlay's native paint layer doesn't occlude `#toolbar`'s `border-bottom` under the buttons. Overlay colors follow `#btnTheme`'s live light/dark toggle: `watchTheme()` polls the renderer's own `data-theme` attribute (~1x/second, no preload/IPC bridge — same "renderer never needs Node access" reasoning as the rest of this section) and calls `win.setTitleBarOverlay()` on change.

First real Windows run (2026-08-20, see changelog) surfaced 4 bugs in an earlier version of this mechanism, all now fixed: overlay colors not following the theme toggle (fixed by the poll above — the earlier version had no theme tracking at all); the right-padding reservation going stale across maximize/restore/fullscreen, leaving `#toolbar`'s buttons hidden under or misaligned from the overlay buttons (root cause: it measured `navigator.windowControlsOverlay`'s rect via a `geometrychange` JS listener, which didn't fire reliably across those window-state transitions — replaced with the `env()` CSS approach above, a native continuously-live value with no JS/event timing involved, so the same staleness can't recur); and the toolbar's `border-bottom` invisible under the buttons (the `TITLEBAR_HEIGHT` 49px fix above). None of the four re-verified live — reasoning checked against Electron/Chromium's WCO docs, not guessed, but this dev environment still has no display server.

**Reuses the `?url=` mechanism above instead of a second loading path.** `main.js` registers a privileged custom `philogg://` scheme (`protocol.handle`, Electron ≥25) serving two things: `philogg://app/philogg.html` (the packaged `philogg.html`, read via `fs`) and `philogg://local/<id>/<basename>` (a local file's content, `id` mapped to an absolute path chosen only from OS-supplied input — argv on Windows/Linux, the `open-file` event on macOS — same trust level as a native file-open dialog; the URL's `<basename>` segment is only read by `philogg.html`'s own last-path-segment naming logic, the protocol handler itself still looks the file up by `id` alone). A file-association launch opens a `BrowserWindow` at `philogg://app/philogg.html?url=philogg://local/<id>/<basename>`; `philogg.html`'s own `loadFromUrlParam()` then just `fetch()`s it, completely unmodified, exactly like it would fetch a remote CI log URL. No `preload.js`/IPC bridge needed — the renderer stays fully sandboxed and never gets Node access; only the main process touches `fs`.

**Why a custom scheme instead of `loadFile()`/`file://`**: `loadFromUrlParam()` deliberately refuses to attempt a fetch when `location.protocol === "file:"` (that guard exists because a real `file://` page genuinely can't `fetch()` anything at all — see "Deep-link loading" above). Loading `philogg.html` itself through `philogg://app/...` instead of a real `file://` URL keeps that guard intact rather than needing a special case carved out of it.

**A file opened after the first one lands in the already-running window, not a new one.** `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()` already ensured a second file-association launch never becomes a second OS process — but the `"second-instance"` handler used to unconditionally call `createWindow()` anyway, which just moved the "new instance" symptom from "new process" to "new, separate app window" instead of actually fixing it. `openFile(filePath)` now reuses the existing `BrowserWindow` when one exists: it registers the new file and calls `window.philoggLoadUrl(url)` in the renderer via `webContents.executeJavaScript()` — `loadFromUrlParam`'s fetch-and-`addFile` body, refactored out as `loadUrlIntoTree(url)` and exposed on `window` for exactly this — then focuses/restores that window, so the file simply joins the existing tree the same way a manual "Open…" would. `createWindow()` (a genuinely new window) is now reached only for the very first file of a run, or if `openFile` is somehow called before any window exists yet. Applies to both the Windows/Linux `"second-instance"` path and macOS `"open-file"`.

**Status**: real Windows run, 2026-08-20 (see changelog) — surfaced and fixed two bugs: opened files were named after their opaque `philogg://local/` id ("1", "2", …) instead of their real filename (fixed by the `<basename>` URL segment above), and every file-association double-click after the first spawned a whole new app window (fixed by the window-reuse `openFile()` above). Not independently re-verified live from this session (no display server here) — reasoning matches the person's exact reported symptoms and Electron's own `executeJavaScript`/`requestSingleInstanceLock` docs, not guessed. **No app icons yet, and deliberately no `build/icons/` directory either** — see the "icon directory doesn't contain icons" CI failure below and `desktop/README.md` → "Adding an app icon": on Linux, electron-builder hard-fails if that directory exists but is empty, it does not silently fall back the way win/mac do.

**Release**: `.github/workflows/desktop-release.yml`, manual-only (`workflow_dispatch`), entirely separate from `release.yml` (which keeps publishing only `philogg.html`, unaffected by any of this). A `prepare` job creates one release tag (`desktop-<short-sha>`) up front so the three per-OS `build` matrix jobs (windows-latest/macos-latest/ubuntu-latest) can each just `npm install && npm run build` and upload their own installer into it, without racing each other to create the same release. `strategy.fail-fast: false` (added after the first real run, see changelog) so one platform's failure doesn't cancel the still-running others. `Upload installer(s)` retries `gh release upload` up to 5x with backoff (added after a second real run saw win/mac both fail there silently with a clean build — see changelog). Three `workflow_dispatch` boolean inputs (`build_windows` default on, `build_mac`/`build_linux` default off, matching the common case of only wanting the Windows installer) pick which platforms actually get a `build` job: `prepare` computes a JSON OS list from the checkboxes (`Compute selected OS matrix` step, plain bash + `jq`) and outputs it, `build`'s `strategy.matrix.os` is `fromJSON(needs.prepare.outputs.os_list)` rather than a fixed 3-OS list. **Not** a job-level `if:` comparing `inputs.*` against `matrix.os` — that was the first attempt and GitHub rejects the whole workflow file at parse time for it (`0` jobs, `startup_failure`): the `matrix` context isn't available in `jobs.<job_id>.if`, only in `runs-on`/`env` and inside steps (see changelog). An unchecked-everything dispatch just produces an empty matrix (0 `build` jobs), no separate guard needed. `philogg.html`'s own tester release (the "html" case) already has its own independent manual workflow (`release.yml`) — deliberately not folded into this one. Each `build` job also stamps `PHILOGG_VERSION` to the commit short-SHA before packaging (`Stamp version` step, same `sed` idiom `release.yml` uses) and passes that SHA to `electron-builder` as `PHILOGG_SHA` so the installer filename carries it too (`electron-builder.yml`'s `artifactName`) — see "Desktop wrapper" → intro and `desktop/README.md` → "Version stamp".

## Folder watch + lazy loading

FEATURE_BACKLOG.md item, built in two rounds this session (2026-08-16): the initial build, then a person-reported follow-up in the same session covering four refinements — a unified "Open…" menu, files rendered *inside* the folder listing instead of moved out below it, a browser-capability gate, and session-cache persistence. All four are folded into the description below rather than kept as a separate "what changed" appendix.

**Entry points**: pick a whole folder via the **"Open…"** button's dropdown menu (**File(s)…** / **Folder…** — replaced two separate always-visible buttons, since the browser-support asymmetry between them, see below, made two equally-prominent buttons misleading) or drag&drop (a dropped folder resolves to a `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` via `getAsFileSystemHandle()`, same as the single-file drop path already used).

**Browser support is checked once, upfront, not tried-and-caught per attempt**: `FOLDER_WATCH_SUPPORTED = !!window.showDirectoryPicker`, computed at script init. Unlike single-file loading (which has a real, fully-working fallback — the hidden `<input>`), there is **no meaningful fallback for folder watching**: without a live directory handle there's no way to list or re-scan a folder's contents at all, so a browser without the File System Access API (Firefox/Zen today) gets a clear popup instead of a silently degraded experience:
- The **"Folder…"** menu item, clicked without support, shows `showCopyToast(FOLDER_WATCH_UNSUPPORTED_MSG)` instead of opening anything.
- A dropped **folder** without support is actively *detected* — via `webkitGetAsEntry()`, which (unlike `getAsFileSystemHandle`) Firefox does implement — specifically so it can show the same popup instead of being silently misread as an empty/broken file list (which is what plain `dt.files` gives you for a dropped directory in a browser without the newer API). A dropped plain *file* is completely unaffected either way.

**Data model**: `state.folders`, a list of `{ id, name, handle, files: [{ name, handle, nodeId }], busy, failed, needsPermission }`. `handle` is always a real `FileSystemDirectoryHandle` now (no more fallback shape to branch on internally). `folder.id` doubles as the persistent IndexedDB key for the folder (see "Persistence" below) — generated via the same `newCacheKey()` file nodes already use, not a throwaway `uid()`, specifically so it's stable across a reload. A file record's `nodeId` is `null` while it's still a grayed, unopened listing entry, set once it's been opened into a real `state.nodes` file node (tagged `node.folderId` with that same folder id).

**Scanning is lazy by construction**: `scanFolderHandle()` (non-recursive — only the folder's own immediate entries, matching the backlog's "list them" scope) reads directory *entries* (`dirHandle.values()`) and filters by `isCompatibleFolderFile()` (currently `[".log"]`, `FOLDER_WATCH_EXTENSIONS` — an array so a future "pluggable parser" session has one obvious place to widen it) — it never reads a file's *content*. Nothing is parsed until a person actually opens one. `mergeScannedFiles(folder, scanned)` folds a fresh scan into `folder.files`, preserving each already-known entry's `nodeId` (and therefore its identity as a real vs. grayed row) while refreshing its `handle` — the one shared implementation behind the initial scan (`addWatchedFolder`→`rescanFolder`), `folderScanTick`'s repeat polls, and a restored folder's reconnect scan.

**UI — files render IN THE FOLDER, in folder order, not moved out below it** (person-reported follow-up; the initial build rendered opened files as ordinary root nodes under `#tree` and only kept not-yet-opened ones in the folder box, which lost the file's position in the folder once opened). `renderFolderSection()` now walks `folder.files` **in order** and, per entry, renders either a real `.tree-row` (`renderNode(rec.nodeId, 0)`, for an opened file — filters and all) or a grayed `.folder-watch-file` row (`renderInactiveFileRow`, for an unopened one) into the *same* `.folder-watch-files` list — so a file's spot in the folder stays visible whether it's open or not, and opening/closing it never reorders anything. `renderTree()` correspondingly **skips** any root id whose node has a `folderId` when building the plain top-level list — those render exclusively inside their folder's own section now. The header still shows a pulsing "ping" ring (`.folder-watch-icon.scanning`, `@keyframes folder-scan-ping`) while the folder is being actively polled, and a ✕ to stop watching (`removeWatchedFolder`).

**Double-click, or a grayed row's own right-click → "Load file"** (`openFolderFileContextMenu`, a minimal one-item menu reusing `#treeContextMenu`, distinguished by `folderCtxTarget` instead of `treeCtxId`) calls `loadFolderFile()`, which reads+parses the file (`loadOneFileIntoTree()` — the read/parse/persist/history/tail-wiring body `loadFileDescriptors` shares with this entry point) and turns it into a **completely normal root file node**, tagged `node.folderId`. It behaves exactly like any manually opened file from there — build filters on it with Ctrl+F — and, since it was opened via a real handle, starts **tailing automatically** for free.

**"No filter can be created on / copied onto an inactive file" holds by construction, not by an extra guard**: a grayed listing row is never a `state.nodes` entry and is rendered by entirely separate DOM/CSS (`.folder-watch-file`, no `draggable`, no `dragover`/`drop` listeners, no click handler that sets `state.activeId`) from a real `.tree-row` — so there is no node id Ctrl+F/paste/drag-and-drop could ever target. Nothing had to be special-cased in `openFilterPopup`/`pasteClipboard`/the tree's drag handlers.

**Closing a folder-loaded file doesn't remove it — it goes back to grayed, in the same spot.** `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo()` special-cases any file node with `node.folderId` into `closeFolderFile()`: the node and its whole filter subtree are torn down exactly like a normal close (`deleteNode` — entries released from `entryIndex`, cache invalidated, `cacheDeleteFile` fires), but the folder's own file record just has its `nodeId` cleared back to `null` instead of being removed from `folder.files` — so it reappears in its original position in the folder's file order (same handle reused, reopening needs no re-scan) instead of disappearing or getting appended at the end. A plain file with no `folderId` is completely unaffected. `removeWatchedFolder()` (the folder's own ✕) clears `folderId` off any currently-open files from that folder first, so they become plain independent files (rendered back under the plain `#tree` list, a later close on them is then a normal full removal) before dropping the folder record and its persisted store entry (`cacheDeleteFolder`).

**New-file detection**: `folderScanTick()` polls every actively-watched folder (`handle` present, not busy, not `needsPermission`, not `failed`) every `FOLDER_SCAN_MS` (3000ms — same handle-polling shape as `tailTick`/`TAIL_POLL_MS`, one level up) via `mergeScannedFiles`. **Deliberately doesn't detect removed files** — matches the backlog's literal scope ("auto-detect NEW files"); opening a since-deleted grayed entry surfaces a toast instead of crashing. A read/permission failure sets `folder.failed` (stops the scanning ping) rather than throwing, same graceful-degradation shape as a tailed file's own `tail.failed`.

**Persistence — a watched folder now survives a reload** (person-reported follow-up: "currently no reload survives folder watch"). The mechanism relies on a real, spec-guaranteed fact: a `FileSystemHandle` is designed to survive IndexedDB's structured clone algorithm — that's the actual, standard way browsers let a handle outlive a page reload at all, no different in kind from how the pre-existing tail-handle mechanism already trusted `FileSystemFileHandle` objects to keep working across `getFile()` calls. `persistFolder(folder)` stores `{ key: folder.id, name, handle }` in a new `folders` IndexedDB object store (`CACHE_DB_VERSION` 2→3); `cacheDeleteFolder` removes it when the folder is dropped. Each file's own record in the existing `files` store gained a `folderId` field (written by `persistFileNode`, read back by `restoreSessionFromCache`) so an already-open file re-links to its folder by that shared, stable id after a reload.

Boot sequence: `restoreSessionFromCache()` (files, filters, bookmarks, settings — unchanged) runs first, so any restored file's `node.folderId` is already set by the time `restoreWatchedFolders()` runs right after. That function re-registers each stored folder (`needsPermission: true` to start, seeding `folder.files` with whatever already-restored nodes point at it) and calls `folder.handle.queryPermission({ mode: "read" })` — **no user gesture required** for a *query*, only for a *request*. If the browser still considers it granted, `tryReconnectFolder` flips `needsPermission` off and rescans immediately: watching resumes with no visible interruption, and `mergeScannedFiles` **reattaches a fresh tail handle** to any already-open file that doesn't have one yet (a restored folder-owned node never does — see "Tailing (live file updates)"'s note on `tryReattachFileTail` being deliberately skipped for `node.folderId` files, since a reconnected folder handle already makes reattachment possible for free for exactly the files it owns; the reattachment here is guarded on `!node.tail` so it never touches a node that's already tailing).
