If permission was **not** silently re-granted (typically after an actual browser restart, not just a tab reload), the folder renders with a lock icon and a **Reconnect** button instead of the scanning ring — `reconnectFolder()`, the button's click handler, is the one place `requestPermission()` is called, since that method's browser-side prompt requires an actual click to invoke at all.

**Test coverage**: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`. **Group 37** (rewritten in place this session for the merged-view layout — see tests/README.md's "Extending this suite" convention): extension filtering, sorted listing in folder order, the scanning-CSS-class gate, a plain click on a grayed row leaving `state.activeId` untouched, both load entry points, the loaded node's `folderId` tag and automatic tailing, a file's **position in the folder order staying fixed** across open/close, reopening via the reused handle, `folderScanTick` picking up a new file, and `removeWatchedFolder` leaving already-open files in place (now rendered back under plain `#tree`) with `folderId` cleared. **Group 38** (this session's follow-up): 38a — the unified Open menu's toggle/close/dispatch behavior; 38b — the capability gate's popup notice from both the menu and a detected folder drop, and that plain file drops stay unaffected; 38c — a real two-window IndexedDB round trip (same shared-`IDBFactory` pattern as Group 20) proving `folderId` and the folder record itself survive a reload, PLUS the reconnect flow, tested by directly swapping in a working fixture handle before calling `reconnectFolder` — noted in the test itself as the one part a real IndexedDB round trip can't exercise in jsdom (a genuine `FileSystemHandle` keeps its methods through structured clone in a real browser; a fake one built from a JS class does not, which fake-indexeddb correctly reproduces as "the permission needs reconfirming" — the exact degraded-but-graceful path this feature is supposed to take when a real browser doesn't silently re-grant access either).

## Filter save / load (JSON files)

Right-click a filter → **"Save filter…"** writes a `.json` file via `showSaveFilePicker` (download-link fallback where that API is missing); right-click any node (filter or file) → **"Load filter…"** reads one back via `showOpenFilePicker` (hidden `<input>` fallback), inserting the result at the clicked node. `serializeFilterBranch()` / `importFilterJson()` do the actual work; format is `{ format: "philogg-filters", version, activeRef, roots: [...] }`, `FILTER_FILE_VERSION = 2`.

**What gets saved** is the *branch* a filter belongs to: its ancestor chain from just below the root file, plus its own full subtree — that's what makes the overall result reproducible on load, not just the single clicked node.

**and/or/link nodes and their `linkedId`**: since bulk-combine only ever pairs two filters from the *same* file (`describeBulkActions`), a `linkedId` target is always reachable somewhere in the same file's tree — but it can sit outside the branch above (a sibling chain). Rather than dropping such nodes, `serializeFilterBranch()` pulls in the target's own ancestor chain too (not its subtree — just enough to recompute its result via `getEntries()`), transitively, so a combined filter is never silently broken or its logic altered, even when the target chain's own ancestor is itself an `and`/`or`/`link` node. Each independent chain this produces is written as its own entry in `roots`, tagged `attach: "target"` (the primary chain — goes exactly where "Load filter…" was invoked) or `attach: "file"` (a pulled-in dependency — always recreated directly under the target file's top level, never nested inside the clicked node, even when loading deep inside an existing structure).

**Loading onto a different tree re-evaluates, it doesn't replay a stored result**: nothing beyond the filter *definitions* (type/value/inverted, `linkDirection`/`linkN`/`linkOrderEnforced`/`linkExclusive`, `contextBefore`/`After`) is written to the file — every load creates fresh nodes with new uids, and `getEntries()` is always computed lazily against whichever file the reconstructed chain now structurally sits under. So an `and`/`or`/`link` filter loaded onto a different file (or a different point in the same file) is re-applied against that destination's own data, not the source file's original computed entries. Internal `linkedId` references are serialized as per-file `ref` numbers and remapped to the new uids on import; a corrupt/hand-edited file with a dangling `linkedRef` has that one combined node dropped (with its subtree) rather than left in a broken half-wired state.

**Known gap**: loading never checks whether the destination already has an equivalent branch — every load creates a fresh, independent copy, so loading the same file twice (or onto a tree that already has a matching chain) produces visible duplicates. No dedup/merge-with-existing-branch logic exists; flagged as a possible future addition, not built.

## Reusable filter library (`FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`)

Named presets, applied to **any** file in one click — the global counterpart to the per-file filter history below (see "File filter history"), and explicitly scoped simpler per the backlog item's own wording: no content-fingerprint matching, the person just picks where a preset applies. A filter node's context menu gains **"Save to library…"** (opens `#filterLibrarySaveDialog`, a small name-prompt modal pre-filled with the node's own name); every node's context menu (file or filter, mirroring where "Load filter…" already appears) gains **"Apply from library…"** (opens `#filterLibraryDialog`, a list of saved presets each with its own Apply/delete button).

**Storage**: a fourth IndexedDB object store, `filterLibrary`, in the same `philogg-session-cache` DB (`CACHE_DB_VERSION` bumped 3→4). Keyed by a random id (`newFilterLibraryKey()`), unlike `fileHistory`'s content-fingerprint key — there's no file to fingerprint here, the whole point is that a preset is file-agnostic. A record is `{ key, name, savedAt, roots, activeRef }`, where `roots`/`activeRef` are **exactly `serializeFilterBranch()`'s own output shape** — the same branch/chain-pulling logic "Filter save / load" above already established (ancestor chain + subtree, plus any `linkedId` dependency's own chain pulled in as a separate `attach:"file"` entry) is reused unchanged, just written to IndexedDB instead of a `.json` file.

**Applying reuses `importFilterJson()` unchanged**: `applyFilterFromLibrary(targetId, record)` sets `loadFilterTargetId = targetId` (the same module-level handoff `loadFilterFromFile` uses) and calls `importFilterJson(JSON.stringify({ format: FILTER_FILE_FORMAT, version: FILTER_FILE_VERSION, activeRef: record.activeRef, roots: record.roots }))` — wrapping the stored branch back into the exact envelope a `.json` filter file already has. This means applying a library preset gets every guarantee "Filter save / load" already documents **for free**: fresh uids every time (the same preset can be applied repeatedly without conflict), `linkedRef`s remapped, and — the important one for a preset meant to travel between files — **re-evaluated against whichever file it lands on now, never a replayed stored result**. No separate apply/materialize code path was written at all.

**Deliberately no dedup/matching logic** (unlike the per-file filter history's tiered fingerprint matching) — the backlog item scoped this explicitly simpler: "storage tier and matching rules would be much simpler (no content fingerprinting — the person picks)". Deleting a saved preset (`deleteFilterLibraryEntry`) is immediate, no confirmation dialog — consistent with the rest of the app's undo-first-not-confirm-first convention (filter node deletion, bookmark deletion, etc. all work the same way; a preset is easy to re-save if deleted by mistake, and low-stakes since it's a saved definition, not live data).

Regression-tested: **Group 59** — 59a (context menu → naming dialog, pre-filled name, blank-name no-op, the saved record's shape); 59b (applying onto a DIFFERENT, previously-untouched file — fresh uid, same filter definition, re-evaluated against that file's own data rather than the source file's; delete-from-library, both the dialog's own re-render and the actual IndexedDB record); 59c (persistence across a simulated reload — a separate store from the session cache's own `meta` record, readable with zero files loaded and no boot-time restore wait unlike `restoreSessionFromCache`).

## Session cache (survives a browser reload)

The working session — loaded files, the complete filter tree per file, bookmarks, and a small set of view settings (level quick-filter, column sort, active node) — is persisted automatically and restored on the next page load. No user action required; a "Session restored" toast confirms it happened. Theme and the Full/Filtered/Stacked layout preference already lived in `localStorage` before this feature and stay there (display preferences, not session data).

**Storage**: IndexedDB (`localStorage`'s ~5 MB quota is far too small for log files), DB `philogg-session-cache` (`CACHE_DB_VERSION`, bumped as stores were added). `files` holds one record per root file — keyed by `node.cacheKey`, a random id assigned at file creation, because runtime node ids are fresh uids every load and can't key anything persistent. The stored text is **rebuilt from the parsed entries** (`rebuildFileText`: `entries.map(e => e.raw).join("\n")` — `raw` accumulates continuation lines, so multi-line stack traces round-trip; blank lines and pre-header junk the original parse already dropped are the only loss, and re-parsing the rebuilt text is a fixed point) and, since the "Folder watch" feature, also carries a `folderId` field for re-linking to a watched folder on restore. `meta` holds a single `"session"` record: file order, serialized filter trees, bookmarks, settings. `fileHistory` is the separate per-file filter-memory store (see "File filter history" below). `folders` stores watched folders' own directory handles (see "Folder watch + lazy loading" above) — a `FileSystemHandle`, unlike the plain-data records the other three stores hold, is the one thing here that's specced to survive IndexedDB's structured clone with its methods intact, which is the whole mechanism that lets a watched folder resume after a reload at all.

**Filter serialization** reuses the filter-save/load JSON's field set and `ref`/`linkedRef` indirection (`serializeFilterTreeForCache` / `materializeCachedFilters`, deliberately parallel to `serializeFilterBranch` / `importFilterJson`) — but without the branch/chain-pulling logic, because the cache always writes each file's *entire* tree, so every `linkedId` target is trivially included. Restore materializes fresh uids and remaps `linkedRef`s per file, dropping dangling ones the same way filter-file import does.

**Bookmarks** are persisted as *(file cacheKey, entry ordinal, note, bookmarkedAt)* — entry ids don't survive a reload, but entry order within a rebuilt file is identical, so the ordinal is a stable address. An entry shared between a merged file and a source file is recorded against the first root that contains it (save and restore use the same first-wins scan order). Dangling bookmark ids (entry lost to tail rotation / file delete) just don't get written. This supersedes the old "bookmarks are session-only" limitation; they're still deliberately **not** part of the filter save/load JSON (a bookmark anchors a spot in the data, not a filter definition).

**Write strategy**: `meta` is small and rewritten via a single debounced hook at the end of `render()` (`schedulePersistMeta`, 400 ms) — every state mutation already ends in a `render()`, so no per-mutation persistence calls exist anywhere. File texts are written once on load (`loadFileDescriptors`) / merge (`mergeFiles`), and debounced per-file on tail changes (`schedulePersistFileNode`, 4 s — well above `TAIL_POLL_MS`, since rebuilding a large file's text every poll tick would be wasteful). A `pagehide` listener flushes pending debounces (an IndexedDB write started there normally completes even though its promise never resolves). Deleting a file deletes its `files` record immediately (`cacheDeleteFile`); the meta record follows via the normal render-hook rewrite.

**Restore** (`restoreSessionFromCache`) runs once at boot, after the initial empty-state render, reusing the loading-progress overlay per file. It's guarded by `sessionRestoreInProgress` so its own intermediate renders don't persist half-restored state, and any error abandons the restore (whatever loaded stays usable) rather than blocking the app on a corrupt cache. Every failure path in the storage layer (`openCacheDb`/`cacheStoreOp`) resolves `null` instead of throwing — no IndexedDB (private mode, old jsdom) just means the feature silently turns off and the app behaves exactly as before it existed.

**Escape hatch**: `localStorage["philogg-cache-enabled"] = "0"` disables the whole feature. Deliberately no UI for it — clearing happens naturally by deleting files, and a toolbar toggle for a should-just-work feature felt like clutter; revisit if a real need shows up.

## File filter history (per-file memory across sessions)

Person-requested follow-up to the session cache, from a design discussion the same session (2026-08-14) before being built. Solves a different problem than the session cache: the session cache restores a whole *session* automatically on reload; this remembers filters *per file*, indefinitely, independent of any particular session, and offers them back — never auto-applies them — the next time a file with matching content is opened, even in a brand new tab/session that never touched the old one.

**Storage**: a third IndexedDB object store, `fileHistory`, in the same `philogg-session-cache` DB (`CACHE_DB_VERSION` bumped 1→2 so `onupgradeneeded` creates it for existing users). One record per qualifying file, **keyed by the file's own content fingerprint** (`getFileFullHash`) rather than by session/`cacheKey` — this is the mechanism that makes the memory outlive a session: nothing about "which session wrote this" is stored at all, only "what content this filter tree belongs to". A record holds `{ name, fullHash, entryCount, timeSpan, filters, savedAt }` — `filters` is the same `serializeFilterTreeForCache` shape the session cache and filter-save/load JSON already use. **Deliberately scoped to the filter tree only** — no bookmarks, no view settings, no active-node ref — at the person's explicit request when this was scoped; those live in the session cache already, and duplicating them here would raise exactly the staleness/re-anchoring questions the session cache's own bookmark-ordinal scheme already has to deal with, for a part of the feature nobody asked for.

**Qualifying** = a real (non-merged) file with at least one real filter child. An empty tree has nothing worth remembering; a merged file is excluded even once it has its own filters, since `mergeFiles` starts every merge with a deliberately empty tree by design (see "Tree interactions" / multi-select bulk actions) — remembering filters *for a merge* would be remembering filters for a combination that's unlikely to recur byte-for-byte across sessions the way a single source file's content can.

**No cap on the number of remembered files.** This was an open question going into the build (an earlier draft considered a "last 10–20 files" LRU cap) — resolved against a cap because the two things that usually justify one don't apply here: storage size doesn't scale with log file size (a serialized filter tree is a few KB at most, not the log text itself — unlike the session cache's `files` store), and the natural per-record key (content fingerprint) already means loading the same content twice overwrites in place rather than accumulating duplicates, so there's no unbounded-growth-from-normal-use failure mode to cap against either. A long tailing session doesn't spam the store: a new record is only written when the filter tree is actually saved (debounced from `render()`, `schedulePersistFileHistory`, same 400 ms window as `schedulePersistMeta`), not on every tail-growth tick — a file that keeps growing under a stable filter tree just keeps overwriting its own single record under its (also-changing) current fullHash, and each earlier snapshot's record is left behind, which is a *feature* here (see tier 2 below), not a leak. If unbounded growth ever becomes a real concern in practice (long-lived shared/kiosk profiles), the natural follow-up is a **time-based expiry** (drop records unmatched for N months) rather than a count cap, since relevance here tracks recency of a match, not an arbitrary "last N files" number — deliberately deferred rather than speculatively built.

**Matching** (`matchFileHistory`) reuses the exact same tiered content-fingerprint technique Session export/import's `matchExportedFile` already established, just run in the opposite direction: one just-loaded candidate file checked against every stored record (since the history store is unbounded, unlike export/import's "handful of currently loaded files"), instead of one export record checked against a handful of loaded candidates. **Tier 1**: exact whole-file fingerprint match. **Tier 2**: the loaded file's own time span fully *contains* a stored (smaller) snapshot's span — the file has grown (typically via tailing) since that snapshot was saved — confirmed by re-slicing the loaded file down to that historical window (`windowSlice`, reusing `lowerBoundByTs`/`upperBoundByTs`) and checking both the entry count and the window's own fingerprint match the stored record exactly. Ties within tier 2 (a file grown past several old snapshots) resolve to the most recently saved one. **Tier 3 deliberately gets no preview at all**, not a lighter-weight one — an ambiguous/unverifiable match here would need the same manual-disambiguation machinery Session export/import's tier 3 dialog uses, which is disproportionate for a convenience feature; a miss just costs a missed convenience, never a wrong restore.

**Where the lookup happens**: `loadFileDescriptors` (the single funnel behind Open Files, drag-drop, and File System Access picker loads), right after the file finishes parsing and its `cacheKey` write fires — a freshly loaded file always starts with zero filter children at that point, so the lookup can only ever attach a *candidate* match, never something that competes with real filters already on screen. The result is stored as `node.filterHistoryMatch = { filters, tier }` on the file node itself. **Not run** for `mergeFiles` (excluded by design, see "Qualifying" above) or `restoreSessionFromCache` (that flow already brings its own filters back via the session cache — running this lookup there too would be redundant and could theoretically flash a ghost preview mid-restore for no benefit).

**UI**: a "ghost preview" (`renderFilterHistoryPreview`, called from `renderNode`) renders directly under a file's tree row, in the same visual slot real filter children would occupy, whenever `node.children.length === 0 && node.filterHistoryMatch` — condition-based, not event-based: no separate "was this offered/dismissed already" flag exists anywhere. One grayed, non-interactive row per node in the stored tree (`renderGhostRow` — icon, name, type tag, mirroring `renderNode`'s own visual language closely enough to read as "this is what your filters looked like", but with no click/drag/context-menu handlers at all), a short note line (entry count, "File has grown since —" prefix on a tier-2 match), and a **Restore filters** button that calls the existing `materializeCachedFilters(fileNode, match.filters)` and re-renders. **Disappears automatically** the instant the file gets *any* real filter child — whether via Restore or the person just creating their own filter without ever clicking it — because that's the entire visibility condition; this is exactly the "goes away once you've started your own filtering, restored or not" behavior requested when this was scoped.

**(Resolved) Emptying the tree back out now overwrites the old restore state instead of resurrecting it.** Previously `node.filterHistoryMatch` was never deleted, only hidden by the `children.length` condition above — so deleting every filter the person had just added made the tree empty again, the condition flipped back to "showable", and the ghost preview resurfaced showing the *original, pre-session* filters, not anything reflecting what the person had just done. Fixed this session (2026-08-14, person-reported via screenshot): `buildFileHistoryRecords()` (see "Storage" above) now tracks a per-file, session-only `node.filterHistoryTouched` flag, set the first time `children.length` goes from 0 to >0 (own filter or a Restore) — at that same moment `filterHistoryMatch` is cleared to `null`, so it can never resurface later purely because the tree emptied back out. If the tree is later emptied again (`children.length` back to 0) while `filterHistoryTouched` is set, the persist cycle now **deletes** that file's stored record instead of leaving it untouched — both directions (gaining a filter, losing the last one) overwrite whatever restore memory came before, and a file with no filters left is indistinguishable from one that never had any, including on the next reload. A freshly loaded file whose ghost preview hasn't been acted on yet (`filterHistoryTouched` still unset) is unaffected — nothing is decided about it until the person actually does something.

**Test coverage**: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 30 (jsdom + `fake-indexeddb`) — 30a: tier-1 exact-match persistence and restore across two windows sharing one `IDBFactory` (same reload-simulation pattern Group 20 established), including the ghost preview's row count/content and the Restore button materializing the tree; 30b: tier-2 matching against a grown file, plus the negative case (a genuinely unrelated file's span doesn't overlap at all, so it's not even a near-miss); 30c: the exclusion rules (merged files, empty filter trees), the ghost preview's auto-hide once the file gets any real filter child, and (extended this session) the in-memory match actually clearing once the tree is populated; 30d: the same tier-1 round trip but through the REAL `loadFileDescriptors` entry point — an actual `File` read via `FileReader` (jsdom implements both natively, no stubbing needed), proving the `persistFileNode` → `matchFileHistory` hook wired inside `loadFileDescriptors` itself works end-to-end, not just the lower-level pieces 30a-c exercise directly; **30e (new, this session)**: the full reported bug scenario end-to-end — session 1 saves a tree, session 2 loads the same content, creates its own filter (old record correctly overwritten, already worked), deletes that filter again (ghost stays hidden, in-memory match stays cleared, stored record is removed — the actual bug), then a simulated session 3 reload of the same content finds no match at all.



- **Column sort** (`Time`/`Level`/`Thread`/`Location` headers, `.th-sortable`): clicking toggles ascending/descending (`state.sortColumn`, `state.sortDir`, arrow indicator via `updateSortIndicators()`). Applied in `renderTable()` to a **copy** of `getVisibleEntries()` (`entryComparator`) — never sorts the underlying node/file arrays in place. This matters: those arrays must stay chronological for the link filter's binary search (`lowerBoundByTs`) and the OR-filter's chronological merge to keep working; sorting only the display copy keeps the sort a pure presentation feature with zero risk to the rest of the pipeline. `Method`/`Message` aren't sortable columns (no obviously useful ordering for free text).
- **Status strip** (`#statusStrip`, `renderStatusStrip()`): a single always-visible line above the table — "N of M shown" (M = the active node's root file's total entry count via `getRootFileId`, N = `currentViewEntries.length` after node filter + level quick-filter) plus "filtered to X, Y" when level toggles are active. Only shown in the log view (hidden for the extraction table and link pair view, which have their own info lines).
- **Message hover popover — removed.** `#msgPopover` used to replace the native `title` tooltip on `.col-msg` cells with a delayed, selectable preview. It was removed for two reasons: (1) it duplicated what the detail panel (bottom of the log view, always shows the full selected message) already covers, so it added interaction surface without adding capability; (2) it caused a real, hard-to-spot bug — the popover for the *previously* hovered row lingers (by design, to avoid flicker while scanning) for up to 150ms after the pointer moves to an adjacent row, and since it's `position:fixed` with `pointer-events:auto` sitting right below/above the row it describes, it could swallow a click meant for the row now under the cursor. Caught via jsdom by checking the popover's `hidden`/`show` classes across a simulated row-to-row hover transition — see Testing approach below.
- The severity color bar (`.col-bar`, first column of `--row-grid`) was widened from 3px to 5px, since it remains the primary at-a-glance signal for scanning a long list.

## Session export / import (sharing an analysis with a colleague)

The toolbar's **"Session…"** button → **"Export session…"** / **"Import session…"** (see "Header / toolbar layout" → `#btnSession`/`#sessionMenu`). Both actions used to live in the tree's per-node context menu instead (next to Save/Load filter, plus a dedicated empty-background context menu just to keep "Import session…" reachable with zero files loaded) — moved to the toolbar this session (person-requested) since neither action is actually about whichever file/filter the context menu was opened on; the toolbar button works identically regardless of how many files are loaded, so the empty-background special case is gone too, not just relocated. `openSessionExportDialog()`/`importSessionFromFile()` were already self-contained (the export dialog guards on `state.rootIds.length` itself, the import flow needs no node reference at all), so the move was pure UI wiring with no logic change. Format is `{ format: "philogg-session-export", version, exportedAt, files: [...], settings }`, `SESSION_FILE_VERSION = 1`.

**What gets exported**: a user-picked subset of the currently loaded root files (`#sessionExportDialog`, one row per file with an include checkbox), each file's *complete* filter tree (reusing `serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters` from the session cache — no branch/chain-pulling needed since the whole tree goes anyway), that file's bookmarks (first-wins scan across files, same convention as `buildCacheMeta`), and session-wide settings (level filter, sort, active node — recorded as `{exportId, ref}` since export ids are only stable within one export document). A per-file **"embed log text"** checkbox (deliberately per-file, not global — some logs in a session may be sensitive while others aren't) includes `rebuildFileText(node)` in that file's record, making the export self-contained for that file.

**The core problem this solves**: the person importing almost certainly has their *own* copy of the same log, not the exporter's literal file — same name, different name, or (for a tailed/live log) a newer snapshot that kept growing after the export was made. `matchExportedFile()` resolves each exported file against the importer's currently loaded files in three tiers:

1. **Tier 1 — full-content match**: the export carries a whole-file fingerprint (`fullHash`, via `fingerprintText`); any loaded file whose own full fingerprint (`getFileFullHash`, cached on `node._fullHash`) matches is a confident hit. Applied silently.
2. **Tier 2 — overlapping time window**: covers the motivating scenario (receiving copy of a tailed log kept growing past the exported snapshot). The exported file's timestamp span must be **fully contained** in the loaded file's span — a file that only partially overlaps (e.g. a rotated/truncated head) can't be verified this way and falls through to tier 3. Within the window: entry-count pre-filter first (cheap, and a strong signal — same density in the same window), then a fingerprint of the concatenated raw text of just that window, compared against the export's `fullHash`. An appended entry whose timestamp lands exactly on the window's end boundary inflates the count and correctly demotes the match to tier 3 rather than silently misapplying. Surfaced via a passive toast (`"X matched via overlapping time range"`) — applied automatically, but the person is told why, since it's a weaker guarantee than tier 1.
3. **Tier 3 — manual reassignment** (`#sessionMatchDialog`, one dialog per unresolved file, sequential): near-misses from tiers 1–2 (right file, wrong count/hash, or partial overlap) are shown first and badged "possible match"; other loaded files follow; then **"Load a file from disk…"** (opens the normal file picker, re-runs tiers 1–2 against the newly loaded file, loops back to the dialog if that still doesn't resolve it); **"Load the embedded log"** (only shown when the export has `text` for that file — materializes it via `addFile`, treated as tier-1-trustworthy since re-parsing the exporter's own rebuilt text is a fixed point); and **"Skip this file"**. Cancel on the dialog skips this file and every remaining unresolved one (already-applied files are kept). No backdrop-click dismissal on this dialog (unlike the export dialog) — a stray click mid-import shouldn't silently count as a decision.

**Bookmarks**: tier 1/2 targets are provably identical inside the exported window, so a bookmark's stored **ordinal** is trusted outright (`applySessionEntryByOrdinal`, same scheme as the session cache). A tier-3 manually-picked file has no such guarantee, so bookmarks there are re-anchored by **timestamp** (binary search via `lowerBoundByTs`) and confirmed against the hit ± 2 neighbours by **raw-line fingerprint** (`applySessionEntryByContent`) — enough slack to absorb small insertions/deletions elsewhere in the file without losing every bookmark to one nearby edit. A bookmark that can't be confirmed is dropped and shows up in the final summary toast as unplaced (`"N of M bookmarks placed"`), never silently misplaced onto the wrong line.

**Fingerprinting is deliberately not `crypto.subtle`/SHA-256**: it's a matching aid, not a security boundary (the worst-case cost of a collision is a wrongly-*suggested* match that the person can still reject in the tier-3 dialog), Web Crypto's async API is awkward for hashing several loaded files as a synchronous pre-filter, and its availability under a `file://` origin (this app's primary deployment mode) isn't worth depending on. `fingerprintText()` runs two 32-bit FNV-1a passes with different offset bases and concatenates them into 16 hex chars — cheap, synchronous, good enough for a matching aid. Do not swap in Web Crypto in a future session without revisiting this reasoning.

**Settings/active-node application is all-or-nothing per import, not per file**: if every exported file ends up skipped, session-wide settings (level filter, sort, active node) are left untouched rather than clobbering the current view for zero gained files.

**Test coverage**: `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 21 (jsdom) — toolbar `#btnSession`/`#sessionMenu` open/close/dispatch and the node context menu no longer carrying either item (updated in place this session for the toolbar move), export dialog with per-file include/embed checkboxes (`downloadJsonFallback` stubbed to capture the JSON, since jsdom has no `showSaveFilePicker`), tier-1 round-trip against a renamed byte-identical file (filters, `linkedId` remap, highlight colour, bookmarks with notes, settings, active node), tier-2 auto-match against a grown file (asserts `tier === 2` explicitly, to catch a future change that accidentally widens tier 1's matching instead of exercising the window path), tier-2 rejection on a density mismatch falling through to the manual dialog, tier-3 manual pick with one of two bookmarks deliberately unresolvable (proves the fallback doesn't silently over- or under-match), the version guard, and embedded-log materialization into an empty session. Group 48a covers the empty-background context menu's removal and the toolbar button working with zero files loaded.

## Extraction workflow: live match feedback & token chips

- **Live match count** (`#filterLiveMatch`, `evaluateLiveMatch()`): debounced (150ms) re-evaluation of whatever's currently typed in `#filterInput`, run against `getVisibleEntries()` — the same rows the new filter would actually apply to. For an extraction pattern (`isExtractPattern`), shows "Matches: N of M rows · K columns"; for plain text, "Matches: N of M rows"; an invalid pattern shows "Invalid pattern" in the `--level-error` color. Zero matches gets a `--warn`-colored nudge rather than an error, since "the pattern is fine but nothing matches yet" is a normal mid-typing state, not a failure.
- **Live pattern preview** (`#filterPatternPreview`, `updateFilterPatternPreview()`, same debounce as the live match count above): for an extraction pattern, shows the pattern evaluated against a real sample message with matched substrings highlighted in place — see "Extraction: live pattern preview + ignored columns" below for the full writeup, including how this doubles as the ignore-toggle UI while authoring.
- **Token chips** (`#filterTokenChips`, one button per placeholder type, now grouped directly under `#filterInput` in the popup's input section — see "Filter popup" above): clicking inserts the literal token (`[value:float]`, `[value:int]`, `[value:time]`, `[value:word]`, `[value:hex]`, or `[*]`) at the current cursor position in `#filterInput` (`insertTokenAtCursor`), replacing any active text selection, same as normal typing would. (The old `#filterHint` plain-text fallback row listing the same placeholders was removed — the chips already insert them directly, making the text redundant.)
- **Extraction table column sort** (`.extract-sort-btn`, one small button inside each `<th>`): sorts `extractRowsData` itself — numeric-capable types (`float`/`int`/`hex`/`time`) numerically via the same `parseValueForPlot` the Plot tab uses, `word` lexically via `localeCompare`, unparseable values always last regardless of direction. The sort button is a separate nested control with its own `mousedown`/`click` handlers (`stopPropagation`) specifically so it doesn't trigger the header's existing mousedown-driven column cell-selection — the two interactions share the same `<th>` but are otherwise independent. **Because the sort reorders `extractRowsData` in place** (rather than keeping a separate display-order array), it also reorders the connected **Plot tab's line-chart point order** for that extraction, since line/bar plotting reads points in row order by design (see Plotting section above) — this was accepted as a reasonable trade-off over the bookkeeping risk of two parallel orderings that could drift out of sync. Sorting also clears any active cell selection in the table (`state.tableSelection = null`), since a positional "row,col" selection would otherwise silently point at different rows post-sort. Sort state resets whenever the active extraction node changes.

## Theming

Rewritten this session (2026-08-22) from a two-way Light/Dark toggle into a full theme picker: six built-in themes (Dark, Light, and four Catppuccin flavors — https://catppuccin.com/palette/) plus unlimited user-imported custom themes, all selected from one `<select id="settingsThemeSelect">` dropdown in Settings → Appearance (previously an `.assert-mode-btn` Light/Dark button pair, see `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 3/70h/70h2's updated text and the new Group 85).

**The var surface** (`THEME_COLOR_KEYS` in the Theme script section) is unchanged from before: `--bg-app`, `--bg-panel`, `--bg-elevated(-2)`, `--border(-soft)`, `--text-primary/secondary/tertiary`, `--accent(-strong/-soft)`, `--level-error/warn/info/debug/trace` (+ `-soft` alpha variants), `--warn`. `--level-error/warn/info/debug-on` + `--accent-on` (`THEME_ON_KEYS`) are the text color for the four *active* level-quick-filter buttons and for anything sitting on a solid `var(--accent)` surface (brand mark, Save button, active chips/badges) — split out of the level/accent colors themselves into their own vars (defaulting to near-black in `:root`) precisely so a theme can flip them to white without a dedicated CSS selector per theme. `--border-hover` is *derived*, not stored — `color-mix(in srgb, var(--border) 70%, var(--text-tertiary) 30%)` in `:root`, so every theme (including a custom one applying vars inline) gets a correct hover-state border automatically from its own `--border`/`--text-tertiary` with no extra required JSON key.

**Stylesheet audit, same session, follow-up ("Prüfe... ob Du das konsequent umgesetzt hast").** A pass over every rule outside the `:root[data-theme=...]` blocks found ~18 places still hardcoding the DARK theme's own hex/rgba values instead of the var every sibling rule already used — invisible on Dark (where the hardcoded value and the themed one happened to match) but wrong on every other theme: the scrollbar thumb, `#btnOpen`/`#btnSession`/`.toolbar-icon-btn` hover borders, `.brand-mark`'s gradient/text, `.toolbar-badge`/`.column-chip.active`/`.btn-mini` text, and — the highest-impact group — `.crumb.current`/`.level-btn.lvl-*`/`.level-badge`/`.log-row.lvl-*:hover`/`.token-chip`'s alpha-tinted borders and backgrounds, all still using the dark theme's specific level-color rgba() triplets regardless of the active theme. Fixed via `--accent-on`/`--border-hover` above for the flat-color cases, and `color-mix(in srgb, var(--x) N%, transparent)` for the alpha-tinted ones (CSS can't apply an arbitrary alpha to a `var()` color any other way; every browser this app targets — Chromium, for the File System Access API it already requires — supports `color-mix()`). The timeline minimap was audited too and needed no changes: `.minimap-lvl-*`/`.minimap-bg-bar`/`.minimap-full-range`/etc. were already `fill:var(--level-error)` etc. from when the minimap was first built. **Deliberately left alone**: modal/dialog backdrop scrims (`rgba(16,19,26,.7-.88)`) and `box-shadow`s (`rgba(0,0,0,...)`) — a dark dimming layer and a black shadow are a normal convention regardless of the app's own light/dark theme, not a theming bug; and `COLUMN_PALETTE`'s 6 fixed extraction-column/plot colors (`.pattern-chip-num`'s `color:#0b1016`) — see "Visual language" below, deliberately theme-independent by design, unrelated to `--accent-on` despite the visual similarity. Group 82a is a static regression guard (`.textContent` of `<style>`, not computed style — jsdom can't resolve `color-mix()`) against the specific hardcoded literals this audit found creeping back in.

**Two application mechanisms, one var surface.** The six built-ins are plain CSS: one `:root[data-theme="<id>"]{...}` override block per theme (Light's block also sets the four `-on` vars to white, same as before; Catppuccin Latte does too — both are light-background themes whose level colors are too dark for the near-black default). Custom/imported themes have no CSS block — `applyTheme(themeId)` sets `data-theme` on `<html>` either way, but for a custom theme it also walks `THEME_COLOR_KEYS`/`THEME_ON_KEYS` and sets each one as an inline `style.setProperty("--x", ...)` on `document.documentElement`, after first clearing every one of those properties (so switching *back* to a built-in never leaves a stale inline override shadowing its CSS block — verified in Group 81b). `setTheme()` calls `applyTheme()` then persists the id to `localStorage` (`philogg-theme`, same key/tier as before); `initTheme()` on load resolves a stored id through `resolveThemeId()` (falls back to `"dark"` if it names a since-deleted custom theme — Group 81c) or, with nothing stored yet, falls back to `prefers-color-scheme` same as before.

**Custom themes** are plain JSON, validated and stored as `{id, name, colors: {...THEME_COLOR_KEYS}, activeTextLight}` in a `customThemes` array persisted to `localStorage` (`philogg-custom-themes`) — same tier as the theme choice itself, not IndexedDB (unlike the Log Formats/Filter Library lists, which expect larger/more numerous entries; a handful of ~25-key color maps doesn't need that). Settings → Appearance has a "Custom themes" card (reuses the `filter-library-row` list styling from Log Formats) with:
- **Download template…** (`buildThemeTemplateJson`) — seeds the JSON from the *currently active* theme's own `getComputedStyle` values (whichever theme that is, built-in or custom), so the file that comes out is always already valid and ready to tweak, not a fixed baseline disconnected from what's on screen. Ships via the same `showSaveFilePicker`/`downloadJsonFallback` pattern as `saveFilterToFile`.
- **+ Import theme…** (`importThemeJson`, via `#themeFileInput` / `showOpenFilePicker`, same pattern as `loadFilterFromFile`) — rejects malformed JSON, a missing/wrong `format` tag (`"philogg-theme"`), a newer `version`, or a `colors` object missing any required key (reports which ones), each via `showCopyToast`. A valid import gets a generated id (`"custom:" + timestamp + random`), is appended to `customThemes`, and is switched to immediately.
- Each row in the list: a small 4-stripe swatch (bg/accent/error/info) built from the theme's own colors, click-to-activate, and a delete button (falls back to Dark if the deleted theme was active).

`activeTextLight` is the one JSON field a theme author needs for text contrast — a boolean, not the five raw `-on` keys — since which text color to use is an implementation detail of these specific button/badge states, not something a template-filling user should have to reason about; `true` sets all five `THEME_ON_KEYS` (four level-on + `accent-on`) to white, `false`/omitted leaves the dark near-black defaults (which is also what every Catppuccin dark flavor, and both Dark/Light for `accent-on` specifically, relies on implicitly by not overriding it — every built-in `--accent` stays bright/mid-bright by design, verified across all six).

**Highlight-color picker "Theme" mode** (same session, follow-up request): the per-filter-node highlight-color picker (`#colorPickerPopup`, opened from a tree row's swatch — see "Tree interactions" — entirely separate from app theming before this session) now has a `highlightPalette` concept of its own. Each Catppuccin flavor's `BUILTIN_THEMES` entry carries its 14 named Catppuccin "accent colors" (rosewater/flamingo/pink/mauve/red/maroon/peach/yellow/green/teal/sky/sapphire/blue/lavender — https://catppuccin.com/palette/); a custom theme's JSON may set its own optional `highlightPalette` array the same way. A new "Free"/"Theme" toggle in the popup (`setColorPickerMode`, persisted to `localStorage["philogg-cp-mode"]`, same tier as the theme choice) switches between the original behavior (hue wheel + the generic, theme-independent `HIGHLIGHT_PRESETS`) and Theme mode, which hides the wheel and shows ONLY `getActiveHighlightPalette()` — the active theme's `highlightPalette` if it has one, else `HIGHLIGHT_PRESETS` as a graceful fallback (Dark/Light, or a custom theme that didn't define one). `openColorPicker()` re-renders the preset row on every open, so switching the app theme while the popup is closed is picked up the next time it's opened without needing to also re-toggle the mode. Group 82b/82c.

**Accent color** (same-day clarification: "Highlightfarbe" turned out to mean the app's own accent/selection color — the one already driving `.toolbar-icon-btn.active`, `.crumb.current`, `.btn-mini`, and the minimap's `.minimap-full-range`/`.minimap-rendered-range` time-span highlight, all confirmed already `var(--accent...)`-driven by the stylesheet audit above — not the per-filter-node picker covered just above, which stays as a separate, unrelated feature). Settings → Appearance gained an "Accent color" row, visible only for a theme with its own `highlightPalette` (the four Catppuccin flavors; a custom theme too, if its JSON sets one) — a row of swatches from `themeOwnPalette(themeId)` (same palettes as the highlight picker's Theme mode, but WITHOUT the `HIGHLIGHT_PRESETS` fallback: Dark/Light and a plain custom theme just don't get this row, rather than offering theme-unrelated swatches under a "this theme's colors" label) plus a "↺" reset swatch. `setAccentChoice(themeId, hex)` stores the pick in `accentChoices` (`{themeId: hex}`, `localStorage["philogg-accent-choice"]` — per-theme, so a Mocha pick doesn't leak into Latte) and re-applies the theme; `applyAccentChoice()` (called from inside `applyTheme()`, after a theme's own CSS/custom-colors are in place) overrides `--accent`/`--accent-strong`/`--accent-soft`/`--accent-on` together via JS — `mixHex()`/`hexToRgba()`/`relativeLuminance()` — mixing the picked color 25% toward white (dark flavors) or black (Latte, detected from the active theme's own `--bg-app` luminance, not a hardcoded per-theme flag) for `-strong`, and flipping `-on` to white below a luminance threshold so a dark/saturated pick (e.g. Latte's Red) doesn't produce unreadable text. **Deliberately NOT** a `color-mix(var(--accent))` CSS formula (unlike `--border-hover` above) — that would apply even with nothing picked, silently replacing each flavor's hand-curated two-tone default (e.g. Mocha's Teal+Sky, not a mechanical tint of one color) the moment `color-mix()` support landed; JS only touches these vars when an actual override is stored. Group 87.

**Catppuccin style-guide compliance audit** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested against https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/blob/d09787dd98ca6fba08af5ef2ae94a7e09f17daca/docs/style-guide.md's "General Usage" table — the only section of that guide aimed at a general app rather than a terminal/code-editor; fetched fresh for this session, not recalled from training). Three confirmed gaps, all fixed:
- **"On Accent" text = Base**, not black/white. All four flavors now point `--accent-on`/`--level-*-on` at `var(--bg-app)` (Base, per the background remap directly below) instead of the generic near-black/`#fff` every other theme uses. `applyAccentChoice()` (Accent color, above) still computes its OWN black-or-white via `relativeLuminance()` when a person re-picks `--accent` to something outside the palette's originally-paired set — Base is guaranteed readable against Catppuccin's own small set of default accent/level colors (that's the guide's point), but not necessarily against an arbitrary one of the 14 named colors used as a full accent surface, which the re-pick feature allows; kept as a deliberate two-tier design (spec-correct default, safety-net override) rather than only one or the other.
- **"Selection Background" = Overlay 2 @ 20-30% opacity**, not the accent tint. New `--selection-bg` var (`:root` default = `var(--accent-soft)`, i.e. unchanged for Dark/Light/custom themes, which the guide doesn't govern) overridden per Catppuccin flavor to `color-mix(in srgb, var(--level-debug) 25%, transparent)` — reusing `--level-debug`, already mapped to Overlay 2 per flavor, rather than a new hardcoded hex. `::selection` now reads `var(--selection-bg)`.
- **Background Pane / Secondary Panes hierarchy, remapped on person direction** (initially flagged as a deliberate deviation and left alone — kept to preserve visual consistency with Dark/Light's own pre-existing hierarchy — then explicitly asked to be remapped to match the guide instead; see Group 88). The guide's roles: `--bg-app` (the page/table background, i.e. the actual "Background Pane") = **Base**; `--bg-panel` (toolbar/sidebar/dialogs, "Secondary Panes") = **Mantle**; `--bg-elevated`/`--bg-elevated-2`/`--border-soft`/`--border` ("Surface Elements" + one "Overlay" for the most prominent stroke) = **Surface 0/1/2, Overlay 0** in ascending prominence. The SAME role mapping applies to all four flavors, including Latte — Base is the brightest token there (unlike the three dark flavors, where it's a mid tone), so `--bg-app` still lands on "the main pane," it just means Latte's main content area now reads as the single brightest surface in the app, with its Surface-tier dialogs/popups reading progressively grayer/darker against it — the mirror image of how the three dark flavors' Surface tier reads progressively brighter, both the expected shape for their own flavor rather than a special case. This flips which surface reads as "brighter" for Catppuccin flavors specifically (sidebar/toolbar now read darker than the log table, unlike Dark/Light's own look) — a real, visible, intentional difference from Dark/Light now, not a bug.

**Known, deliberate deviations** (same audit, not changed — flagged for a person decision rather than silently "fixed"):
- **Warnings = Peach, not Yellow.** The guide's "General Usage" table says Yellow; its own "Code Editors → General Editor Elements" table separately lists "Warnings: Yellow, Peach" (both allowed there). Peach was chosen to match PhiLogg's own pre-Catppuccin warn semantic (`--warn`/`--level-warn`, amber-ish, not yellow) — see the Catppuccin CSS blocks' own comment. Yellow remains unused as a level color across every Catppuccin flavor.
- **Information/`--level-info` = Blue, not Teal** (the "General Editor Elements" table's "Information: Teal" — no equivalent line exists in "General Usage"). Teal is already `--accent`; reusing it for `--level-info` would make INFO-level rows visually collide with active/selected UI state. Blue was already this app's pre-Catppuccin info color too.
- "Tags, Pills" (guide: Blue) — PhiLogg's chips (breadcrumb, column, token) use `--accent` (Teal for the dark flavors), not Blue. Lower-confidence mapping (PhiLogg's chips aren't quite the guide's "tag/pill" use case), not changed.
- `Cursor` (guide: Rosewater) has no PhiLogg equivalent — `<input>` fields use the browser's native caret, not a CSS-stylable color the app currently sets (`caret-color` is unused).

Level colors (`--level-error/warn/info/debug/trace` + `-soft`) were re-themed for Light back in 2026-08-17 (from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` — previously reused the dark-mode hex values verbatim, which read poorly against the light panel background); values unchanged this session. Not regression-tested for actual rendered color (jsdom can't assert CSS color values or contrast — see "Testing approach" / `tests/README.md`'s "Known gaps"), but Group 81a does assert the resolved `getComputedStyle(...).getPropertyValue("--x")` string for a couple of load-bearing vars per built-in theme, which at least catches a wrong/typo'd hex or a misrouted `[data-theme]` block.

## Visual language (for consistency in future work)

Dark theme by default, five other built-in themes (Light + four Catppuccin flavors) and unlimited custom ones selectable via the Settings → Appearance dropdown (see Theming above) — all surface/text/accent colors are CSS custom properties at `:root`, precisely so every theme stays in sync from one set of rules. Key ones: `--bg-app` / `--bg-panel` / `--bg-elevated(-2)` for surface layering, `--accent` (teal in dark mode, `#4fc7c3`) for selection/active states, `--level-error/warn/info/debug/trace` (+ `-soft` low-alpha variants) for log-level coloring, each with its OWN value per theme (fully re-themed, not just carried over from Dark — see Theming above) — applied to the row background now, not just the badge/accent-bar (an earlier iteration only tinted the badge; rows are fully tinted per level today, with hover states that intensify rather than replace the tint, via `color-mix(in srgb, var(--level-x) N%, transparent)` since this session's audit — see Theming). `--font-mono` for anything log-data-shaped, `--font-ui` for chrome. Icons are small hand-drawn inline SVGs (`ICON_FILE`, `ICON_FILTER`, `ICON_CLOCK`, `ICON_TABLE`, `ICON_MERGE`, `ICON_COMBINE`, `ICON_LINK`, `ICON_CONTEXT`, `ICON_CLOSE`, `ICON_COPY`, `ICON_CUT`, `ICON_PASTE`, `ICON_SAVE`, `ICON_LOAD`, `ICON_SESSION_EXPORT`, `ICON_SESSION_IMPORT`, `ICON_HELP`, `ICON_PIN`) — follow that pattern (no icon font/library) if a new node type needs one. `COLUMN_PALETTE` (6 colors, teal/violet/amber/blue/pink/green) is reused for both extraction-column chips and plot series, and is the one deliberate exception to "every color is a theme var" — checked and left alone in this session's stylesheet audit (see Theming), since its whole point is a fixed set of colors that reads consistently regardless of theme. **Any new color needs a CSS variable, not a hardcoded hex** — that's what keeps every theme override a single block instead of a parallel hunt through the stylesheet (this session's audit exists precisely because that rule wasn't followed consistently before — see Theming's "Stylesheet audit" for what it found).

**Shared control box model** (this session, 2026-08-22, "Main window visual consistency fix" — extends the Settings dialog's own `.btn-mini`/`.btn-mini-outline`/`.btn-mini-secondary`/`.btn-icon` system to the toolbar/view-bar row, which had drifted into per-control hand-tuned CSS over several sessions — see the now-removed "height-parity bugfix" comment history in git blame for what that drift cost). One height (28px), one centering mechanism (`display:flex; align-items:center; justify-content:center`, or `inline-flex` for an inline-flowing chip like `.crumb`), shared by role instead of copy-pasted line-height/padding arithmetic per control: **icon-button shape** (28x28, `border-radius:7px`) — `.toolbar-icon-btn`, reused as-is (no id override needed) by `#btnPinBookmarks`/`#btnMultilineMsg`/`#btnColumns`, and by every header icon button (`#btnUndo`/`#btnRedo`/`#btnBookmarks`/`#btnShortcuts`); **pill shape** (`border-radius:20px`, height:28px) — `.level-btn`/`.crumb`; `.view-tabs`/`.view-tab` get the same 28px height via `height:100%` on the tab filling its 28px-tall container, rather than a line-height chosen to arithmetically sum to 28px. `#btnOpen`/`#btnSession` (previously two byte-for-byte-identical rule blocks) were merged into one and given the same 28px height. **When adding a new control to the toolbar/view-bar row, reuse one of these two shapes (or add a new one to this same shared system) instead of hand-tuning padding/line-height to visually match its neighbors** — that arithmetic is exactly what broke three separate times before this session. `.tree-action-btn`/`.folder-watch-reconnect`/`.tree-ghost-restore-btn` were deliberately left as their own rules — they don't sit in a row requiring cross-control height alignment, so folding them into this system would be scope creep without a concrete visual bug to fix.

The three panel resizers (`#sidebarResizer`/`#fhSplitResizer`/`#detailResizer`, see "Collapsible sidebar / detail panel" and the fhSplit/detail-panel sections) now show a permanent grip bar (a short rounded 3px-thick bar, `var(--border)` at rest, brightening to `var(--accent)` on hover/drag) instead of being fully transparent until dragged — a bare transparent 7px hit area between two `--bg-panel` surfaces read as a stray dark seam (showing the darker `--bg-app` through), not an intentional divider. Also fixed the same session: `#timelineMinimap`'s `border-bottom` used `--border-soft` while `#sidebar`'s `border-right` (the adjoining seam) used `--border` — now both use `--border`. **Every border meeting at a sidebar/content/resizer seam should use `--border`, not `--border-soft`** (border-soft is for internal, lower-emphasis dividers within a single panel, e.g. `#sidebarHeader`'s own bottom border against the tree below it).

Selection styling convention established through iteration: **rings/outlines for "selected", not background fills** — a filled background for selection was found to be visually indistinguishable from the neutral hover fill, causing a "why are two rows highlighted" confusion. Log-row selection and extraction cell selection still use `box-shadow: inset ... var(--accent)` only, per that reasoning. **Multi-select in the tree is the one deliberate exception**: checked/multi-selected rows get both the ring *and* an `--accent-soft` background fill, because that state now has an explicit, always-visible checkbox as its primary affordance (see Tree interactions) — the fill reinforces "this row is part of a bulk action set" rather than competing with a hover state for meaning. If adding a new kind of selection indicator, default to ring-only unless it has the same kind of unambiguous explicit-control backing that multi-select has.

## Testing approach used during development

No test framework is shipped with the file (it's a deployed artifact, not a repo with CI). Verification during development used **jsdom** (`npm install jsdom` in a scratch dir), loading the real `philogg.html` with `runScripts: "dangerously"` and driving it through the actual DOM — simulating file drops via a synthetic `drop` event with a `File`, clicking real buttons/rows, and reading back rendered DOM state — rather than re-implementing logic in isolation, which repeatedly caught real integration bugs (stray event-listener bubbling closing a just-opened popup, a closure-over-a-shared-loop-variable bug in hover handlers, stale-DOM assumptions in multi-step interactions). **If asked to verify a change**, prefer this approach — spin up jsdom, load the file, and drive it like a user — over just reading the code and reasoning about it, since several real bugs here were only caught this way. Needs `clientHeight`/`getBoundingClientRect` stubbed (jsdom has no real layout engine) and `navigator.clipboard` stubbed for copy-related tests. jsdom also ships **no IndexedDB** — the session-cache group injects a `fake-indexeddb` `IDBFactory` via `beforeParse` (now a devDependency in the suite's `package.json`); passing the *same* factory instance into two successive jsdom windows simulates a browser reload against the same "disk". All other groups run without it, which doubles as a test of the cache's graceful-degradation path (`openCacheDb` resolves `null`, feature silently off).

**The most consequential bug caught this way**: double-click-to-jump-to-full-log silently stopped working entirely, on every row, every time. Root cause — `selectEntry()`'s plain-click path called `renderVisibleRows()`, which does `tableRows.innerHTML = ""` and rebuilds every row as a *new* DOM node on every single click, including the first click of a double-click. Browsers only count a second click toward a `dblclick` if it lands on the *same element* as the first; since that element no longer existed by the time the second click landed, `dblclick` never fired — not intermittently, but always. Confirmed via jsdom by asserting `row === document.querySelector('[data-entry-id="..."]')` was still true *after* dispatching a plain `"click"` event on it. Fixed by having a plain click only toggle the `.selected` class on the existing rendered elements (`updateSelectedRowClass`) instead of tearing the row list down; the arrow-key path still goes through `renderVisibleRows()` via `scrollToIndex()` since it may need to bring an off-screen row into view. **Lesson for future work on this file**: recreating a DOM element in response to the first half of any multi-step native browser gesture (double-click, drag, anything relying on element identity across events) will silently defeat that gesture — verify element identity survives a single interaction before assuming a click/drag handler is "just cosmetic."

**jsdom gotcha worth knowing before writing more tests**: top-level `function` declarations in the inline `<script>` become `window` properties (so `window.render()`, `window.createFilterNode(...)` etc. work directly from a Node test), but top-level `let`/`const` — `state`, `extractRowsData`, `plotConfig`, `undoStack`/`redoStack`, and most other module state — do **not**; they only live in the shared global lexical scope, per spec, same as in a real browser. Reading (or resetting) them from outside requires injecting a second `<script>` element into the same jsdom document (`document.body.appendChild(...)`) whose body exposes getters (`window.__t = { get state() { return state; }, ... }`) or small helper functions (`window.__resetUndoRedo = () => { undoStack = []; redoStack = []; }`) — since that injected script runs in the same realm's global lexical scope, it can see the bindings directly. `window.state.foo = x` (mutating a property of the object the getter returns) works fine without a setter; `window.state = x` (replacing the binding itself) does not and fails silently.

One thing jsdom **can't** catch: real hit-testing / paint order (`elementFromPoint`, whether a `position:fixed` overlay visually covers something and intercepts its clicks) — jsdom has no layout or rendering engine, so this class of bug (e.g. the message-popover issue above, or the file-drop overlay swallowing tree drag events) has to be reasoned about directly from CSS (`position`, `z-index`, `pointer-events`) and event bubbling/timing, then verified by state-based proxies (checking `hidden`/`show` classes at the right moments) rather than by literally observing an element being obscured.

## Known limitations / intentionally deferred

- Value assertions **and `node.ignoredColumns`** are keyed by column **index**, not name — editing an extraction pattern so columns shift position (e.g. removing an earlier placeholder) leaves any assertion, or any ignored-column marker, on a later column silently pointing at the wrong data. No migration/warning exists yet; re-set the assertion / re-check ignored columns after a pattern edit that reorders columns. For ignored columns specifically, this also means the filter popup's `filterPopupIgnored` working set (pre-populated from the node's `ignoredColumns` when Edit is opened) can go stale if the pattern text is then heavily retyped in the same edit session — same sharp edge, one level earlier.
- (Resolved) Bookmarks used to be session-only; the session cache now persists them (with files, filters, and settings) across reloads. (Resolved) **Tail handles are now persisted too** — see "Tailing (live file updates)" below for `tryReattachFileTail()`. Remaining session-cache limitations: **A restored merged file no longer shares entry objects with its source files** — it's rebuilt from its own cached text, so the identity-sharing described in `mergeFiles` doesn't survive a reload; each file still works standalone, it just costs the duplicate memory and a bookmark on a shared entry resolves to only one of the files. **Undo/redo stacks and the clipboard are not persisted** — they're deliberately session-only, same as before this session's scope extension. Disabling the cache has no UI — `localStorage["philogg-cache-enabled"] = "0"` is the escape hatch.
- Session export/import (see "Session export / import" above): tier-2 matching only covers a loaded file whose time span **fully contains** the exported span — a receiving file that's a rotated/truncated *partial* overlap of the export can't be auto-verified and always falls to the manual tier-3 dialog, even though a human could often tell at a glance that it's the right file. Import has no dedup against an already-present equivalent branch, same known gap as filter save/load — re-importing the same session twice produces visible duplicate filter trees. Fingerprinting/hashing a file is O(file size) and runs synchronously on the main thread; not chunked/yielded like the initial parse, so a very large file could cause a brief UI stall during export or tier-1/2 matching on import. A merged file's re-matching is best-effort only: it round-trips fine when it happens to reproduce the same combined content and entry count, but there's no dedicated identity-matching path for "this merged file's *sources* changed" the way there is for a plain file.
- Undo/redo still doesn't cover filter/file *creation*, folder-file close, or context-filter creation (see "Undo / redo" for the full current scope and why) — everything else named in the original feature-backlog item (file deletion, value/pattern edits, invert toggle, assertion changes) is now covered.
- Filter inversion (NOT) is unsupported by design for `link` (pair-entry ids don't correspond to `parentEntries` ids — see "Inversion (NOT)"), `extract` (an inverted match set has nothing left to extract), and `context` (every window contains its own reference entry, so an inverted result would always be empty).
- The `context` filter's bracket rendering resolves group start/end caps against the full result array (not just the virtualized render window), so caps are always correct — but if a group's start scrolls more than the row buffer (`BUFFER_ROWS`) above the viewport, only what's actually rendered gets a bracket; the line simply doesn't extend into rows that were never created as DOM nodes. Cosmetic only, no functional impact.
- 3D scatter plot (explicitly lower priority, not built).
- Cross-file filter combination (AND/OR/Link across two different root files) is unsupported by design — merge first.
- Filter save/load never checks whether the destination tree already has an equivalent branch — every "Load filter…" creates a fresh independent copy, so re-loading the same file (or loading onto a tree with a matching chain) produces visible duplicates. See "Filter save / load" section above.
- (Resolved) Extraction table and link pair view are now both row/block-virtualized — see "Virtualized rendering: extraction table & Link pair view".
- (Resolved) The timeline minimap used to rebuild its whole-file background buckets (an O(root file size) pass) on every `renderTable()` call. The background pass is now memoized (`minimapBgCache`, keyed on root id + entry count + time span + bucket count — so it self-invalidates on tail append, rotation, and resize); only the overlay pass (O(current view)) still runs per render. This was the biggest fixed cost when switching between filters on large files.
- Sorting the extraction table (per-column sort buttons) reorders `extractRowsData` directly rather than keeping a separate display order, so it also reorders the connected Plot tab's line/bar point sequence for that extraction — accepted trade-off, see "Extraction workflow" above.
- (Resolved, this session, 2026-08-17, from `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md`) Level colors are now re-themed for light mode instead of reusing the dark-mode hex values verbatim — see "Light theme" below.
- Tailing requires the File System Access API (Chromium) — Firefox and the legacy `<input>` fallback load files as one-time snapshots without live updates. Byte-offset slicing assumes the writer flushes whole UTF-8 characters (log writers do). On rotation/truncation the file node's entries are replaced and the old ids are released from `entryIndex` via `releaseEntriesFromIndex()` (no per-rotation index leak anymore); a merged file that reused the old entry objects still keeps the stale ones — and, because it references them, also keeps them alive in the index, which is intentional. Each tail change now invalidates only the caches that actually depend on the changed file(s) (`invalidateCachesForRoots`, see Memoization) instead of every cache globally — filters under untouched files stay cached across polls. Within the changed file itself, filters still recompute from scratch each tick; an incremental filter-append remains the next optimization if a single very large tailed file with deep filter chains ever feels sluggish. (Resolved, this session, 2026-08-17, person-reported) A plain (non-folder) file's tail now survives a page reload too, same as a watched folder's own files already did: `persistFileNode` stashes `node.tail.handle` alongside the file's text, and `restoreSessionFromCache` calls the new `tryReattachFileTail(node, handle)` — `handle.queryPermission({mode:"read"})` (no user gesture needed) either resumes tailing invisibly (fresh `offset` computed from the restored text's own length, so the next poll only reads genuinely new bytes) or leaves the file a static snapshot, same graceful-degradation shape `restoreWatchedFolders` already used for directory handles. Root cause of the person-reported bug: a file the session cache silently auto-restored on relaunch — experienced as just "opening the file" — never got a `node.tail` at all, so an actively-growing log neither updated nor showed the pulsing live dot until manually re-opened. Skipped for folder-owned files (`node.folderId`), which keep reattaching via `restoreWatchedFolders`/`mergeScannedFiles` as before. (Resolved, same day, person-reported same-session follow-up) With that fix in place, tailing DID start updating and showing the live dot — but stopped again a few seconds later. Root cause: `tailTick`'s catch block treated ANY `getFile()`/read failure as permanent (`t.failed = true` on the very first one) and logged nothing at all, so there was no way to tell a transient failure (most plausibly the writer briefly holding the file locked without shared-read access — an ordinary condition for a log actively being appended to) from a genuinely permanent one, or even to see that a failure had happened. Fixed with a consecutive-failure counter, `t.errorCount`/`TAIL_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_ERRORS` (5) — see "Tailing (live file updates)" → "Read failures are retried, not fatal on the first one."
- The Highlight view has no tail auto-follow of its own — while the Filter view scrolls with new tailed entries (`state.tailFollow`), the Highlight view just keeps whatever scroll position it had. Deliberate v1 scope: it's meant as a stable reference view while browsing, and follow-to-bottom would fight that. Revisit if this turns out to matter for live-tailing workflows.
- The hue wheel in the highlight-colour picker only picks hue — saturation/lightness are fixed (68%/55%) rather than a full 2D color picker. Chosen for a simpler, more predictable widget; the preset row covers cases where a specific exact color matters.
- (Resolved) In stacked Filter/Highlight layout, the Filter/Highlight tab buttons used to stay visible but functionally inert. The tri-state Full/Filtered/Stacked toggle now switches straight out of Stacked into tabs mode on the clicked panel instead of doing nothing — see "Highlight view (Filter/Highlight split)".
- A row that's both highlight-matched and part of an active time-context filter's bracket shows both markers in the same left-gutter slot (`.hl-markers`/`.ctx-bracket` both sit at `left:-9px`), overlapping rather than each getting its own lane. Accepted trade-off, not expected to come up often — revisit if it does.
- Highlight-rule matching walks every colored node in the tree on every Highlight-view render (memoized per-node via `getEntries`'s own cache, so cheap in practice) — would be the first thing to optimize if someone sets colors on a very large number of nodes at once.
- The collapsed sidebar's 40px rail (see "Collapsible sidebar / detail panel" below) shows only the active node's ancestor chain (or root files if nothing's active), not the whole tree — a 40px strip can't fit a large tree anyway, and hover-peek exists precisely to show the real thing. A person wanting to jump to a sibling branch while collapsed has to peek (hover) rather than click a rail marker for it directly.

## Status / changelog

Keep this section updated as features land — newest first, short entries, enough for a future session to know what exists without re-reading the whole chat history.

- **Main window visual consistency fix** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested — extends the Settings dialog's own `.btn-mini` button system to the toolbar/view-bar, which had drifted into per-control hand-tuned CSS: three separate rounds of "height-parity bugfix" comments on `.crumb`/`.level-btn`/`#btnPinBookmarks` chasing pixel parity via line-height arithmetic instead of a shared box model). Two problems fixed. **(1)** Introduced a shared 28px, flex-centered control height reused by role instead of copy-pasted padding/line-height: an icon-button shape (28x28, `border-radius:7px`) for `.toolbar-icon-btn` — now also covers `#btnPinBookmarks`/`#btnMultilineMsg`/`#btnColumns` with no id-specific override needed — and a pill shape (`border-radius:20px`, height:28px) for `.level-btn`/`.crumb`; `.view-tabs`/`.view-tab` get the same 28px height via `height:100%` filling its container instead of a hand-computed line-height. `#btnOpen`/`#btnSession` (previously two byte-for-byte-identical rule blocks) were merged into one, also given the 28px height. All the "height-parity bugfix"/ROUND-1/ROUND-2 comments this replaced are gone. **(2)** The three panel resizers (`#sidebarResizer`/`#fhSplitResizer`/`#detailResizer`) were fully transparent at rest, only showing an accent line on hover/drag — between two `--bg-panel` surfaces this read as a stray dark seam (`--bg-app` showing through) rather than an intentional divider, almost certainly the reported "gap between Files & Filters and the minimap." Now show a permanent grip bar (`var(--border)` at rest, `var(--accent)` on hover/drag). Also fixed the actual token mismatch at that seam: `#timelineMinimap`'s `border-bottom` used `--border-soft` while `#sidebar`'s `border-right` (the adjoining seam) used `--border` — both now `--border`. Pure CSS, no functional/DOM changes; `.tree-action-btn`/`.folder-watch-reconnect`/`.tree-ghost-restore-btn` deliberately left alone (not part of a row requiring cross-control alignment). See "Visual language" → "Shared control box model" above. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 90 (new); Group 26/29b rewritten in place to assert the new 28px-height model instead of the superseded line-height arithmetic; Group 86a's `#btnOpen:hover` selector-text lookup updated for the merged rule. 1669 passed, 0 failed.
- **Bugfix: a file opened via desktop launch argument now gets tailed** (this session, 2026-08-22, `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` item 11). `loadUrlIntoTree` — the single path behind every desktop-wrapper file open, including the very first file passed as a command-line/launch argument (`desktop/main.js` `fileArgFromArgv`/`createWindow`), a later file-association double-click (`second-instance`/`open-file`, via `window.philoggLoadUrl`), and an ordinary `?url=` CI report link — built its node purely from already-fetched text via `addFile()`, so it never got a `node.tail`: a live log opened this way just sat as a dead byte snapshot even though the desktop wrapper serves it straight off disk, where it can keep growing. Fixed by recognizing the desktop wrapper's own `philogg://local/<id>/…` scheme (`isDesktopLocalUrl`) — every fetch to it re-reads the file fresh via `fs.promises.readFile`, see `registerProtocol` in `desktop/main.js` — and attaching a `node.tail` whose handle (`urlTailHandle`) just re-fetches that same URL and wraps the bytes in a `Blob`, reusing the existing `tailTick` poll loop (see "Tailing") completely unchanged. An ordinary `http(s)` `?url=` deliberately stays untouched (no repeated background fetches to an arbitrary report link). As a side effect this also covers the backlog item's softer second ask ("periodically re-check static files for changes"): a desktop-opened file that never grows still gets polled every `TAIL_POLL_MS`, instead of never being looked at again after the initial load. `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 89 (new); Groups 66/67's fetch mocks updated from `text()` to `arrayBuffer()` to match the production code's switch (needed for correct byte-offset tracking). 1650 passed, 0 failed.
- **Catppuccin background hierarchy remapped to match the style guide** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-directed follow-up to the compliance audit below — asked explicitly whether to keep the app's own pre-existing brightness ordering or remap to the guide, chose remap). `--bg-app`/`--bg-panel`/`--bg-elevated`/`--bg-elevated-2`/`--border-soft`/`--border` now map to Base/Mantle/Surface0/Surface1/Surface2/Overlay0 respectively — the same role assignment across all four Catppuccin flavors — instead of the previous Crust/Mantle/Base/Surface0/Surface1/Surface0 ordering that preserved this app's own Dark/Light hierarchy (main pane darkest, chrome brighter) at the cost of inverting the guide's intent (main pane = Base, a brighter tone; chrome = the darker Crust/Mantle). Catppuccin themes' sidebar/toolbar now read visibly darker than the log table — a real, intentional divergence from how Dark/Light look, not a bug. `--accent-on`/`--level-*-on` (On Accent text = Base) simplified to `var(--bg-app)` uniformly across all four flavors as a result (previously `--bg-elevated` for the three dark ones / `--bg-panel` for Latte, back when those held Base). See "Theming" below; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 88, Group 85a's Mocha/Latte assertions updated for the new values.
- **Catppuccin style-guide compliance audit** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested, checked against https://github.com/catppuccin/catppuccin/blob/d09787dd98ca6fba08af5ef2ae94a7e09f17daca/docs/style-guide.md fetched fresh, not from memory). Two confirmed gaps fixed immediately: "On Accent" text now uses each flavor's own Base color (new `--accent-on`/`--level-*-on` var wiring) instead of a generic near-black/white; text selection (`::selection`) now uses Overlay 2 @ 25% opacity for the four Catppuccin flavors (new `--selection-bg` var, reusing `--level-debug` which already = Overlay 2 per flavor) instead of the accent tint every other theme still uses. A third finding (the background-pane brightness hierarchy) was initially left as a flagged, undecided deviation — see the entry above for how that was resolved. Two remaining deliberate, NOT-changed deviations: Warnings use Peach not Yellow (matches this app's own pre-Catppuccin amber warn semantic; the guide's own Code-Editor table allows Peach too); Info uses Blue not Teal (Teal is already `--accent`; reusing it would make INFO rows collide visually with active/selected state). See "Theming" above for the full writeup.
- **Accent color: re-pick the app's own accent from the theme's palette** (this session, 2026-08-22, same-day clarification: "Highlights" in the prior request meant the app's own accent/selection color — buttons and the minimap's time-range marking — not the per-filter-node highlight-color picker the prior entry built). Settings → Appearance gained an "Accent color" row (swatches from `themeOwnPalette(themeId)`, shown only for a theme that has one — the four Catppuccin flavors, or a custom theme whose JSON sets its own `highlightPalette`), letting a person re-pick `--accent` from the active theme's own colors. `setAccentColor`/`applyAccentChoice` recompute `--accent-strong`/`--accent-soft`/`--accent-on` together via a JS mix-toward-white(dark)/black(Latte) + luminance check, persisted per-theme (`localStorage["philogg-accent-choice"]`) so a Mocha pick doesn't affect Latte. Deliberately a JS computation, not `color-mix()`, so each flavor's hand-curated two-tone default (e.g. Mocha's Teal+Sky) stays untouched until someone actually picks something else. See "Theming" above; Group 87.
- **Theming follow-up: stylesheet color audit + theme-aware highlight-color picker** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested: "Prüfe, ob konsequent umgesetzt... gib mir eine Option, eine Highlightfarbe zu wählen, die auf die im Theme festgelegten Farben freigegeben ist").
