Audited every CSS rule outside the theme-definition blocks for leftover hardcoded colors — found ~18 (scrollbar thumb, toolbar button hover borders, brand mark, Save/active-chip text, and the highest-impact group: breadcrumb/level-pill/level-badge/log-row-hover/token-chip alpha-tinted borders and backgrounds) still hardcoding the DARK theme's own hex/rgba values regardless of the active theme — invisible on Dark, wrong everywhere else. Fixed with two new vars (`--accent-on` for text-on-accent surfaces, `--border-hover` derived via `color-mix()` from `--border`/`--text-tertiary`) and `color-mix(in srgb, var(--x) N%, transparent)` for the alpha-tinted cases (CSS's only way to apply alpha to a themed `var()` color). The timeline minimap needed no fixes — already fully `fill:var(--level-error)`-etc. from when it was built. Separately, the per-filter-node highlight-color picker (previously theme-independent) gained a "Free"/"Theme" mode toggle: Theme mode restricts its preset swatches to the ACTIVE theme's own `highlightPalette` (the 14 named Catppuccin accent colors per flavor, or a custom theme's own optional array) instead of the generic curated set, so a chosen highlight color is guaranteed to belong to the current theme. See "Theming" above; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 86.
- **Configurable themes + Catppuccin** (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested). Settings → Appearance's Light/Dark button pair became a `#settingsThemeSelect` dropdown: Dark, Light, and four new Catppuccin flavors (Latte/Frappé/Macchiato/Mocha — https://catppuccin.com/palette/) as built-ins, each its own `:root[data-theme="..."]` CSS block on the existing var set, plus a new "Custom themes" card for importing themes as JSON (download a template pre-filled from whichever theme is currently active, edit the hex values, import it back — validated against the full required-key set, persisted to `localStorage["philogg-custom-themes"]`, applied via inline CSS custom properties on `<html>` instead of a CSS block since there's no way to pre-author one for an arbitrary import). New `--level-*-on` vars replace the old hardcoded near-black text on the active level-quick-filter buttons, themeable via a per-theme `activeTextLight` boolean instead of a hand-written CSS override per light-background theme. See "Theming" above for the full mechanism; `tests/philogg.regression.test.js` Group 85 (Groups 3/70h/70h2 updated in place for the dropdown, not left testing removed buttons).
- New: absolute-value wildcard conditions + separator change (this session,
  2026-08-22, same-day follow-up to the entry directly below — person asked
  whether the `;`-separated syntax was sensible/conventional and requested
  an absolute-value option, *"größer als absolut zehn"*). The condition
  separator changed from `;` to `,` (`[value:int<20,>10]`), and a condition's
  operator can now be prefixed with `|` to compare against `|value|` instead
  of `value` — `[value:float|>=10]` matches both `10` and `-15`. Per-condition,
  not per-placeholder, so an abs and a plain condition can mix on the same
  placeholder. Every visualization spot (pattern preview badge, pattern-view
  chip, table header) shows the `|` prefix too, and the pattern preview's
  sample-match search can now legitimately land on a negative value for an
  abs condition. See "Value-extraction pattern language" → "Value
  conditions". 1539 passed, 0 failed (Group 81 extended, not a new group).

- New: wildcard value conditions (this session, 2026-08-22, person-requested,
  `FEATURE_BACKLOG.md` item 5 implemented differently than scoped there).
  `[value:float]`/`[value:int]` placeholders can now carry an inline numeric
  condition — `[value:float>=10]`, or `[value:int<20;>10]` for a `;`-AND-ed
  range — working through the existing filter/extract wildcard paths only
  (no new filter type, no new node field). Applies to both extraction and a
  plain "text" filter carrying wildcard tokens; the filter popup's live-match
  count and pattern preview (which now only ever shows a sample that
  actually satisfies the condition) and every place that visualizes a
  placeholder's type (pattern preview, post-creation pattern-view chip,
  extraction table column header) now also show the condition. See
  "Value-extraction pattern language" → "Value conditions". 1529 passed, 0
  failed (Group 81, new).

- Bugfix, four rounds (this session, 2026-08-22, person-reported via
  screenshots, same-session follow-up to the Settings/collapse redesign
  directly below): **(1)** the sidebar's hover-peek overlay
  (`#sidebarNormalContent` while `.peeking`, see that entry) had no
  `background` of its own — visually detached from `#sidebar`'s 40px box via
  `position:absolute`, so it painted transparent and the log table/timeline
  minimap underneath bled straight through it; fixed with an explicit
  `background:var(--bg-panel)` on that rule. **(2)** The detail panel's
  collapse/expand chevron pointed the wrong way in both states — `.dc.html`
  names its two path variants by open/closed state, not by the actual
  direction the chevron draws, and both the static HTML fallback and
  `ICON_CHEVRON_DOWN`/`ICON_CHEVRON_UP` were copied over with the names and
  visual directions crossed; swapped both path bodies so `_DOWN` truly
  points down and `_UP` truly points up, matching their already-correct
  `collapsed ? ... : ...` usage. **(3)** Settings' left-nav category buttons
  looked unclickable: their `.active` highlight was driven solely by the
  scroll-position `IntersectionObserver` (see the entry below), so clicking
  a category whose section was already fully on screen — no scrolling
  needed — produced zero visible feedback even though the click handler
  (and `scrollIntoView`) fired correctly. The click handler now also sets
  `.active` directly, immediately, independent of whatever the observer
  decides once scrolling (if any) settles. **(4)** The font-size stepper's
  `−`/`+` glyphs rendered top-left instead of centered in their 32×30 boxes
  — the shared base `.btn-icon` class is `display:flex` but never sets
  `align-items`/`justify-content` (fine for its usual single-SVG-child
  usage elsewhere, invisible until text content needed real centering);
  added both to the `.font-scale-stepper .btn-icon` override. All four
  verified against a real Chromium render (Playwright), not just jsdom,
  which can't catch pure paint/geometry bugs like these — see "Known gaps"
  in `tests/README.md`. 1509 passed, 0 failed (no behavior change, so no
  new test group).

- New: Settings dialog redesign + collapsible sidebar/detail panel (this
  session, 2026-08-22, implementing a Claude-Design handoff bundle —
  "Settings Dialog und Panel-Navigation" — carried over from a prior
  session's design-mockup pass; no new open questions, straight
  implementation against the mockup + a written handoff doc).

  **Settings dialog** (`#settingsDialog`): added a left section-nav column
  (`#settingsNav`, Appearance/Behavior/Log Formats, 216px) next to the
  existing single scrollable body (`.settings-page-body` unchanged in
  substance — all three sections still render there, in order) — clicking a
  nav item scrolls to its section; an `IntersectionObserver` (guarded with
  `typeof IntersectionObserver !== "undefined"` — jsdom has none) keeps the
  nav highlighted on whichever section is actually in view, `rootMargin:
  "0px 0px -70% 0px"` so the top-of-viewport section wins. Every settings row
  now sits inside a `.settings-card` (`--bg-panel`/`--border-soft`/10px
  radius, matching the design's exact color values, which happened to equal
  existing tokens 1:1) with a unified `.settings-row` grid
  (`grid-template-columns:1fr auto`, label+hint left / control right at a
  shared edge), one control height (30px) and radius throughout, and bigger
  type (13.5px labels, 12px hints, up from 12.5px/10.5px). Button hierarchy:
  the filled accent button (`.btn-mini`) is now reserved for the one primary
  action per panel (Save format/Save rule) — list-row `Edit`/`Reset` moved
  from `.btn-mini` to the existing `.btn-mini-outline`, and `+ Add
  format…`/`+ Add rule…` got a new `.btn-mini-dashed` (dashed border,
  transparent fill) distinct from both. The boolean "Closing the last log
  file quits the app" row now renders as an on/off switch
  (`.settings-switch`/`.settings-switch-track`, CSS-only over the same real
  `<input type=checkbox>` — no JS/behavior change, still just
  `checked`/`change`) instead of the plain accent-colored checkbox used for
  filter/plot checkboxes elsewhere, so "app preference" reads visually
  distinct from "filter option." Font-size stepper (`−`/value/`+`) regrouped
  into its own `.font-scale-stepper` sub-container so those three sit in one
  seamless bordered pill (previously each had a full border with a gap
  between, since `.font-scale-row`'s flex `gap` applied to all four children
  including `Reset`). GROUP 79.

  **Collapsible sidebar** (`#sidebar`, left/tree): new `#sidebarHeader` (34px
  — "Files & filters" label + chevron) sits above a `#sidebarScroll` wrapper
  now holding the pre-existing `#treeActionBar`/`#folderWatchList`/`#tree`
  (unchanged internally); both together are `#sidebarNormalContent`. Collapse
  sets `#sidebar`'s width to 40px directly (`sidebarEl.style.width`, same
  mechanism the existing drag-resizer already used — no new competing CSS
  width rule) and swaps in `#sidebarRail`, a small hand-built strip (not a
  second tree renderer) showing one clickable marker per node in the
  **active node's ancestor chain** (root file down to whichever's active —
  or per root file if nothing's active yet; see "Known limitations" for why
  not the whole tree), reusing the same `ICON_FILE`/`ICON_MERGE` glyphs and
  `node.highlightColor` a normal tree row would; refreshed from
  `renderTree()` itself so it never goes stale. **Hover-peek**: hovering a
  collapsed rail (`mouseenter`/`mouseleave` on `#sidebar`) adds `.peeking`,
  which makes `#sidebarNormalContent` — the exact same element/markup used
  when expanded, not a separate peek-only render — `position:absolute` over
  the rail and the content area (270px, box-shadow, `overflow:visible` on
  the still-40px `#sidebar` parent so nothing reflows underneath); the
  header's chevron swaps to a pin icon (reusing the existing `ICON_PIN` from
  the bookmarks-pin feature) whose click is the *same* toggle function,
  exiting `.collapsed` entirely (pins it open) rather than a separate pinned
  sub-state. `#sidebarToggle` (the header's own button) is consequently only
  ever visible in two of the three states (expanded, or collapsed+peeking) —
  the third (collapsed, not peeking) is the rail's own separately rendered
  button; `updateSidebarToggleIcon()` only distinguishes the two states it's
  actually shown in.

  **Collapsible detail panel** (`#detailPanel`, bottom): the previous bare
  `#detailMeta` row is now wrapped in `#detailPanelHeader` (34px — fixed
  "Entry detail" label + `#detailMeta` (now `flex:1 1 auto`, single-line,
  clips instead of wrapping) + chevron), with `#detailBody` (placeholder +
  message) below it. Collapse sets `#detailPanel`'s height to 34px directly
  (same style-driven mechanism as the sidebar) and hides `#detailBody` +
  `#detailResizer` (nothing to drag at 34px). `updateDetailPanel()` now
  branches on collapsed state: expanded keeps the existing
  level/time/thread/location/method fields; collapsed swaps to
  level/time/first-message-line (`.detail-collapsed-msg`, CSS ellipsis) so
  the header never goes informationally empty while closed.

  **Shared across both**: `Ctrl+B` (sidebar) / `Ctrl+J` (detail) toggle
  shortcuts (added before the existing `Ctrl+Z`/`Ctrl+Y` undo/redo branch in
  the keydown handler; `Ctrl+B` returning early there also incidentally
  fixed a latent ambiguity where it would have fallen through to the plain
  `b` bookmark-toggle shortcut too, since that check doesn't itself exclude
  `ctrlKey`) and a resizer double-click (`dblclick` on
  `#sidebarResizer`/`#detailResizer`) both toggle the same way the header
  chevron does. Collapsed state persists to `localStorage`
  (`philogg-sidebar-collapsed`/`philogg-detail-collapsed`, "1"/"0") — same
  display-preference tier as `THEME_STORAGE_KEY`/`FONT_SCALE_STORAGE_KEY`,
  restored via `initSidebarCollapsed()`/`initDetailCollapsed()` at boot.
  **Last dragged width/height is restored in-session, not via
  localStorage** — matching this codebase's existing convention that the
  sidebar/detail drag-resizers themselves are session-only (see "Five
  changes to the Filtered/Full split and the tree" above); `sidebarLastWidth`
  /`detailLastHeight` are plain JS variables, refreshed from
  `getBoundingClientRect()` right before each collapse. GROUP 80.

  Both parts came with a `.dc.html` design-tool file (colors/radii/spacing
  read directly from it, not eyeballed) and a chat transcript recording one
  clarified requirement worth keeping in mind if this needs revisiting: the
  hover-peek must show the *exact* expanded-state content, not a reduced
  preview — an earlier design-tool iteration got that wrong and was
  corrected before handoff; the implementation here satisfies it
  structurally (same DOM, not a second render path) rather than by
  convention. 1509 passed, 0 failed.

- New: `Enter` on a selected row in the Filtered view (or the Filtered/bottom pane of the Stacked view) mirrors that row's own `dblclick` (this session, 2026-08-21, person-requested, German: *"Wenn eine einzelne Zeile in im Filtered oder im unteren Teil des Stacked View ausgewählt ist, soll Enter das gleiche tun wie ein Doppelklick auf die Zeile."*). New keydown branch alongside the existing tree-focus `Enter` handling: with `state.focusRegion === "entries"`, `state.entriesView === "filter"`, a single `state.selectedId` and no active `state.logMultiSelect`, calls `revealInHighlightView(entryIndex[state.selectedId])` — the exact same call the row's own `dblclick` listener already makes (`renderVisibleRows`), so it centres the Full view on the entry without touching `activeId`/the level filter/the Filter view's own scroll position. Left alone with a multi-selection active (no single obvious target) or with focus elsewhere (tree, Full/Highlight pane). GROUP 78.

- Bugfix, two rounds (person-reported, screenshots, 2026-08-21): the
  horizontal scrollbar in the Filter view (item 17 below, shipped earlier
  the same session) left row backgrounds and the usable scroll range not
  actually covering a long message's full width.
  - **Round 1**: the level-tint row background (`.log-row.lvl-error` etc.)
    stopped at the viewport edge instead of the scrolled-to message's real
    width. Root cause: `.log-row`'s own box never grew to match the
    overflow `#tableRows .col-msg{min-width:max-content}` creates one level
    down — a `width:auto` block box sizes to its containing block
    regardless of content; the CSS Grid track inside it can overflow (grid
    tracks refuse to shrink below their minimum) without the box itself
    following. First fix: `#tableRows .log-row{width:max-content;
    min-width:100%}`, making each row's own box follow ITS grid content's
    natural size. (Note `max-content`, not `fit-content` — item 17's own
    first attempt at sizing `.log-row` used `fit-content` and was abandoned
    as a no-op, since `fit-content` clamps to *available* space whenever
    content is wider than it, mathematically identical to `width:auto`'s
    stretch in exactly this overflow case; `max-content` has no such
    clamp.)
  - **Round 2** (same day, follow-up report): round 1's per-row width was
    itself the wrong shape — each row sized independently from only ITS OWN
    message, so short-message rows stayed narrow while only the row
    holding a long message grew, and the scrollbar's usable range shrank
    back the instant that row scrolled out of the virtualized window
    (`#tableRows` only ever has ~30-50 real rows as DOM nodes at a time).
    Reverted the per-row CSS entirely. Real fix: one width computed up
    front from the WIDEST message across the WHOLE current view (not just
    the rendered window), applied to `#tableRows` itself —
    `computeMaxMessageWidth(currentViewEntries)` (cheap O(n) char-count
    scan to find the longest single line, ONE canvas `measureText` call on
    it — monospace font, so char count is a reliable width proxy) feeds
    `syncTableRowsWidth()`, which sets `tableRows.style.width` to
    `lastColumnEdgeX + gap + maxMsgPixelWidth + padding` when that exceeds
    the viewport, or clears the inline style (falls back to the original
    `left:0;right:0` stretch-fill) when it doesn't — so a file with only
    short messages still fills the view. `lastColumnEdgeX` is captured by
    `applyRowGrid` (the existing column-width/visibility recompute) so a
    column resize/hide keeps the row width in sync too, cheaply (no
    re-scan of entries — only `cachedMaxMsgPixelWidth`, computed once per
    `currentViewEntries` change at the same two call sites
    `buildRowOffsets` already has, is expensive). Every `.log-row` is back
    to a plain `width:auto` block, so all of them inherit this one shared
    width uniformly — same background/scroll extent whether a given row's
    own message is long or empty, exactly what fixes the follow-up report.
  - **Round 3** (same day, third report): asked to build the exact repro
    myself — a log with 4 messages of increasing length (short/medium/long/
    a multi-line stack trace) — and check with Playwright whether scrolling
    all the way right actually reaches the longest message's last
    character, or overshoots. It overshot: `#tableRows` was sized correctly
    per round 2's own math, but consistently wider than where the text
    actually ended — the gap grew with the message's length (72px on a
    4878px-wide, 701-character line in the repro). Root cause:
    `measureMsgWidth` used Canvas2D's `measureText()`, which measurably
    diverges from Blink's own CSS layout text renderer for an IDENTICAL
    `font-family`/`font-size` string — confirmed directly, side by side, on
    the exact same 701-character string: canvas measured 6.957px/char, a
    real DOM element measured 6.878px/char (~1.15% apart) — small alone,
    but compounding LINEARLY with message length, so exactly the long-line
    case this feature exists for was the case it got most wrong. Not a
    rounding nit — two different browser text-rendering pipelines (canvas
    glyph shaping vs. layout line-boxing) simply don't promise pixel-
    identical advance widths for the same font, even within the same engine.
    Fixed by dropping canvas entirely: `measureMsgWidth` now reads
    `offsetWidth` off a hidden, reused DOM element carrying the exact same
    `.col-msg` class real rows use (`position:absolute`, shrink-to-fit,
    parked off-screen) — literally the same rendering path a real row uses,
    so it cannot diverge from it. Re-verified the same 4-message repro: the
    gap dropped from 72px to 15.5px, which is exactly `#tableRows`' own
    16px right padding (`padding:2px 16px 20px 14px`) — i.e. the real
    remaining "gap" isn't a bug at all, it's the intentional padding.
    Checked `offsetWidth` specifically (not `getBoundingClientRect().width`)
    against `state.fontScale`'s whole-UI `zoom`, with Playwright, before
    committing to it: `offsetWidth`/`clientWidth`/`scrollWidth` all stay in
    the same "unscaled CSS px" space regardless of zoom level (confirmed:
    identical before/after setting `zoom:1.5` on a test element), while
    `getBoundingClientRect()` returns the zoomed VISUAL size instead — the
    latter would have put `syncTableRowsWidth`'s math off by the zoom factor
    at any scale other than 100%, a bug that would only have shown up once
    someone actually used the font-scale feature at a non-default zoom.
  Verified with a real Chromium session throughout (Playwright, headless,
  `#fileInput.setInputFiles` + screenshots + precise `Range`-based text-edge
  measurements) — jsdom has no real layout engine, the documented blind
  spot. Round 2 and round 3's actual sizing logic
  (`computeMaxMessageWidth`/`syncTableRowsWidth`/`measureMsgWidth`) IS plain
  testable JS though, unlike round 1's pure-CSS fix — GROUP 73b/73c
  (`tests/philogg.regression.test.js`) cover it directly. jsdom has no real
  layout engine either, so a freshly created element's `offsetWidth` is
  always 0 there (same blind spot the highlight-marker tooltip positioning
  code already works around) — `measureMsgWidth` falls back to a flat
  per-character estimate (`MONO_CHAR_WIDTH_FALLBACK`) in that case, which is
  what GROUP 73c's assertions exercise and pin down exactly.

- Bugfix, two rounds (this session, 2026-08-21), person-reported via screenshot: the sample-line pattern **suggestion** (`suggestPatternFromSample`, right below Format Manager) broke on a PLC log, reading everything past the first few lines as one giant message.
  - Round 1: the log uses `;SSS` (semicolon) as the millisecond separator, e.g. `2026-08-14 10:43:18;617`. `SAMPLE_TS_CANDIDATES`'s fractional-seconds regex only matched `.`/`,`, so it fell through to the no-milliseconds shape and left the sample's own ms value (`;617`) as literal pattern text — only lines sharing that exact millisecond kept matching. `compileDateFormat` already escapes whatever literal separator sits between `%d`'s tokens generically, so this was purely a suggestion-heuristic gap — fixed by capturing whichever separator character the sample actually uses and reproducing it in `tsFormat` instead of hardcoding `,`. Incidentally also fixes `.`-separated samples, which previously always got a `,` tsFormat regardless of what the sample used.
  - Round 2 (person reported the fix was still breaking at the same spot): the real remaining cause. The log's header lines (`TRACE\t-\t[System]\t...`) and its FIFO-data lines (`WARN\tAxCtrl\t[-]\t...`) share one shape — `%d %p <bare-word> [%M] %m` — but the suggester only recognized a thread wrapped in quotes or parens, never a bare unquoted one. On the sample line the bare word between level and `[System]` was `-`, which got read as fixed literal text: it matched every header line (all literally `-`) but not the FIFO-data lines, where that position holds a real, varying value (`AxCtrl`, `CrashP`, ...). Fixed by claiming a single whitespace-free token sitting directly between `%p` and an already-claimed `%M` bracket as `%t` — narrow and structurally cued (framed by the level on one side, an immediate `[` on the other) rather than a blind "next word is the thread" guess that would misread an ordinary free-form message's first word.
  - GROUP 77 (both rounds).
- New: six FEATURE_BACKLOG.md items in one session (this session, 2026-08-21) — items 14, 15, 17, 18, 20, 21:
  - **Configurable font size** (14): a whole-UI zoom (`applyFontScale`, `document.documentElement.style.zoom`) rather than threading a font-size variable through the many individually hardcoded font-size declarations in the file. Settings -> Appearance gets a +/-/Reset row, `Ctrl+Plus`/`Ctrl+Minus` do the same; both persisted to `localStorage` (`philogg-font-scale`) like the theme toggle, clamped 70%-160% in 10% steps. GROUP 71.
  - **Tree/Log-view shortcuts** (15): `Ctrl+0` focuses the filter tree at whichever node is ALREADY active — a filter included, not just its root file — so arrow keys continue navigating from wherever the person currently is, falling back to the first root file only if nothing's active yet; `Ctrl+1`/`Ctrl+2`/`Ctrl+3` mirror the Full/Filtered/Stacked toggle buttons (`#fhTabs`) one-for-one and additionally focus that Log view's entries for arrow-key navigation. New `state.entriesView` (`"filter"` | `"highlight"`) tracks which of the two Log views owns ↑/↓ right now — set by the shortcuts above and by a plain click/dblclick in either view (`selectEntry`/`selectHighlightEntry`/`revealInHighlightView`) — and is read by the global ArrowUp/Down handler to pick `moveSelection` vs. the new `moveHighlightSelection` (arrow-key navigation didn't exist at all for the Full view before this). `Enter` on an active filter node while the tree has focus still reveals the Filtered view. **Revised twice same session** after person feedback: the first pass had `Ctrl+1` focusing the tree and `Ctrl+0` revealing the Filtered view (the reverse of what was wanted); the second pass got the Ctrl+1/2/3 mapping right but had `Ctrl+0` jump up to the active node's root FILE instead of staying on whatever filter was already active, undoing the "navigate from wherever you are" point of the shortcut — fixed to keep the existing active node as-is. GROUP 72.
  - **Horizontal scrollbar in the Filter view** (17): `#tableBody` scrolls horizontally now (Filter view only — the Full/Highlight view keeps its original ellipsis truncation). The actual fix is `#tableRows .col-msg{min-width:max-content}`: `.col-msg`'s `overflow:hidden` resets its CSS "automatic minimum size" to 0 per the Sizing spec, which is why the message column's `1fr` grid track always just shrank to fit instead of ever overflowing — restoring an explicit min-width (its own natural, un-wrapped text width) makes that track, and so the row, refuse to shrink below it. The overflow bleeds through `#tableRows`/`#tableSpacer` (both plain `overflow:visible`) up to `#tableBody`, the nearest scrollable ancestor. `#tableHeader` is a separate flex sibling (not a descendant of `#tableBody`), so it gets its own hidden-scrollbar `overflow-x`, a `scrollLeft` synced from `#tableBody`'s scroll event, and its `.row-grid`'s width kept equal to the widest currently-rendered row (`syncTableHeaderWidth`, called from `renderVisibleRows`) since its own content (short column labels) would otherwise size much narrower than the data. **Revised same session**: the first pass (`.log-row{width:fit-content}`) compiled and passed every jsdom test but turned out to do nothing at all visually — verified with a real Chromium session (Playwright) after a person report, not caught by jsdom (no real layout engine there — see "Testing approach"). The `min-width:max-content` fix above was verified the same way: scrolling `#tableBody` now genuinely reveals the rest of a long message, in sync with the header, with the sidebar/tree completely unaffected. GROUP 73 still only covers the JS-observable parts (the `overflow-x` split via computed style, and the header sync/scroll-lockstep logic with `scrollWidth` stubbed) — the CSS overflow mechanism itself has no jsdom coverage either way.
  - **Double-click on a filter opens its edit dialog** (18): NOT a native `dblclick` listener — a tree row's own click handler always ends in a full `render()` that rebuilds every `#tree` row from scratch, the same "DOM identity across clicks" class of bug CLAUDE.md already documents for `renderVisibleRows()`/native `dblclick` (a real double-click's second physical click would land on a different, freshly-rebuilt DOM node and never pair into a native event). Manual click-id+timestamp tracking (`lastTreeRowClickId`/`Time`, 450ms window) sidesteps that entirely. `editFilterNode(nodeId)` is a new shared helper — F2 and the tree context menu's "Edit filter…" action were refactored onto it too (no behavior change there), picking the filter popup or the time-range dialog depending on `filterType`, same split `openTreeContextMenu` already used. Needed its own `ev.stopPropagation()` on the double-click branch — without it, the popup it just opened would immediately close again via the document-level "click outside closes the popup" listener as the same click event finished bubbling (caught by GROUP 74, not guessed). GROUP 74.
  - **Ctrl+W closes the currently open file** (20): the same undo-able `deleteFilterNodeWithUndo` path the tree row's own ✕ button already uses, targeting the active node's root file via `getRootFileId` so it works regardless of which node in that file's chain is active. GROUP 75.
  - **Settings option "Closing the last log file quits the app"** (21), default off: a plain `localStorage` flag (`philogg-quit-on-last-close`, same tier as the theme toggle) rather than session/filter data. `maybeQuitOnLastFileClosed()` calls a bare `window.close()` once `state.rootIds` is empty right after a file close — meaningful mainly under the Electron desktop wrapper (see "Desktop wrapper" below), where Electron intercepts a renderer's own `window.close()` and closes that `BrowserWindow`; in an ordinary browser tab it's a no-op, so the setting simply has no effect there, with no Electron-specific renderer code needed. The checkbox uses the same accent-colored native-checkbox convention as everywhere else in the app (`.plot-checkbox-row`, `.filter-case-toggle`, …) rather than a bespoke toggle, per person feedback on the first pass' unstyled default checkbox. GROUP 76.

  GROUPs 71-76 added. 1428 passed, 0 failed.

- New: Reset button for the builtin default format (this session, 2026-08-21, person-requested: *"add a reset button to the default format where new formats would have there delete button. This button should revert the default entry to it's original state."*). The builtin default row in Settings -> Log Formats never had a delete button (it can't be deleted); `renderFormatList()` now renders a "Reset" button (`.btn-mini-outline`, same secondary style as Cancel elsewhere) in that exact spot instead. `resetDefaultFormat()` restores `name`/`mode`/`pattern`/`regex`/`tsFormat` from the `DEFAULT_LOG_FORMAT` constant and clears the `edited` flag — since `compileOneFormat` only takes the untouched-`HEADER_RE`/`parseHeaderLine` fast path while `edited` is false, this reverts actual PARSING behavior, not just the displayed fields (verified in GROUP 70j by editing the default to a deliberately different pattern, confirming parsing changed, then resetting and confirming a fresh file parses identically to before the edit). Closes the format-edit inline panel first if it happened to be open on the default row, to avoid showing now-stale values. GROUP 70j added. 1378 passed, 0 failed.

- Bugfix: Settings inline panels/toggle were always visible regardless of their "hidden" class — no matching CSS rule existed (this session, 2026-08-21, person-reported with a screenshot: the "Add log format" panel showing immediately on opening Settings, Cancel/Save not closing it). Root cause: this codebase has no global `.hidden{display:none}` rule — every "hidden" toggle needs its OWN element-scoped CSS rule (`#id.hidden{display:none}`, see the many examples throughout the stylesheet) — and the previous session's settings-page rework added `.hidden` to `#formatEditPanel`/`#formatRuleEditPanel`/`#btnAddFormat`/`#btnAddFormatRule`/`#formatEditPatternField`/`#formatEditRegexField` without ever adding matching rules, so `classList.toggle("hidden", ...)` silently had NO visual effect while jsdom-based tests (which only ever asserted `classList.contains("hidden")`, never actual computed style) stayed green throughout. Fixed by adding the three missing rules. Also: `.settings-page-card` is now ~50% larger (`min(1140px, 96vw)`/`min(1080px, 92vh)`, was `min(760px, 92vw)`/`min(720px, 88vh)`), `#btnAddFormat`/`#btnAddFormatRule` hide while their own inline panel is open (restored on Cancel/Save) and match the row Edit buttons' `.btn-mini` style. Caught by manually verifying in a real Chromium session (Playwright) after the person's report — GROUP 70e's visibility assertions were upgraded from `classList.contains("hidden")` to a new `isVisible(el, w)` helper (`tests/philogg.regression.test.js`) that checks `getComputedStyle(el).display` instead, which jsdom resolves correctly from actual CSS rules even without a real layout engine — this is what would have caught the bug in the first place, and prevents the same class of "class toggled, no matching rule" bug from silently passing again. 1365 passed, 0 failed.

- Settings page follow-up: bigger, Add buttons hide while their panel is open (this session, 2026-08-21, person-requested: *"Mache die Settings Page 50% größer... Die Inline Dialoge für 'Add Log format' und 'Add filename rule' sollen nur dann angezeigt werden, wenn man auf den jeweiligen Add Button klickt (dieser ist dann nicht mehr sichtbar, solange der Dialog offen ist)... Nutze für den Add Button den gleichen Stile wie die Edit buttons. Zeige nur das Eingabefeld für conversion pattern oder regex..."*). `.settings-page-card` grew from `min(760px, 92vw)`/`min(720px, 88vh)` to `min(1140px, 96vw)`/`min(1080px, 92vh)` (~50% larger). `#btnAddFormat`/`#btnAddFormatRule` now hide (`.hidden`, restored on Cancel/Save) whenever their own inline panel is open — whether opened via the Add button itself or via a row's Edit button, so only one editor is ever visible at a time — and switched from `.btn-mini-secondary` to plain `.btn-mini`, matching the row Edit buttons' style exactly. The pattern-vs-regex single-field-visible behavior (`updateFormatEditModeUI` toggling `#formatEditPatternField`/`#formatEditRegexField`) was already correct from the original implementation — verified, not changed. GROUP 70e extended: button style, hide/show across both Cancel and Save paths, and the pattern/regex field toggle. 1363 passed, 0 failed.

- Reworked: Settings is now one real settings page, not a dropdown menu (this session, 2026-08-21, person-requested follow-up: *"Es gibt aktuell einen Settings Button ohne Funktion und einen zusätzlichen 'Format manager...' button. Der zweite sollte nicht existieren. Stattdessen sollte der Settings Button ein großes Fenster öffnen indem alle Settings untergebracht sind... Ergänze dabei den Add Format Dialog noch um einen Text Input, wo ich ein Beispiel snippet einfügen kann..."*). Supersedes the `#settingsMenu` dropdown + separate `#formatManagerDialog`/`#formatEditDialog`/`#formatRuleEditDialog` popups described in the entry directly below — those are gone. `#btnSettings` now opens `#settingsDialog` directly: one wide (`min(760px, 92vw)`), tall, scrollable modal card (`.settings-page-card`) with sectioned content (`.settings-section`), built so more sections can be added later without a redesign. Two sections today: **Appearance** (the old standalone `#btnTheme` icon button is gone from the toolbar; theme is now an explicit Light/Dark button pair, `applyTheme()` toggles their `.active` class instead of swapping an icon) and **Log Formats** (the old Format Manager dialog's content, unchanged: format list + filename-rule list, builtin default first and editable). Add/Edit format and Add/Edit rule are no longer separate dialogs either — `#formatEditPanel`/`#formatRuleEditPanel` are inline panels that expand within the Log Formats section itself (`.settings-inline-panel`, `--accent-soft` background + `--accent` border to visually mark them as a temporary editing panel), opened/closed via their own Cancel/Save with no backdrop of their own. Also new: the Add-format panel gained a "Example log line(s)" textarea — `suggestPatternFromSample()` scans the first pasted line for a timestamp shape (against a small candidate list, e.g. `yyyy-MM-dd HH:mm:ss` / `HH:mm:ss,SSS`), a level word, a quoted/parenthesized thread, and a bracketed method (each claimed only if it doesn't overlap an earlier match; delimiters like the quotes/parens themselves are excluded from the claimed span so they round-trip back into the pattern as literal text), turning everything else into literal pattern text with the tail becoming `%m%n` — auto-filling the pattern + timestamp-format fields on every `input` event, always reviewable/editable afterward, never the final word. A live preview (`renderFormatEditPreview()`) re-parses the pasted sample line(s) with whatever's currently in the pattern/regex/tsFormat fields on every relevant `input` event, so a person sees the extracted level/thread/method/message before saving. Caught one bug from this: `Element.prototype.scrollIntoView` (called when a panel opens, to bring it into view) isn't implemented in jsdom — calling it unconditionally threw inside the click handler on every test run (jsdom swallows the exception rather than propagating it, so tests still passed, but noisily) — now guarded with `if (el.scrollIntoView) ...`. Regression suite: GROUP 70e/70h rewritten for the new DOM shape (direct-open settings page, inline panels, sample-suggestion + preview assertions), new GROUP 70h2 (theme buttons actually switch/persist), GROUP 3 and the loading-responsiveness spot-check in GROUP 52 updated off the removed `#btnTheme` (onto the new Settings->Appearance buttons and `#btnPinBookmarks` respectively — see their own updated origin comments in `tests/philogg.regression.test.js`).

- New: configurable log formats + a Settings menu (this session, 2026-08-21, person-requested: *"Ich möchte gerne andere Log Formate parsen können und ein entsprechendes Mapping konfigurieren können. Dazu brauche ich einen Format Manager der auf Basis von Dateinamen patterns ein Logformat zuweisen kann."*, plus two follow-ups: a dummy example file under a renamed `examples/` folder, and `tools/log-simulator.html` gaining the same configurable pattern; and a third round making sure both places share one default pattern and that the builtin default sorts first and stays editable). The single hardcoded log4net-style parser (`HEADER_RE`/`parseHeaderLine`) is now one of possibly many `LogFormat` records (IndexedDB `logFormats`/`formatRules` stores, `CACHE_DB_VERSION` 4→5), each either a log4net/LogViewPlus-style conversion pattern (`%d %p %t %c %M %m %n`, compiled by `compileFormatPattern` — mirrors `compileExtractPattern`'s literal-escaping/named-group approach) or a raw regex with named groups (`validateFormatRegex`), both funneling into one `applyFormatMatch`. Every format still produces the same fixed entry schema (`ts/level/thread/location/method/message`) — no dynamic columns, so sorting/rendering/export/filters are untouched. Filenames map to formats via glob rules (`compileGlob`/`resolveFormatIdForFilename`, first-match-wins by an editable order, reorder via ▲/▼), resolved once at load time (`loadOneFileIntoTree`/`addFile`) and pinned on the file node (`node.formatId`) so a session-cache restore never silently reparses a file under a since-changed rule (explicit `formatIdOverride`, `undefined` = resolve live, a string = pin). `appendTailText` (live tailing) dispatches through the same per-file compiled format. The builtin default (`fmt-default`) is a thin pass-through to the original untouched `HEADER_RE`/`parseHeaderLine` — zero behavior change — until a person actually edits it via the UI (tracked by an `edited` flag), at which point it routes through the generic engine like any other format; it always sorts first in the Format Manager list and is editable (just not deletable). Originally shipped as a new `#btnSettings`/`#settingsMenu` dropdown (copying the `#btnOpen` button+dropdown pattern) holding "Format Manager…" as its one entry, opening a `#formatManagerDialog` that reused the Filter Library's row/CRUD template — **superseded same-session** by the entry directly above (a real Settings page, no dropdown, Add/Edit embedded inline). Also: `Examples/` renamed to `examples/` (only user-visible reference was in `README.md`), and `examples/bracket-format.log` added as a second sample in a different shape (`[%d] %p (%t) %m%n`) to try the feature against. `tools/log-simulator.html` gained matching `#linePattern`/`#tsFormat` fields and a `buildLineFromPattern` generator (the inverse of `compileFormatPattern` — substitutes values into tokens instead of matching them) that reproduces the tool's original hardcoded output byte-for-byte at its defaults; both the builtin `LogFormat` and the simulator's default input share one canonical pattern string (`%d\t%p\t"%t"\t%c\t[%M]\t"%m"%n`), spelled out once in each file with a cross-reference comment since neither file can literally import the other's code. Caught and fixed one real bug while writing/testing this: both compilers' `%n` handling was dropping the literal text (e.g. a closing quote) sitting between the previous token and `%n` — fixed by always flushing the pending literal span before branching on the token type, verified with a direct byte-for-byte round-trip test (generate → parse → compare) before shipping.

- New: File merge follows the same "insert the row first, show progress" pattern as a real file load (this session, 2026-08-20, person-requested immediate follow-up: *"Für ein File Merge folge der gleichen Logik. Lege den Eintrag zuerst an und Zeige den Fortschritt des Merge an diesem Eintrag."*). `mergeFiles` is now `async`: the merged node is inserted into the tree immediately (empty, `loadFraction: 0`, already the active/interactive row via `flushLoadRender`), then filled in over `MERGE_CHUNK_ENTRIES`-sized chunks with a live progress bar (`.tree-load-fill`, same element a real load uses) before a final chronological sort — instead of the old single blocking `flatMap`+`sort` call that could freeze the UI with nothing on screen for a large merge. Both existing call sites (`performBulkAction`'s "Merge N files" bulk action, and the new merge-on-load flow directly above) updated accordingly — the merge-on-load flow now `await`s it so its own promise only resolves once the merge is genuinely done. See "File merge follows the same load-progress pattern" above. Regression-tested: new **Group 69** (row exists with a progress fill immediately, fill gone + correct chronological order once done); the suite's two pre-existing direct `mergeFiles()` calls (Groups 10, 30c) updated to `await` it. 1258 passed, 0 failed total.
- New: Multi-file load shows grayed queued placeholders for the whole batch immediately + asks upfront whether to merge (this session, 2026-08-20, person-requested, German: *"Wenn mehre Log Files geöffnet werden... erscheint das erste, wird geladen und erst danach wird das zweite im File Tree sichtbar... Die Log Files die noch in der Warteschlange fürs Laden hängen sollten schon ausgegraut im File-Tree erscheinen... Werden mehrere Dateien gleichzeitig geladen, frage ob diese direkt gemerged werden sollen... Der Dialog kann direkt mit Enter für Ja und Esc für Nein beendet werden."*). `loadFileDescriptors` now inserts a grayed placeholder row (`createQueuedFileNode`, rendered via `renderQueuedFileRow`/`.tree-row-queued`, styled like folder watch's own grayed unopened-file rows) for every file in the batch up front, in order, before any reading starts — each one flips to a normal, actively-loading row (`activateQueuedFileNode`) exactly when its own turn comes, instead of the whole rest of the batch staying invisible until then. Loading 2+ files at once also opens a small Yes/No dialog (`confirmMergeOnLoad`/`#mergeLoadDialog`) asking whether to merge them into one chronologically-sorted file once loaded — Enter answers Yes (the Merge button is focused on open, so this is the browser's own default button-activation, no custom keydown code), Escape answers No via the existing global `Escape` handler. Along the way, one file's read/parse failure inside a batch no longer aborts every later file in it (each iteration is now individually `try`/`catch`ed) — needed so a later placeholder can't get stuck grayed forever. See "Multi-file load: grayed queued placeholders + merge-on-load prompt" above. Regression-tested: new **Group 68** (all placeholders visible+ordered before any load starts, dialog open with Merge focused, Escape→No leaves files un-merged with placeholders flipping one at a time, click Yes→merges once loaded, a single-file load skips the dialog entirely, one failing file doesn't stop a second valid one in the same batch). 1238 passed, 0 failed total.
- Fix: folder-watch section (not just `#tree`) now scrollable too — same-day follow-up (this session, 2026-08-20, person-reported with a screenshot: an opened folder with many not-yet-loaded log files, showing `#folderWatchList` overflowing well past the sidebar's bottom with no scrollbar, *"Der File-Tree sollte, wenn die Anzeige vollläuft... in der Höhe Scrollbar sein"* having only been fixed for `#tree` itself in the entry directly below). Root cause: `#tree`'s own `overflow-y:auto`/`min-height:0` only scrolls `#tree`'s own content — `#folderWatchList` (the not-yet-opened folder-watch file rows) is a separate `flex:0 0 auto` sibling sitting above it, with no scroll behavior of its own, so a folder with many files still just overflowed the sidebar. Fixed by moving the scroll container up a level: `overflow-y:auto` on `#sidebar` itself, with `#treeActionBar`/`#folderWatchList`/`#tree` all reverted to plain natural-height (`flex:0 0 auto`) children — one continuously-scrolling column instead of `#tree` trying to scroll independently within it. See "Tree interactions" below (updated in place). Pure CSS, no jsdom coverage possible (same as the entry below). 1214 passed, 0 failed (suite unaffected, ran as a sanity check).
- Fix: 3 person-reported bugs from real Windows Electron testing, plus a `philogg.html`-side tree-scrolling bug (this session, 2026-08-20, person-reported: *"Ich habe einen Dateityp mit der Anwendung verknüpft und dann eine Datei geöffnet. Das Log wird korrekt geladen, aber nur mit '1', die nächste mit '2' benannt, statt mit dem eigentlichen Dateinamen."* / *"Wenn ich eine zweite Datei durch Doppelklick auf die Datei öffne, startet eine neue Instanz der Anwendung... Wenn bereits eine Instanz läuft, sollen weitere Dateien ebenfalls darin landen."* / *"Der File-Tree sollte, wenn die Anzeige vollläuft... in der Höhe Scrollbar sein."*). (1) **Wrong file name ("1"/"2")**: `desktop/main.js`'s `philogg://local/<id>` URL carried only the opaque numeric id as its last path segment — exactly what `loadFromUrlParam`'s name-from-URL logic (see "Deep-link loading" below) picks up — so every desktop-opened file showed its registration id instead of its real name. Fixed by embedding the file's real basename as a second path segment, `philogg://local/<id>/<basename>`; the protocol handler's lookup still keys off the id alone. (2) **A second file opened a whole new app window**: `requestSingleInstanceLock()` already stopped a second file-association double-click from becoming a second OS *process*, but its `"second-instance"` handler still unconditionally called `createWindow()`, which just relocated the "new instance" symptom to "new window" instead of fixing it. Fixed with `openFile()`: when a window already exists, it hands the new file to it via `window.philoggLoadUrl(url)` (`webContents.executeJavaScript`) and focuses/restores that window instead of creating another one — `loadFromUrlParam`'s fetch-and-`addFile` body was refactored out into `loadUrlIntoTree(url)` and exposed on `window` specifically to make this callable from the main process. Applied to macOS `"open-file"` too, for the same later-file case. (3) **`#tree` not scrollable when it overflows** (e.g. many files via folder watch): classic flexbox gotcha — a flex item's default `min-height:auto` equals its content's intrinsic height, so `#tree`'s existing `flex:1 1 auto; overflow-y:auto` never actually got to shrink and scroll; fixed with `min-height:0`. See "Desktop wrapper" and "Tree interactions" below for both mechanisms in full. Regression-tested: new **Group 67** (renderer-side half of fixes 1+2 — `window.philoggLoadUrl` exposed, repeated calls accumulate files in the same tree instead of replacing them); `desktop/main.js`'s own Electron-API changes and the pure-CSS tree fix aren't reachable from the jsdom suite (see `tests/README.md` "Known gaps"). 1214 passed, 0 failed total.
- Fix: 4 frameless-window bugs on Windows + Electron builds gain the same version scheme as the html tester build (this session, 2026-08-20, person-reported with 4 annotated screenshots after the first real Windows build of the frameless window feature below, plus one more ask in the same message). (1) *"Diese passen farblich nur zur Dark Version"*: the overlay buttons were fixed to dark-theme colors at window creation with no live tracking — `desktop/main.js` gained `watchTheme()`, polling `document.documentElement`'s `data-theme` attribute from the main process (~1x/second) and calling `win.setTitleBarOverlay()` on change, still without a preload/IPC bridge. (2) *"Im Vollbild Modus verschwinden die Buttons... hinter den Fensterbuttons"* / (3) *"Bei Rückkehr... nicht mehr rechtsbündig"*: both traced to the same root cause — the right-padding reservation was computed once via a `navigator.windowControlsOverlay` `geometrychange` JS listener, which didn't reliably re-fire across maximize/restore/fullscreen, leaving stale padding. Replaced with the `titlebar-area-*` CSS environment variables Chromium exposes for exactly this — a native, continuously-live value with no JS/event timing involved, so it can't go stale the same way. (4) *"die Helle Linie... unterhalb der Buttons nicht zu sehen"*: `#toolbar`'s `border-bottom` was occluded by the overlay's native paint layer at its 50px height; `TITLEBAR_HEIGHT` dropped to 49 so that row falls just outside the reserved strip. (5) *"Die Electron Builds sollten... dem gleichen Versions Schema folgen"*: `desktop-release.yml`'s `build` job gained a `Stamp version` step reusing `release.yml`'s exact `sed` idiom to rewrite the checked-out `philogg.html`'s `PHILOGG_VERSION` from `"dev"` to the commit short-SHA before packaging (never committed back), and passes the same SHA to `electron-builder` as `PHILOGG_SHA`; `electron-builder.yml` gained `artifactName: "${productName}-${env.PHILOGG_SHA}.${ext}"` so installer filenames read `PhiLogg-<sha>.exe`/`.dmg`/`.AppImage` instead of the placeholder `1.0.0` from `package.json` (left untouched — electron-builder wants real semver there, a git SHA isn't one). None of this re-verified live (no display server, no trigger access from this session) — reasoning checked against Electron/Chromium's Window Controls Overlay and `env()` docs, not guessed. Doesn't touch `philogg.html`'s own code or the regression suite — full suite re-run clean, 1208 passed, 0 failed (sanity check only).
- Fix: `desktop-release.yml` rejected outright by GitHub, 0 jobs (this session, 2026-08-20, person-reported: *"Die Ausführung hat nicht funktioniert. Analysiere das Problem auf Github"*, after the checkbox feature below). Checked the actual runs (`gh`/GitHub Actions API): both the push that introduced the checkboxes and the following manual dispatch ended `startup_failure`/`failure` with **zero jobs and zero check-runs created** — GitHub rejecting the workflow file at parse time, before anything runs, not a build/runtime failure. Cause: the `build` job's `if: (matrix.os == 'windows-latest' && inputs.build_windows) || ...` referenced the `matrix` context from a job-level `if:` — per GitHub's own context-availability table, `matrix` is available in `jobs.<job_id>.runs-on`/`env` and inside steps, but **not** in `jobs.<job_id>.if`, so the whole file failed to parse. Fixed by computing the OS list instead of filtering it: `prepare` gained a `Compute selected OS matrix` step (plain bash + `jq`, reading the three `inputs.build_*` booleans) that outputs a JSON array, and `build`'s `strategy.matrix.os` is now `fromJSON(needs.prepare.outputs.os_list)` — no job-level `if:` needed, an all-unchecked dispatch just yields an empty matrix (0 `build` jobs) rather than needing an extra guard. Not re-verified with a fresh dispatch from this session (no trigger access) — the `matrix`-in-`if:` diagnosis is corroborated by the 0-jobs/0-check-runs evidence, not directly quoted from GitHub's own error text (couldn't reach the raw failure annotation from this session either). Doesn't touch `philogg.html` or the regression suite — full suite re-run clean, 1208 passed, 0 failed (sanity check only).

- Fix: `desktop-release.yml` CI failing on Windows/macOS at the upload step, round 2 (this session, 2026-08-20, person-reported follow-up after the icon fix below actually ran in CI: *"der letzte Windows und Mac OS build sind fehlgeschlagen... ubuntu-latest war erfolgreich"*). Checked the real run (`gh`/GitHub Actions API, not guesswork): the icon fix worked — `npm run build` succeeded on all three platforms this time (`default Electron icon is used` on win/mac, exact fallback the previous fix intended). Both failures are in the later "Upload installer(s)" step: `gh release upload` exits 1 with **zero stdout/stderr output** on both platforms (confirmed via two independent log fetches, including a `failed_only` run-level fetch, so it's not a log-fetching artifact) — 39s elapsed on macOS and 8s on Windows before the bare exit, and the same run's Windows build step separately shows an unrelated ~48s stall downloading an NSIS binary, i.e. general network flakiness on that run's hosted win/mac runners. ubuntu's own upload took 4s and succeeded. Couldn't pull the raw log archive to get the actual `gh` error text — `results-receiver.actions.githubusercontent.com` and the signed `blob.core.windows.net` log URL are both outside this session's egress allowlist. Given a clean build, a silent failure only in the final network call, and independent evidence of transient network trouble in the same run, this reads as a transient upload hiccup rather than a workflow logic bug — so `Upload installer(s)` now retries each `gh release upload` up to 5x with linear backoff (5/10/15/20/25s) via a small `upload_with_retry()` shell function, logging an `::warning::` per retry and an `::error::` if all 5 fail, instead of failing the job on the first blip. `-e` still applies to a real, final failure (the retry function's own `return 1` isn't inside an `if`/`&&`, so exhausting retries still fails the step) — only the intermediate attempts are shielded, via the `if gh ...; then` inside the loop. Not re-verified with a fresh CI run from this session (no `workflow_dispatch` trigger available here) — next manual dispatch is the real test; if it fails again with output this time, the retry loop's own echo lines will at least show *something* happened, unlike before. Doesn't touch `philogg.html` or the regression suite — full suite re-run clean, 1208 passed, 0 failed (sanity check only).
- Fix: `desktop-release.yml` CI aborting on Windows (this session, 2026-08-20, person-reported: *"es gibt jetzt ein Desktop build Script, das ist aber wie folgt abgebrochen"*, pasting a log ending in `Terminate batch job (Y/N)?` / `^C` / `The operation was canceled` right after `npm install`). Root cause traced via the actual run's job logs, not the pasted snippet alone: the `ubuntu-latest` matrix job failed first, at `npm run build` — electron-builder's Linux target hard-errors `icon directory ... doesn't contain icons` / `ERR_ELECTRON_BUILDER_CANNOT_EXECUTE` because the placeholder `desktop/build/icons/` directory (added when the wrapper was scaffolded, containing only a `README.md`) exists but has no real icon files in it; unlike Windows/macOS, electron-builder's Linux packaging treats an existing-but-empty `icons/` dir as "icons were meant to go here" and refuses to fall back to its default icon. That failure tripped the matrix's default `fail-fast: true`, which cancelled the still-running `windows-latest` job mid-`npm install` — the `Terminate batch job (Y/N)?`/`^C` the person saw is `cmd.exe`'s normal reaction to Actions sending a cancel signal into `npm.cmd`, not an `npm install` problem itself. Two changes: (1) deleted `desktop/build/icons/README.md` (and the now-empty directory) so electron-builder falls through to its bundled default icon on all three platforms, matching what `desktop/README.md` already claimed; guidance for adding a *real* icon later moved into a new "Adding an app icon" section there, with an explicit warning against re-creating an empty `build/icons/` as a placeholder. (2) `strategy.fail-fast: false` added to the `build` matrix in `desktop-release.yml` so one platform's failure no longer cancels the others — each of the three now runs to its own real conclusion. Not re-run in CI from this session (no `workflow_dispatch` trigger available here); reasoning verified against the actual failed run's per-job logs (`gh`/GitHub API), not guessed from the pasted excerpt. Doesn't touch `philogg.html` or the regression suite — full suite re-run clean, 1208 passed, 0 failed (sanity check only).

The published GitHub Release asset is now named `philogg-<commit-date>-<short-sha>.html` (e.g. `philogg-2026-08-20-a1b2c3d.html`) instead of the generic `philogg.html`, so the version and its age are visible from the filename alone without opening the file or checking the release page. `<commit-date>` is the checked-out commit's own commit date (`git show -s --format=%cd --date=format:%Y-%m-%d HEAD`), not the build/run date — keeps the filename stable and re-runnable for the same commit and ties "how old" to the version's actual origin rather than whenever someone happened to trigger the workflow. Implementation: the stamped `philogg.html` is copied to that computed name (`cp philogg.html "${ASSET_NAME}"`) before `gh release create` uploads it; the tracked `philogg.html` and the version-stamping logic are unchanged. Also anstatt eines Zeitfensters, eine feste Anzahl an Logeinträgen davor oder danach."*). A new `filterType: "countContext"` sibling of the existing `context` (time) filter — windows by a fixed number of entries before/after each reference entry (in the root file's own chronological order) instead of a millisecond duration. Reachable via the tree's right-click menu, "Count context…", right next to "Time context…"; shares the exact same bracket/anchor-dot rendering, `_contextRanges`/`_anchorIds` shape, and NOT-exclusion reasoning as the time-based filter (see "Count context filter" above for the full mechanism, including the index-based merge and its one accepted tie-related rendering edge case). Threaded through every persistence carrier (`cloneSubtree`, `snapshotSubtree`/`restoreSubtree`, `serializeFilterBranch`/`importFilterJson`, `serializeFilterTreeForCache`/`materializeCachedFilters`, `FILTER_TYPES`). Regression-tested: new **Group 64** (window construction by entry count, overlapping/adjacent-range merge, start/end-of-file boundary clamping, child-filter chaining, bracket/anchor-dot rendering, dialog round-trip, clone/snapshot/restore field carrying); Group 8 extended with the 4th NOT-exclusion check; Group 60b (tree context menu ordering) updated in place for the new "Count context…" item after "Time context…". 1185 passed, 0 failed total.
- Bugfix: `tools/log-simulator.html` produced badly corrupted `.log` files — lines mashed together with no newline between them, and runs of stray leading whitespace before some lines (this session, 2026-08-19, person-reported, three attached files: `app-1.log` looked fine at first glance but was corrupted from partway through; `app-2.log`/`app-3.log`, created via "Start next file" rotation, were corrupted almost throughout). Root cause: a genuine race condition, not a formatting bug. `writeText()` reads the module-level `fileHandle`/`bytesInFile`, awaits the actual (slow, multi-step) disk write, and only afterward does `bytesInFile += <byte length>` — and nothing serialized that against a second write starting in the meantime. Two paths trigger it: (1) `btnBurst`("Write burst") was gated only on `!fileHandle`, not on `running`, so a burst click while the periodic ticker was also active put two independent `writeOneLine()`/`writeText()` chains genuinely in flight at once, both reading the same stale `bytesInFile` and writing to the same/overlapping byte position — verified live in a race-forcing jsdom harness (fake writable with real async latency + an overlap counter): the pre-fix code showed 27 concurrent-writable-span events and 21 corrupted lines under the exact scenario, output bytes matching the reported symptom exactly (a write landing short of a stale `bytesInFile` leaves the file's real end/EOF *beyond* the new write, which the File System Access spec zero-pads — rendered as literal NUL-byte runs (visually a run of blank characters), i.e. the "stray leading whitespace"). (2) `nextFile()` ("Start next file ▸") never waited for an in-flight write either — if its `openNewFile()` reset `bytesInFile` to 0 for the freshly-truncated new file while an old write's tail was still pending, that old write's late increment landed on the NEW file's counter instead, drifting it away from 0 before the file had a single real byte — corrupting every write to it from the very first one, permanently, since the offset never resynced with reality (matches app-2.log/app-3.log being broken almost from their start). Fixed with a single global async queue (`enqueue()`) that every disk-touching operation — `openNewFile()`, `writeText()`'s write, `truncate()`'s clear — now funnels through, so each one only ever reads `fileHandle`/`bytesInFile` after every earlier queued operation (including its own trailing counter update) has fully finished; a rotation's reset is always the last word, and two writes can never target the same offset. Re-ran the same race-forcing harness against the fix: 0 overlap events, 0 corrupted lines. Not part of `philogg.html`'s own regression suite (see the entry below), so this was verified with a throwaway jsdom harness (not committed) rather than a new suite group.
- Bugfix: the live tail dot (`.tree-live`) never went dark once shown, even long after a file genuinely stopped growing — and a rotated-out Folder-watch file kept showing it right alongside the new file actually receiving lines (this session, 2026-08-19, person-reported (German): *"Die Anwendung erkennt aktuell zuverlässig, dass eine Datei noch beschrieben wird und markiert sie visuell als 'Trailing'. Dann verliert die Datei den Zustand aber nie, obwohl längst keine Einträge mehr geschrieben werden. Auch wenn dann eine neue Datei im Folder Watch erscheint und ich diese traile, bleibt die alte (nicht mehr weiter beschriebene) im Trailing Zustand."*). Root cause: the dot's condition was `node.tail && !node.tail.failed` — "a live handle still exists and hasn't errored", which stays true indefinitely once a writer just goes idle or moves on to a different file, since the old handle stays perfectly readable, just idle; `t.failed` only ever flips on genuine read errors, never on inactivity. Fixed with a staleness window: `node.tail` now tracks `lastGrowth` (stamped on every real byte-level change — growth or rotation-reset) and new `isTailLive(t)` additionally requires a growth within `TAIL_LIVE_MS` (10s), not just an unfailed handle. Since a poll tick with nothing new otherwise produces no re-render at all, `tailTick()` also does a lightweight second pass every tick comparing each tail's live status against its own cached `wasLive` and fires a cheap `renderTree()` (not a full `render()`, nothing else changed) when it flips purely from time passing — otherwise the dot could only clear itself whenever some unrelated tail change happened to trigger the next render. See "Tailing (live file updates)" above (updated in place). Regression-tested: new **Group 63a/63b** — the dot staying lit through an inert-but-not-yet-stale tick, going dark once backdated past the staleness window with no byte change in that same tick, relighting on a genuine new write, and the Folder-watch rotation scenario itself (two tailed files, only one still growing — exactly one dot shown, on the right file). 1161 passed, 0 failed total.
- `homepage/index.html` follow-up (this session, 2026-08-19, person-requested): two changes to the marketing page from the entry directly below. (1) Screenshots were hard to read at the ~528px-wide two-column card size (downscaled from the underlying 1440×900 captures) — each feature card now shows a **cropped, zoomed-in region** of its screenshot instead of the whole thing (e.g. just the filter tree, just the plot area), click/Enter opens the untouched full screenshot in a lightbox overlay. Implemented with a `.zoom-frame` div per screenshot carrying `data-cx/cy/cw/ch` (crop rect in the original 1440×900 pixel space) plus `data-iw/ih`; a small inline script computes `background-size`/`background-position` percentages from those (`background-size = imageSize/cropSize * 100%`, `background-position = cropOffset/(imageSize-cropSize) * 100%` — the standard CSS-background-crop formula) and sets `aspect-ratio` on the container, so the crop stays pixel-accurate and responsive at any container width without JS recalculating on resize. No image processing/duplicate files — same 7 PNGs, just windowed differently per placement. Verified via Playwright screenshots of each individual `.zoom-frame` (crisp, legible) and the lightbox open/close, at both desktop and a 390px mobile width. (2) Copy pass: removed marketing-style phrasing (rhetorical "statt X" contrasts, benefit-selling headlines like "gibt sie dir zurück"/"ohne die Zahlen erst nach Excel zu schaufeln") in favor of plain, factual feature descriptions — headings now name the feature ("Verschachtelter Filterbaum", "Extraktionstabelle") rather than pitching it. Also removed the two references to this project's specific log format (the `%d %p "%t" %l [%method] %m%n` pattern string, and "log4net-artiges Pipe/Tab-getrenntes Format... genau das Format, das in diesem Projekt anfällt") — the page now describes the parser generically ("toleranter Zeilenparser, erkennt Mehrzeilen-Stacktraces als Fortsetzung"), since the format detail is internal implementation, not something to sell.

Ich möchte diese Homepage nutzen um bei meinen Kollegen für das Tool zu werben."*). `homepage/index.html` (self-contained, inline CSS, no build step or dependencies — same single-file philosophy as `philogg.html` itself, reuses its teal accent) plus `homepage/screenshots/*.png` (7 real Chromium/Playwright screenshots of the actual app, not mockups). Not part of `philogg.html`, doesn't touch its code or the regression suite — a separate deliverable one level up in `homepage/`, linking back to `../philogg.html`. Screenshots were captured against a synthetic demo log (generated for this purpose only, not committed) covering worker threads, retried job failures, and position/offset telemetry, driving the real app through its own exposed top-level functions (`createFilterNode`, `createLinkNode`, `render`, `applyFhView`, `toggleBookmark`, `applyTheme`, …) via Playwright rather than simulating every click — the log view with a nested filter tree, the extraction table + its Plot tab, the Link (nearest-neighbor pairing) view, the Full/Filtered Stacked split with highlight-colored rows, the bookmarks panel + detail panel, and the dark theme. - Bugfix: `tools/log-simulator.html` was writing everything into an invisible `.crswap` file, never the real `.log` (person-reported via screenshot: a 0 KB `app-1.log` next to a growing `app-1.log.crswap`). Root cause: `FileSystemWritableFileStream` writes into a hidden swap file and only renames it into the real, visible file on `close()` — the original implementation opened one writable in `openNewFile()` and kept it open across every tick until "next file"/"truncate"/tab-close, so the on-disk file stayed empty for the entire run. Fixed by switching from "hold one writable open" to "open, write at an explicit byte position, close" **per write** (`writeText()`) — `fileHandle` (a `FileSystemFileHandle`, safe to hold indefinitely) replaces the old persistent `writable` var; the byte offset is tracked in `bytesInFile` (already existed for the stats panel) and passed explicitly as `{type:"write", position, data}`, since `keepExistingData:true` resets the stream's own write cursor to 0, not the file's end — a plain positional write with that option would silently overwrite the start of the file instead of appending. Verified with a jsdom smoke test using a fake writable that models the real close-renames-swap semantics: the file is provably closed (visible on disk) after every single tick now, not just at rotation/truncation boundaries.
- New dev tool: `tools/log-simulator.html` — a log-writer for testing tailing/folder watch (this session, 2026-08-19, person-requested: *"Ich möchte gerne das Trailing testen können. Erstelle mir ein kleines Tool, mit dem ich das testen kann."*). A second, separate self-contained HTML file (not part of `philogg.html`, no shared code) that uses the File System Access API (`showDirectoryPicker({mode:"readwrite"})`, `FileSystemWritableFileStream`) to actually write real `.log` files to a chosen folder, so PhiLogg's own tailing/folder-watch code can be exercised against a genuinely growing file instead of manually editing one by hand. UI: pick a target folder, Start/Stop writing, and a **"Start next file ▸"** button that closes the currently-open writable and opens a new one (`<prefix>-<n>.log`) without touching the old file again — the exact rotation behavior requested, and what exercises `folderScanTick`'s new-file detection. Also added, judged useful for the same testing purpose: a line-interval slider with jitter (simulates a real writer's irregular timing), a "write burst" button (dumps 50 lines at once, for testing a tail poll catching up on a big jump), and a "truncate current file" button (rewrites the same filename from empty, exercising PhiLogg's `size < offset` truncation-reset path in "Tailing (live file updates)" — the one rotation style *not* covered by "start next file"). Lines are generated in PhiLogg's exact expected format (`HEADER_RE`/`parseHeaderLine`: `%d\t%p\t"%t"\t%l\t[%method]\t%m%n`, verified against the real parser via a jsdom smoke test with a mocked directory handle) with randomized level/thread/source, occasional multi-line ERROR stack traces (to test continuation-line handling), and — per the request for "eine wiederkehrende Nachricht, die X,YZ Werte enthält" — a recurring `"Position update x=<float> y=<float>"` message driven by a random walk, matching the exact `x=[value:float] y=[value:float]` extraction case "Plotting" above already documents as the motivating scenario for axis-equal scaling. Not part of `philogg.html`'s own regression suite (`tests/philogg.regression.test.js` covers PhiLogg's parsing/tailing logic already, and doesn't need PhiLogg's HTML tool doubled here) — no `showDirectoryPicker` fallback exists since there is no meaningful one (same reasoning as PhiLogg's own folder-watch feature), so a plain popup message replaces the whole UI in a browser without File System Access API support.
- Bugfix: tailing was closing an open Plot axis dropdown (this session, 2026-08-19, person-reported — see "Plotting" above for the full mechanism). `renderPlotControls()` did an unconditional `plotControls.innerHTML = html` on every call, including the ones a tail poll tick's plain `render()` triggers via `renderExtractTable()` while the Plot tab is open — tearing down and recreating `#plotXSelect`/`#plotYSelectSingle` even though tailing never touches the columns/`plotConfig` those controls are built from, closing whichever dropdown happened to be open. Fixed with a content-diff guard (`lastPlotControlsHtml`, cleared by `resetPlotConfig()` on a genuine node switch): skip the rebuild when the freshly generated HTML is byte-identical to what's already there. Regression-tested: new **Group 62** — DOM node identity for both axis dropdowns survives a real `tailTick()` that adds a row (chart mark count still grows to reflect it), a second empty tick is equally inert, and an actual control change (chart type) still forces a real rebuild. 1150 passed, 0 failed total.
- Level quick-filter no longer narrows the Full view; changing it now also auto-reveals Filtered (this session, 2026-08-19, same-day follow-up to the entry directly below, person-requested: *"die Log Level Filter sollen sich nicht mehr auf das full Log auswirken. stattdessen soll ein andern der Log Level Filter auch zu einem automatischen Sprung von Full nach Filtered führen"*). `renderHighlightView()` stopped calling `applyLevelFilter()` — the Full view's own entry list is always the root file's complete, unfiltered entries now, a stable "whole file" reference the level quick-filter can no longer thin out. `renderLevelBar()`'s click handler gained a `revealFilteredView()` call (the same auto-reveal a filter/node switch already triggers, see the entry below), so a level-filter change made while on the Full tab is never invisible — it jumps to the view it actually affects. No-op in Stacked layout or when already on Filtered. See "Highlight view" above ("Level quick-filter applies to the Filtered view ONLY", updated in place) and "General 'don't jump' scroll anchoring + auto-reveal Filtered" above (new "3." paragraph). Ctrl+click toggles a row into/out of `state.logMultiSelect`; Shift+click selects the contiguous range from the last-clicked anchor (standard file-explorer semantics, replace not extend). Ctrl+C copies the raw text of the multi-selected rows (or just the single selected entry), sorted chronologically, taking priority over the tree's own filter-node clipboard only when `focusRegion` is "entries" not "tree". Found and fixed a real CSS bug while building this (caught via a real-browser Playwright screenshot check, not the jsdom suite): the multi-select highlight was initially a plain `background`, which silently lost to the existing per-level row-tinting rules (`.log-row.lvl-error` etc. — same specificity, later in the cascade) and was completely invisible; switched to `box-shadow`, the same technique `.pinned-row` already uses for exactly this reason (doesn't touch `background-color`, so it composes over any level tint), with an explicit combined-shadow rule for a row that's simultaneously the multi-select's focused row and a set member. The initial cut just called `render()` after flipping `state.multilineMessages`, which reset the Filtered view's scroll to the top (`renderTable()`'s normal node/filter-switch behavior) and left the Highlight view's raw `scrollTop` pixel value untouched even though rows above it may have grown/shrunk — both wrong. Fixed with a one-shot re-anchor: `#btnMultilineMsg`'s click handler now calls new `topVisibleEntryId()` for both viewports *before* flipping the flag (capturing which entry is currently at the top, in the OLD mode), storing the result in new `pendingTableTopAlignId`/`pendingHighlightTopAlignId` — consumed once by `renderTable()`/`renderHighlightView()` on the very next render, which scroll to that SAME entry's *new* pixel offset instead of resetting to 0 or leaving `scrollTop` stale. Guarded against a Link-view edge case: `renderTable()` (and the pending-id consumption inside it) doesn't run at all while a `link` node is active, so capturing a table-view align target there would leave it sitting unconsumed until some later, unrelated render — the click handler skips that capture (mirroring the existing `isLinkActive` check `minimapMarkedEntries()` already uses) when the Link view is what's actually showing. See "Multiline message display toggle" above (updated in place, new "Toggling re-anchors..." paragraph) for the full mechanism. - Multiline message display toggle (this session, 2026-08-18, person-requested: *"Ergänze einen toggle, mit dem man die Log Views zwischen der aktuellen Darstellung und Mehrzeiligen Messages umschalten kann. Default bleibt die aktuelle Ansicht, Zustand ist global und wird im Cache gespeichert. Mehrzeilige Darstellung gemäß der Zeilenumbrüche in der Message."*). New `#btnMultilineMsg` in `#viewBar` (same `.toolbar-icon-btn`/float/26px treatment as `#btnPinBookmarks` right beside it) toggles `state.multilineMessages` (default `false`) between the existing single-line/truncated `.col-msg` and rendering an entry's message with its literal `"\n"` line breaks (continuation lines accumulated during parsing, see "The log format it parses") as real line breaks, in **both** "Log views" that share `.col-msg` — the Filtered table and the Highlight/Full view. Deliberately `white-space:pre`, not `pre-wrap`: only the message's own embedded line breaks produce new lines, a long single line is never soft-wrapped — exactly the literal ask ("gemäß der Zeilenumbrüche"), and what makes a row's rendered height **exactly predictable from its message's line count alone** (`rowHeightForEntry`/`messageLineCount`, `MULTILINE_LINE_HEIGHT = 15px` per extra line, matching a `line-height:15px` CSS override), never measured from the DOM. That predictability is what makes variable-height virtualization tractable at all here: `renderVisibleRows`/`renderHighlightVisibleRows`/`scrollToIndex`/`scrollToHighlightIndex`/`updateMinimapRenderedRange` all gained an `if (state.multilineMessages && …RowOffsets)` branch that swaps the previous flat `index * ROW_HEIGHT` arithmetic for a binary search (`findOffsetIndex`) over a prefix-sum array (`tableRowOffsets`/`highlightRowOffsets`, built once per current-view-list change via `buildRowOffsets`, not per scroll frame) — the off-by-default path is byte-for-byte unchanged. Persisted through the same session-cache `settings` carrier `pinBookmarksInFilteredView` already uses (`buildCacheMeta`/`restoreSessionFromCache`) — global across the whole app, additive-only field, no `CACHE_FORMAT_VERSION` bump needed. Deliberately **not** threaded into the separate JSON session-export/import feature (`buildSessionExport`/`runSessionImport`) — the request specifically said "im Cache gespeichert" (the browser's IndexedDB session cache), which is a different, narrower mechanism than that portable-file feature. See "Multiline message display toggle" below for the full mechanism. Regression-tested: new **Group 55** (55a/b/c) — default OFF unchanged; per-row DOM height growing only for multi-line entries in both Log views; `tableSpacer` summing real per-row heights; exact reversion on toggle-off; the offset-based virtualization landing a correct scroll position and DOM row when a tall row sits before the jump target (`scrollToIndex` via `jumpToEntry`), not just the flat math the default path still uses; and the toggle surviving a simulated reload via the session cache. 987 passed, 0 failed total.

Aktuell liegen die Start und Endpunkte genau am Rand des Plot und werden dadurch immer nur halb angezeigt... man sollte aber ein kleines Stück Panning und Zoom erlauben, damit man diese Punkte auch zentriert anzoomen kann... Außerdem ist der Tooltipp für Punkte weit rechts im Plot teilweise nicht mehr im Bild."*). Two independent fixes. (1) `clampZoomAxis`'s bounds widened by a small `PLOT_ZOOM_OUT_BUFFER` (8% of the home span, each side) — a mark sitting exactly on the home view's edge used to render only half-visible with no way to bring it fully into view, since the old clamp pinned both zoom-out and pan exactly to the home domain. The **default (unzoomed) view itself is completely unchanged** — the buffer only ever matters once zoom/pan is actually used, which as a side effect also fixes panning being a hard no-op at 100% zoom (there's now `±buffer` of room to slide even there). (2) `positionPlotTooltip` replaces the old fixed `cursorX+14`/`cursorY-28` tooltip offset, which never accounted for the tooltip's own rendered width — a mark far right in the chart pushed it partly outside `#plotChartArea`. Now measures the tooltip's real `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight` and flips to the opposite side on whichever axis lacks room (independently for X/Y), clamped to the chart area's bounds as a backstop; placed above-right of the cursor by default with a fixed gap, so it also never overlaps the pointer itself. See "Plot zoom / pan / hover tooltip" above (updated in place) for both.
